<p> The Vancouver Sun - RSS Feedhttps : **27;5785;TOOLONG , 21 Mar 2020 01:25:32 +0000COVID-19 : Officials say health workers will be protected , equippedhttp : **32;5814;TOOLONG ... &lt;img src= " https : **34;5848;TOOLONG ... title= " Nurse in full protective gear screens people at the COVID-19 drive-through testing clinic recently launched by Island Health in Victoria . " alt= " Nurse in full protective gear screens people at the COVID-19 drive-through testing clinic recently launched by Island Health in Victoria . " data-portal-copyright= " CHAD HIPOLITO " **28;5884;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1246173 " /&gt; &lt;iframe width= " 100% " height= " 100% " frameborder= " 0 " allowfullscreen= " true " src= " https : **34;5914;TOOLONG ... &gt; &lt;/iframe&gt; &lt;p&gt; Provincial health officials offered words of support to their front-line colleagues Friday while boosting efforts to contain the COVID-19 outbreak in B.C. with a new order barring the public from dining inside restaurants . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; During her daily coronavirus update , Dr. Bonnie Henry , the provincial health officer , told her fellow health workers she was looking at how to protect them from being infected and making sure they receive the support they need to do their jobs . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; I know how challenging it is when we are dealing with a new virus , a virus particularly for which we have no cure , &amp;rdquo ; she said . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; We have no vaccine , we have no way of preventing it and we know that it can cause quite severe illness in people that we love , from people that we care for to people that we&amp;rsquo ; re close to . And it&amp;rsquo ; s been a tremendous challenge for our health-care sector to watch what has been happening over the last few weeks and months around the world . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Henry said that about two dozen B.C. health care workers have been infected but all have had only &amp;ldquo ; very mild&amp;rdquo ; illness . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; She said a large number of recently retired nurses , physicians and other health care professionals who have joined the COVID-19 response , she said . The cancellation of elective surgeries has freed up workers for the COVID-19 response , she added . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; On Friday , Henry used her powers under the public health emergency declared on Tuesday to order all restaurants in the province to move to a &amp;ldquo ; takeout and delivery model&amp;rdquo ; only . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Previously , she had ordered all bars , pubs and night clubs to close because they could not ensure patrons could maintain &amp;ldquo ; social distancing&amp;rdquo ; of at least one to two metres away from one another . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; I&amp;nbsp ; also say that for many businesses , they can and should stay open to support us , but they need to have the important social distancing measures in place to ensure there&amp;rsquo ; s not that many people in the office environment , for example , there&amp;rsquo ; s not face-to-face meetings , there&amp;rsquo ; s not congregation in groups , &amp;rdquo ; she said . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Henry and Health Minister Adrian Dix also announced 77 new cases of the disease , bringing B.C.&amp;rsquo ; s total to 348 . That includes 200 cases in the Vancouver Coastal Health region , 95 in Fraser , 30 in Vancouver Island , 19 in Interior and four in Northern . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Twenty-two people have been hospitalized , 10 of them in intensive care . Six people have fully recovered . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Nine have died . That includes an eighth death at the Lynn Valley Care Centre , Henry revealed Friday . Thirty-six residents and 16 health workers have tested positive for the disease at the long-term care facility in North Vancouver , Henry said . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Dix said the province has taken action through its pandemic preparedness plan to prepare for a potential surge in patients at hospitals . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Before the COVID-19 response , the acute care system ran at about 103.5 per cent capacity , but it had dropped to 78.5 per cent by Friday , Dix said . Critical care beds are now running at 61.8 per cent capacity . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Dix said there have been 17,912 COVID-19 tests completed in B.C. as of Friday . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; He echoed Henry&amp;rsquo ; s support for health-care workers . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; We must do everything possible to support our front line workers and hospitals , long-term care , the entire health-care sector to give them confidence that they can do their jobs with the lowest possible risk to their health and well being , &amp;rdquo ; he said . &amp;ldquo ; Frontline staff are critical to beating this virus . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Dix said he wanted doctors , nurses , care aids , hospital cleaners , food-support workers and all others at the front line that government is &amp;ldquo ; enormously&amp;rdquo ; grateful for all of their work . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; We&amp;rsquo ; re making headway together against this threat , but we&amp;rsquo ; re far from prevailing , everybody knows that , &amp;rdquo ; he said . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; We also know , as Dr. Henry has said , that you&amp;rsquo ; re performing miracles every day . I want to make it as clear as I can : We&amp;rsquo ; re going to spare no effort to ensure that you have the equipment you need to get the job done , including the equipment to keep you safe . We do have supplies in place . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " **29;5950;TOOLONG " target= " blank " rel= " noopener " **36;5981;TOOLONG &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " http : **25;6019;TOOLONG " target= " blank " rel= " noopener " **31;6046;TOOLONG ... &lt;/p&gt; Nick EaglandSat , 21 Mar 2020 01:25:32 **45;6079;TOOLONG ... **29;6126;TOOLONG That Matter : The Right to Be Coldhttp : **36;6157;TOOLONG ... &lt;img src= " https : **34;6195;TOOLONG ... title= " Sheila Watt-Cloutier is author of the memoir , The Right to Be Cold , a chronicle of Canada 's North detailing the devastating impact of climate change on Inuit communities . Wosk Centre for Dialogue presented Sheila Watt-Cloutier with the Jack P. Blaney Award for Dialogue in recognition of her outstanding global leadership is using dialogue in her work as an advocate for Indigenous , environmental and cultural rights . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Shauna Sylvester , executive director of the Centre says , &amp;ldquo ; Sheila&amp;rsquo ; s quiet determination has moved thousands , from global leaders to young climate advocates . She is an educator , dialogue innovator and elder who has helped us understand the experiences of the Arctic and learn about what this means for our shared future . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Watt-Cloutier previously held the role of Canadian president of the Inuit Circumpolar Council and later became its international chair where she worked extensively to open space for dialogue that welcomes and invites the voices of Indigenous Peoples . Watt-Cloutier&amp;rsquo ; s dialogue approach brings in Indigenous storytelling as an empathy-driven connector between the minds and hearts of those she works with . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Watt-Cloutier is author of the memoir , &amp;nbsp ; The Right to Be Cold , &amp;nbsp ; a chronicle of Canada&amp;rsquo ; s North detailing the devastating impact of climate change on Inuit communities . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; We invited Sheila Watt- Cloutier to join us for a Conversation That Matters about the changes she has witnessed&amp;nbsp ; in the Arctic . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Conversations That Matter is a partner program for the Morris J. NewsNationalOutdoor LivingWorldDaphne Bramham : Stay home . Keep your distance . It 's our best chance at ending this pandemichttp : **32;6344;TOOLONG ... &lt;img src= " https : **34;6378;TOOLONG ... title= " A customer leaves Tim Hortons with takeout . The company closed all of its eating areas on Tuesday . " alt= " A customer leaves Tim Hortons with takeout . The company closed all of its eating areas on Tuesday . " data-portal-copyright= " Paul Chiasson " **28;6414;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1243735 " /&gt; &lt;iframe width= " 100% " height= " 100% " frameborder= " 0 " allowfullscreen= " true " src= " https : **34;6444;TOOLONG ... &gt; &lt;/iframe&gt; &lt;p&gt; It&amp;rsquo ; s hard to grasp the notion that the difference between life and death on a massive scale in this pandemic depends on something as simple as people staying home and forgoing close contact with others . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Social distancing saving lives ? It seems absurd , which may be why I keep hearing about people blithely carrying on as if there&amp;rsquo ; s no risk to themselves or to others . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; With country after country after country closing borders , schools , restaurants , bars and advising people to work from home if possible , we&amp;rsquo ; re long past any notion that this is a hoax. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; It&amp;rsquo ; s true that there is much that remains unknown . It&amp;rsquo ; s also true that mistakes will be made as experts and politicians rush to keep up . The delay in closing the Canada-U.S. border may well have been one of those . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; For now , we&amp;rsquo ; re still at the stage where suppressing the spread of COVID-19 &amp;mdash ; keeping the number of infections as low as possible for as long as possible by social distancing , along with the closure of schools , bars and restaurants &amp;mdash ; could halve the number of COVID-19 deaths and cut peak demand for critical care by two-thirds . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Even with those sharp reductions , our troubles will still be far from over . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; There is likely to be an eight-fold increase in the demand for critical care beds beyond available capacity , based on modelling done by the Imperial College COVID-19 Response Team using data from China and South Korea . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The &lt;a href= " https : **33;6480;TOOLONG ... target= " blank " rel= " noopener " &gt;study&lt;/a&gt; released earlier this week laid out scenarios for Britain and the United States . No one has yet predicted how many Canadians might die . But the modelling suggests that half of all those needing critical care will . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Using expert clinical opinion , the Imperial College team estimated the infection fatality rate for those over 80 at 9.3 per cent . The rate drops to 5.1 per cent for people aged 70 to 79 , and 2.2 per cent for those 60 to 69 , declining to 0.002 per cent for children under nine . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; As absurdly simple as it seems to suppress a pandemic by staying on the couch , it is still economically devastating . Canadians got a glimpse of its cost on Wednesday . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Phase One of the federal government&amp;rsquo ; s response is $52 billion &amp;mdash ; three per cent of our gross domestic product &amp;mdash ; to mitigate the damage caused by extraordinary job losses and business closures . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; That $52 billion only covers four months . Wrestling this virus to something approaching manageable is going to take much longer and is dependent on finding a vaccine , which could take up to 18 months . It is also yet to be determined whether anyone who has had the infection will have immunity when the next wave of the virus hits . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; For those who resent any restrictions placed on their freedom , these are difficult times . For those who thrive on social contact , it&amp;rsquo ; s hard to accept your friends declining invitations as being in your best interest as well as their own . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; It&amp;rsquo ; s distressing to all but the most introverted of introverts to witness the life being sucked out of our neighbourhoods as restaurants , cafes , gyms , yoga and Pilates studios , hairdressers , theatres , music venues , museums , libraries are shuttered . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; For those of us who live in condos and apartment buildings , it&amp;rsquo ; s hard to know where social distance begins and ends . Do you share the elevator ? Or wait ? &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Those , of course , are problems that pale next to the difficulties faced by women living in abusive situations , the homeless and the poor who rely on public spaces for warmth , bathrooms , and even food . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; We&amp;rsquo ; re learning what this new normal looks like with the hope that we may get a reprieve in the summer if the spread of infection subsides , before it is expected to rage back to life in the fall and we will be asked once again to make a few sacrifices for the greater good . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; But this is marathon , not a sprint . It may test the degree to which we are willing to sacrifice for the greater good . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; On Monday , civil libertarians warned not to overstep Canadians&amp;rsquo ; rights and freedoms in response to the prime minister&amp;rsquo ; s statement that the government would be looking at the Emergencies Act to see whether amendments were needed to &amp;ldquo ; enable us to take the actions needed to protect people . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; On Wednesday , the B.C. government joined other provinces in declaring a state of emergency in addition to the previously declared public health emergency . It now has wide powers to enforce a wide range of orders , including its use of private property , entry without warrant , quarantines and rationing of food , fuel , medical equipment or other essential services . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; So far , we&amp;rsquo ; ve only been asked to be our best selves , good citizens and neighbours . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Most Canadians have responded as expected , with kindness and care , even in long grocery lineups. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; But the opportunity to suppress this pandemic is rapidly closing and we&amp;rsquo ; ve been put on notice that we can do this the easier way . We can wash our hands , keep our distance and stay home . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Or we can do it the harder way , with more sickness , more deaths , and police officers pulled away from more pressing duties to enforce those orders . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " **29;6515;TOOLONG " **36;6546;TOOLONG &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Twitter : @bramhamdaphne &lt;/p&gt; Daphne BramhamFri , 20 Mar 2020 02:09:13 **45;6584;TOOLONG ... &amp;amp ; ChildHealthLifeLocal HealthLocal **45;6631;TOOLONG ... Todd : China 's students complaining about some Canadian private schoolshttp : **33;6678;TOOLONG ... &lt;img src= " https : **34;6713;TOOLONG ... title= " An internal Immigration Department report from the Canadian Embassy in Beijing shows students and parents are complaining about the low quality of education at Canadian private high schools . " alt= " An internal Immigration Department report from the Canadian Embassy in Beijing shows students and parents are complaining about the low quality of education at Canadian private high schools . " data-portal-copyright= " Francis Georgian " **28;6749;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1238257 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; Students from China are complaining that private high schools in Canada take advantage of them by not properly teaching English and then inflating their grades in a dubious attempt to qualify them for university . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Immigration officers in the Canadian embassy in Beijing are worried about increasing reports in China that small private Canadian secondary schools are abusing the country&amp;rsquo ; s fast-growing international-school program and harming Canada&amp;rsquo ; s global reputation . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Many of the private Canadian high schools that target young people from China , which focus on teaching English , are located in shopping malls and have &amp;ldquo ; very few students , &amp;rdquo ; says an internal Immigration Department report obtained under an access to information request . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " http : **34;6779;TOOLONG ... target= " blank " rel= " noopener " &gt;Canada took in a record 642,000 foreign students in 2019&lt;/a&gt; , three times more than in 2009 . The latest figure includes 141,000 students from China , one in three of whom are in B.C. For the first time in decades the volume of students from China in Canada reflects an annual decline , of 0.5 per cent , according to the Canadian Bureau for International Education . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; About one in seven of the international students in Canada , almost 90,000 , enrol in &lt;a href= " https : **33;6815;TOOLONG ... target= " blank " rel= " noopener " &gt;secondary school programs&lt;/a&gt; , some of which are privately run . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; There is concern that the excellent reputation of education in Canada may be decreasing in the China market . &amp;hellip ; ( We are ) concerned that some of our programs are currently being abused , and corrective action should be taken , &amp;rdquo ; said the immigration officials stationed in the capital of China . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The Canadian embassy staff have been increasingly hearing from officials and parents that young people from China are going to Canada&amp;rsquo ; s &amp;ldquo ; lower quality schools , with very few students enrolled , mainly Chinese nationals , one or two classrooms in the school and very often ( the ) institution is located in a shopping mall . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; University administrators and Chinese officials have both told Canadian embassy staff that Chinese students attending the private secondary schools have &amp;ldquo ; very low English abilities after two to three years in such institutions . &amp;hellip ; Some of the schools are inflating the grades of the students to allow them to be admitted to some of the top universities in Canada . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; A Vancouver immigration lawyer , Richard Kurland , obtained the 2017 Immigration Department document two years after filing an access to information request . He said he&amp;rsquo ; s never seen such a direct demand from an embassy for Canada&amp;rsquo ; s provincial politicians , who control education , to solve a problem . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; Rare is an internal report so plain . It&amp;rsquo ; s dramatic . The gloves are off . We should fix this , &amp;rdquo ; said Kurland. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;h2 class= " cat-head short " **40;6850;TOOLONG ... &lt;ul class= " relatedlinks " &gt; &lt;li&gt;&lt;a href= " http : **34;6892;TOOLONG ... Todd : Canadian educators fear river of Chinese students to dry up&lt;/a&gt;&lt;/li&gt; &lt;li&gt;&lt;a href= " http : **34;6928;TOOLONG ... Todd : Toronto and Canada breaking North American records for immigration **27;6964;TOOLONG &lt;li&gt;&lt;a href= " https : **33;6993;TOOLONG ... Todd : Do Canada 's 71,000 minor foreign students have real ' custodians ? ' &lt;/a&gt;&lt;/li&gt; &lt;/ul&gt; &lt;p&gt; A spokesperson for B.C.&amp;rsquo ; s Education Ministry said the province is home to 364 private , or so-called independent , schools , which teach kindergarten-to-Grade 12 students . They include schools that are religious , expensive and those that which specialize in foreign students . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Although B.C.&amp;rsquo ; s ministry doesn&amp;rsquo ; t track the K-12 private schools that cater to international students , the spokesperson said there are almost 5,000 &amp;ldquo ; non-resident&amp;rdquo ; students in private schools in the province . No further breakdown was available . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Asked about the Canadian embassy&amp;rsquo ; s worries that students from China are paying large fees to attend low-quality private schools in Canada , a B.C. Education Ministry official said it had &amp;ldquo ; not been made aware of this concern . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; When Immigration Department officials in Ottawa were asked what they&amp;rsquo ; re doing to fix the problem of poor-calibre private language schools , a spokesperson said , &amp;ldquo ; &lt;span class= " s1 " &gt;As education is the responsibility of provincial/territorial governments ... you may wish to consult provincial/territorial governments on their policies . " &lt;/span&gt; &lt;span class= " s1 " &gt; &lt;/span&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Steve Kaufman , a former Canadian diplomat in Hong Kong and China who also created the LingQ language program , said many English-language schools in Canada are ineffective . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; That&amp;rsquo ; s in part because of antiquated teaching methods , and in part because it is too easy for Chinese students to blend into the Chinese community here . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Kaufman , who speaks 20 languages and routinely does business in Asia , pointed to a Canadian study that found Mandarin-speaking newcomers have a much harder time learning English than people from other cultures , including Eastern European , in part because they tend to isolate among people of their language group . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The Institute for Research on Public Policy discovered the Mandarin-speaking newcomers it tracked had made &amp;ldquo ; no significant progress&amp;rdquo ; in their English fluency seven years after arriving in Canada . A University of Alberta professor , Tracey Derwing , found most avoided conversations with English-speaking Canadians . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The internal report from the Canadian embassy in Beijing said parents in China have an extremely hard time figuring out which language schools in Canada are legitimate . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The parents&amp;rsquo ; search is limited by their own lack of ability in English and by &amp;ldquo ; the Great Firewall of China , &amp;rdquo ; says the immigration report , referring to the way the People&amp;rsquo ; s Republic of China blocks access to many foreign websites. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The embassy staff in Beijing said parents often have to make school choices for their children based on the advice of Chinese-speaking agents who make their pitches at &amp;ldquo ; big education fairs in China every year . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The more than 680,000 students that China sends aboard to study each year frequently make news headlines , particularly when worries arose among educators in the U.S. and Canada that a trade war &lt;a href= " http : **34;7028;TOOLONG ... target= " blank " rel= " noopener " &gt;would cause a dramatic drop in the number of high-fee-paying students from the populous country&lt;/a&gt; . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; There were more than 145,000 foreign students in B.C. in 2019 , of which more than 43,000 were from China . The B.C. Education Ministry&amp;rsquo ; s website says international education makes up B.C.&amp;rsquo ; s &amp;ldquo ; third largest export . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " **26;7064;TOOLONG " **33;7092;TOOLONG &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " http : **25;7127;TOOLONG " target= " blank " rel= " noopener " **38;7154;TOOLONG &lt;/p&gt; Douglas ToddThu , 19 Mar 2020 22:01:22 **45;7194;TOOLONG ... NewsNationalStaff BlogsWorldDouglas Todd : Keeping calm in an anxious , COVID-19 erahttp : **37;7241;TOOLONG ... &lt;img src= " https : **34;7280;TOOLONG ... title= " An original 1939 British Ministry of Information ' Keep Calm and Carry On ' poster on display at Christie 's auction house in London in 2012 . With extraordinary measures being taken worldwide against the spread of COVID-19 , we must take precautions for ourselves and the common good . But panic is not an option . " alt= " An original 1939 British Ministry of Information ' Keep Calm and Carry On ' poster on display at Christie 's auction house in London in 2012 . With extraordinary measures being taken worldwide against the spread of COVID-19 , we must take precautions for ourselves and the common good . But panic is not an option . " data-portal-copyright= " Sang Tan " **28;7316;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1243119 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; Keep Calm and Carry On . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; That was the message on the 1939 posters aimed at lifting the morale of the British public before the outbreak of the Second World War , which was about to bring mass air attacks . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The world is not now at war , militarily . But an invisible &amp;ldquo ; enemy&amp;rdquo ; has infiltrated in the form of a novel coronavirus . It has infected hundreds of thousands of people and killed several thousand around the world ( mostly in China and Italy ) , with several deaths in Canada ( many of whom were in the same North Vancouver seniors home ) . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The world is on high alert . And it&amp;rsquo ; s an especially nerve-wracking time for the hundreds of millions of people &amp;mdash ; roughly one in eight people in the West &amp;mdash ; who struggle with chronic anxiety , which makes them prone to bodily distress and constant feelings of dread . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; This COVID-19 outbreak is a reminder that , even at the best of times , &lt;a href= " http : **34;7346;TOOLONG ... target= " blank " rel= " noopener " &gt;life is risky . &lt;/a&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The regular seasonal flu normally kills 3,500 a year in Canada , almost all of whom have compromised health systems . Yet that never makes front-page headlines . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Nor do the 92,000 Canadians who are diagnosed each year with heart failure , the almost 2,000 Canadians who die in automobile accidents or the &lt;a href= " http : **34;7382;TOOLONG ... target= " blank " rel= " noopener " &gt;1,000 who die annually on the job . &lt;/a&gt; The list of risks goes on and on . We should never try to convince ourselves that our leaders &amp;mdash ; or anyone &amp;mdash ; can make us absolutely safe . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Yet with governments around the world making extraordinary moves to ramp up preventative measures against COVID-19 , leading to many suffering financially , the public has a right to be concerned . We have a duty to take precautions , for the sake of ourselves and , as in war , the common good . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;7418;TOOLONG ... title= " ' Actual panics are rare , ' says Baruch Fischoff , an expert in risk analysis . ' You 'll see them in movies more than in real life . Under crisis situations , people typically rally around one another , they support one another , they act bravely . ' " alt= " ' Actual panics are rare , ' says Baruch Fischoff , an expert in risk analysis . ' You 'll see them in movies more than in real life . Under crisis situations , people typically rally around one another , they support one another , they act bravely . ' " data-portal-copyright= " cmu.edu " **28;7454;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1243148 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; Panic is not an option , however . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; And , fortunately , few do actually panic in times of pandemic or other widespread crises , says Baruch Fischhoff , an expert in risk analysis whose work is being &lt;a href= " https : **33;7484;TOOLONG ... target= " blank " rel= " noopener " &gt;highlighted by the American Psychological Association&lt;/a&gt; . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; Actual panics are rare . You&amp;rsquo ; ll see them in movies more than in real life . Under crisis situations , people typically rally around one another , they support one another , they act bravely . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; That doesn&amp;rsquo ; t mean a few aren&amp;rsquo ; t behaving badly , though . They include the opportunists from Vancouver , Kelowna and various corners of the planet getting their clutches on stacks of toilet paper , cleansers and meat to sell for an inflated profit . In times of war a small number of carpet-baggers always appear . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; And China&amp;rsquo ; s foreign ministry spokesman &lt;a href= " https : **33;7519;TOOLONG ... target= " blank " rel= " noopener " &gt;Zhao Lijian did not do anyone any favours&lt;/a&gt; last week when he spread an unfounded conspiracy theory that the U.S. military caused the COVID-19 outbreak in the city of Wuhan , where Chinese authorities initially tried to cover it up . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Canadians have had a rough start to 2020. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;h2 class= " cat-head short " **40;7554;TOOLONG ... &lt;ul class= " relatedlinks " &gt; &lt;li&gt;&lt;a href= " http : **34;7596;TOOLONG ... Todd : The pros and cons of **25;7632;TOOLONG &lt;li&gt;&lt;a href= " http : **34;7659;TOOLONG ... Todd : Men 's work often comes at high **25;7695;TOOLONG &lt;li&gt;&lt;a href= " http : **34;7722;TOOLONG ... Todd : Free psychotherapy is just good **30;7758;TOOLONG &lt;/ul&gt; &lt;p&gt; The year began with a handful of Wet&amp;rsquo ; suwet&amp;rsquo ; en hereditary chiefs and various activists joining forces to **25;7790;TOOLONG ; by blockading railways and roads . Then COVID-19 arrived in this cosmopolitan country , with 87 per cent of our cases linked to travel . It&amp;rsquo ; s paradoxical that worried politicians have responded by , in many ways , shutting down Canada . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; This year has not been a lot of laughs for Canadians . Still , in the midst of these larger troubles , old-fashioned stoicism can go a long way . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; There are many things one can do to stop dread and anxiety becoming overwhelming : &lt;/p&gt; &lt;h3&gt;Keep things in perspective&lt;/h3&gt; &lt;p&gt; Don&amp;rsquo ; t be too hard on yourself if you&amp;rsquo ; re experiencing more nervousness than usual . Give yourself credit for tolerating the current uncertainty , says Anxiety Canada . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;h3&gt;Assess the facts&lt;/h3&gt; &lt;p&gt; With a few hundred Canadians having confirmed or presumptive COVID-19 , it means the current odds of being among such known cases in this country are , roughly speaking , only about 70,000 to one . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; And &amp;ldquo ; take a deep breath and remind yourself that most people who contract COVID-19 will only experience mild symptoms , &amp;rdquo ; adds the American Psychological Association . Fewer than one in 100 will die , and they&amp;rsquo ; ll mostly be those who are already ailing . The average age of the Italian fatalities has been 81. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;h3&gt;Monitor your news intake&lt;/h3&gt; &lt;p&gt; Canadian journalists are largely being responsible in their coverage of COVID-19 . But the avalanche of news stories , which the public is devouring , can give the impression things are spinning out of control . They&amp;rsquo ; re not . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;7817;TOOLONG ... title= " Anxiety Canada 's Mindshift app. " alt= " Anxiety Canada 's Mindshift app. " data-portal-copyright= " " **28;7853;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1243174 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; Take an analytical approach to the news . Treat reports from social media and even friends with healthy skepticism . Exaggeration can slip into even the most earnest news stories , put together by journalists who are themselves excitable . Verify where information is coming from , with health agencies being among the most trusted . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; As we think about and focus more on coronavirus , the perception of threat increases , not the actual risk , but our perception of it , &amp;rdquo ; &lt;a href= " https : **33;7883;TOOLONG ... target= " blank " rel= " noopener " &gt;says Anxiety Canada&lt;/a&gt; , which has long produced a helpful mobile app for dealing with anxiety called &lt;a href= " https : **33;7918;TOOLONG ... target= " blank " rel= " noopener " &gt;Mindshift. &lt;/a&gt; It&amp;rsquo ; s based on the proven principles of &lt;a href= " http : **34;7953;TOOLONG ... target= " blank " rel= " noopener " &gt;Cognitive Behavioral Therapy&lt;/a&gt; . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; By limiting or eliminating contact with the media you can help yourself manage your own anxiety &amp;hellip ; Commit to only checking in a couple of times a day . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Anxiety Canada goes further and says &amp;ldquo ; stop talking about coronavirus , &amp;rdquo ; including at the proverbial water cooler . &amp;ldquo ; To keep thinking about it influences our sense of threat/risk . To counteract this , don&amp;rsquo ; t initiate the conversation and change the subject if it does come up . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;h3&gt;Protect yourself&lt;/h3&gt; &lt;p&gt; The most important advice might be the most basic , which we all now know . Wash your hands often . Try to stop touching your face , since viruses enter our bodies through our eyes , nose and mouth . &amp;nbsp ; Avoid handshakes and hugs and keep a distance of one metre from anyone exhibiting symptoms . If you have a cough or fever stay home . Et cetera. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Finally , even though risk-analyst Fischoff says the normal seasonal flu is currently a greater threat to North Americans than he hopes COVID-19 will ever be , he realizes many people are worried about loved ones who might be in tougher situations than themselves , either because they already struggle with anxiety or because they&amp;rsquo ; re being hit financially . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; Be supportive of people who are under greater stress , &amp;rdquo ; Fischoff says . It&amp;rsquo ; s what we always hope we would do anyways. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; But don&amp;rsquo ; t let your concern for others trick you into believing things are worse than they are . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " **26;7989;TOOLONG " target= " blank " rel= " noopener " **33;8017;TOOLONG &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " https : **25;8052;TOOLONG " target= " blank " rel= " noopener " **31;8079;TOOLONG ... &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;em&gt;&lt;a href= " **27;8112;TOOLONG " &gt;CLICK HERE&lt;/a&gt; to report a typo. &lt;/em&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;em&gt;Is there more to this story ? Email &lt;a href= " **28;8141;TOOLONG " **35;8171;TOOLONG &lt;/em&gt; &lt;/p&gt; Douglas ToddThu , 19 Mar 2020 15:00:15 **45;8208;TOOLONG ... **28;8255;TOOLONG BlogsWorldCOVID-19 : Border to close between B.C. , Washington for non-essential travelhttp : **34;8285;TOOLONG ... &lt;img src= " https : **34;8321;TOOLONG ... title= " Very few cars were in evidence at Peace Arch Border Crossing on Wednesday . " alt= " Very few cars were in evidence at Peace Arch Border Crossing on Wednesday . " data-portal-copyright= " NICK PROCAYLO " **28;8357;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1243628 " /&gt; &lt;iframe width= " 100% " height= " 100% " frameborder= " 0 " allowfullscreen= " true " src= " https : **33;8387;TOOLONG ... &gt; &lt;/iframe&gt; &lt;p&gt; If someone had asked Laurie Trautman a month ago whether she thought travel across the Canada-U.S. border would be restricted because of the novel coronavirus outbreak , she wouldn&amp;rsquo ; t have been able to fathom the idea . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; However , when Prime Minister Justin Trudeau and U.S. President Donald Trump announced on Wednesday that all non-essential traffic between the two countries would be cut off , she wasn&amp;rsquo ; t surprised . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; In retrospect , a month ago I would have really never anticipated this happening . But these are just such unprecedented times that almost nothing is surprising , &amp;rdquo ; said Trautman , who is the director of the Border Policy Research Institute at Western Washington University . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Two days after Canada decided to close its borders to non-residents , with some exceptions , and redirect all international air travel to just the airports in Vancouver , Calgary , Toronto and Montreal , Trudeau said travellers will not be permitted to cross the Canada-U.S. border for recreation , shopping , or tourism . He did not say when the directive will take effect . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; There are 19 land border crossings between B.C. and the U.S. &amp;mdash ; five in the Lower Mainland and 11 in the Interior leading to Washington State , and three in northern B.C. along the Alaska border . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; According to Statistics Canada the Lower Mainland ports of entry &amp;mdash ; Boundary Bay , Peace Arch , Pacific Highway , Aldergrove and Huntingdon &amp;mdash ; saw 11 million travellers cross in 2019 . Peace Arch was the busiest , with 4.35 million crossings . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; **25;8422;TOOLONG &lt;img src= " https : **34;8449;TOOLONG ... title= " " alt= " " data-portal-copyright= " " **28;8485;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " " /&gt; &amp;ldquo ; In both our countries , we&amp;rsquo ; re encouraging people to stay home . We&amp;rsquo ; re telling our citizens not to visit their neighbours unless they absolutely have to , &amp;rdquo ; Trudeau said . &amp;ldquo ; This collaborative and reciprocal measure is an extension of that prudent approach . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; A spokesperson for the Canada Border Services Agency was unable to provide a definition of what constitutes essential or non-essential travel . A query to the U.S. Customs and Border Protection agency did not receive a response . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Essential travel will be allowed to continue to preserve critical supply chains , Trudeau said , which means trucks will continue to cross the border to deliver food , fuel and medicine , and essential workers such as health-care professionals who live and work on opposite sides will also be permitted to cross . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Government officials also said exemptions will remain in place to ensure Canadians who are now in the U.S. are able to return home . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; James Brander , an economics professor at the University of B.C.&amp;rsquo ; s Sauder School of Business , said there is tremendous uncertainty surrounding everything that involves the coronavirus , including how the border restrictions will work . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; He said the decision about whether everyday cross-border business activity that is not necessarily related to supply chains will be considered essential will be an important one . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; If all this business service-related activity is shut off , then I think that will be yet another significant or noticeable reduction to economic activity in both places , &amp;rdquo ; Brander said . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Although trade will not be affected , businesses on both sides of the border will feel the pinch of fewer visitors . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; According to licence plate survey data from the Border Policy Research Institute , at specific retail locations in Whatcom County , Canadians constitute an average of 17 per cent of weekend customers . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Trautman said big retail stores like Ross , Trader Joe&amp;rsquo ; s and Costco are popular with Canadian shoppers , but she believes those big corporations will be able to prevail more easily than smaller retailers . For instance , border gas stations and mailbox outlets rely primarily on Canadians who pop over for a quick visit . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; Those are going to struggle , I think , to weather this storm . Depending on how long those restrictions are in place , those places will not bounce back as quickly , if at all . We&amp;rsquo ; ll see , you know , it&amp;rsquo ; s just so unprecedented , &amp;rdquo ; Trautman said . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; She said although it is speculative at this point , if the restrictions go on for a long time , Canadians could alter their consumer behaviour , as they have done when the dollar has dropped significantly , and that could take a long time to bounce back once it&amp;rsquo ; s entrenched . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Walt Judas , CEO of the Tourism Industry Association of B.C. , said the travel restrictions that have been implemented in recent weeks and days have had an immediate impact on tourism across the province . He said some hotels are already at less than five-per-cent occupancy , and others are closed or on the brink of closing because they have no guests . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; Any additional measures are going to add to what&amp;rsquo ; s already happened , but most of the damage has already been done , &amp;rdquo ; Judas said . &amp;nbsp ; &amp;ldquo ; We&amp;rsquo ; re following the lead of health authorities and recognizing , of course , that the industry is reeling as a consequence , but everybody understands and knows it&amp;rsquo ; s the right thing to do . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; B.C. Minister of Public Safety and Solicitor General Mike Farnworth , who declared a provincial state of emergency on Wednesday , welcomed the changes at the border , as did Premier John Horgan. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; We need to limit non-essential travel over the U.S. border , while maintaining our supply chains , so people have access to essentials , &amp;rdquo ; Horgan said in a statement . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The restrictions were also endorsed by at least one local government that borders Washington State , despite the drawbacks . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; This is necessary . There&amp;rsquo ; s going to be pain all around , &amp;rdquo ; said Abbotsford Mayor Henry Braun. &amp;ldquo ; There&amp;rsquo ; s going to be an economic blow here that we haven&amp;rsquo ; t experienced in our lifetime , because we&amp;rsquo ; re dealing with something that we haven&amp;rsquo ; t seen in 100 years . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;em&gt; -- with file from Canadian Press&lt;/em&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " **31;8515;TOOLONG " **38;8548;TOOLONG &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " http : //twitter.com/jensaltman " target= " blank " rel= " noopener " **31;8588;TOOLONG ... &lt;/p&gt; &lt;h2 class= " cat-head short " **40;8621;TOOLONG ... &lt;ul class= " relatedlinks " &gt; &lt;li&gt;&lt;a href= " https : **33;8663;TOOLONG ... Impact of virus response builds into landslide of layoffs and lost **29;8833;TOOLONG &lt;/ul&gt; Jennifer SaltmanThu , 19 Mar 2020 00:55:07 **45;8864;TOOLONG ... **30;8911;TOOLONG : Vancouver International Airport introduces more screening measureshttp : **32;8943;TOOLONG ... &lt;img src= " https : **34;8977;TOOLONG ... title= " Craig Richmond , president of Vancouver Airport Authority , speaks to reporters at YVR Tuesday . " alt= " Craig Richmond , president of Vancouver Airport Authority , speaks to reporters at YVR Tuesday . " data-portal-copyright= " Jason Payne " **28;9013;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1242594 " /&gt; &lt;iframe width= " 100% " height= " 100% " frameborder= " 0 " allowfullscreen= " true " src= " https : **34;9043;TOOLONG ... &gt; &lt;/iframe&gt; &lt;p&gt; Vancouver International Airport , one of just four Canadian airports allowed to accept international flights as of Wednesday , is stepping up its screening , adding more signs and information , and targeting passengers who exhibit signs of illness . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; However , despite criticism from passengers , measures to prevent the spread of COVID-19 that have been standard at airports in other countries hit by the virus , like temperature testing , will not be implemented . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; We have full confidence that the prime minister&amp;rsquo ; s announcement will strengthen measures already underway in Canada to detect and combat the spread of COVID-19 , including effective airport screening , &amp;rdquo ; &lt;span class= " Apple-converted-space " &gt; &lt;/span&gt; YVR&amp;nbsp ; CEO Craig Richmond told reporters on Tuesday morning . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; On Monday , Prime Minister Justin Trudeau said the border would be closed to non-Canadians &amp;mdash ; except permanent residents , immediate family , diplomats , aircrew and U.S. citizens &amp;mdash ; and all flights from Europe , Asia , South America and Africa will be directed to airports in Vancouver , Calgary , Toronto and Montreal . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Domestic flights and those from the U.S. , Mexico , the Caribbean and St. Pierre et Miquelon can land at their usual airports . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Richmond said YVR is taking its direction from the federal government , in particular the Public Health Agency of Canada , when it comes to passenger screening and Canada Border Services Agency officers are on the front lines performing the duties . Airlines are responsible for preventing anyone who is ill from boarding their planes . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Airport screening began in Vancouver in late January with signs and a question to travellers on the customs kiosks about where they had travelled . Almost three weeks later more screening questions were added . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; As of Wednesday , passengers arriving at YVR will be subject to additional , broader questioning about their health and will have to acknowledge that they are being asked to voluntarily self-quarantine for 14 days . They will answer questions at kiosks and more when they meet with border officers . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Those who are observed be sick or admit to having symptoms will be given a mask kit and asked to fill out a form . They will then be referred to a quarantine officer , assessed , told to register with their local health authority within 24 hours and then sent home . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; If a traveller doesn&amp;rsquo ; t have any symptoms , they are released with an information handout . If they have a connecting flight , they will continue their journey . Richmond said some connecting processes will be shut down after this week because there won&amp;rsquo ; t be many people connecting . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Anyone who is ill on a flight will be met by border officers at their gate , given a mask kit and escorted through the airport . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; When asked whether YVR plans to do take any further action beyond screening passengers using questions , Richmond said the airport is ready for all contingencies . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; This continues to be a quickly evolving situation , and we&amp;rsquo ; ll continue to work closely with the government and other public health agencies to support and implement any additional measures under their direction , &amp;rdquo ; he said . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The airport itself has posted more signage for arriving passengers , put hand sanitizer in high-traffic areas , increased cleaning throughout the terminal and suspended some customer-care programs . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; We&amp;rsquo ; ll continue to make adjustments as needed , &amp;rdquo ; Richmond said . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; As for the airport authority&amp;rsquo ; s finances in the face of so many flight cancellations and travel restrictions , Richmond said he has been through 9/11 , SARS , airline bankruptcies and more , and what is happening now is unprecedented . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; However , he is confident the airport will weather the storm , and in the meantime the airport will continue to operate . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; The airport will never close , come hell , high water or COVID-19 . We will stay open and we will be ready to take off again when people start travelling , which we hope is soon as possible , &amp;rdquo ; Richmond said . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; There have been concerns from the public and government officials about allowing U.S. citizens into Canada , because of the growing severity of the outbreak in the U.S. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; B.C. Health Minister Adrian Dix agreed with the prime minister&amp;rsquo ; s new measures but said one more is needed : prohibit all non-essential visitors from the U.S. He said this is particularly important for B.C. , as it is next door to a U.S. COVID-19 hot spot in Washington state . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; It&amp;rsquo ; s our strong view and our strong message that visitors from the United States not come to B.C. , &amp;rdquo ; Dix said on Monday . &amp;ldquo ; Don&amp;rsquo ; t come , because at this moment that is the wrong thing to do . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; On Tuesday , Premier John Horgan said he fully supports Dix&amp;rsquo ; s position that people should not visit from the U.S. , but that the lines of trade should remain open . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;em&gt; -- with files from The Canadian Press&lt;/em&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " **31;9079;TOOLONG " **38;9112;TOOLONG &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " http : //twitter.com/jensaltman " &gt;twitt ... &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; **25;9152;TOOLONG &lt;h2 class= " cat-head short " **40;9179;TOOLONG ... &lt;ul class= " relatedlinks " &gt; &lt;li&gt;&lt;a href= " https : **33;9221;TOOLONG ...
