" data-portal-copyright= " DARRYL DYCK " **28;9528;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1241722 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; My 91-year-old mother is now in isolation as the COVID-19 pandemic rampages around the globe and increasing concern about its spread in Canada . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; More than empty grocery shelves , not being able to visit Mom brings home what pandemic means . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Fortunately , she is not ill . Fortunately , she is fully aware of why this is necessary . Both of us have nothing but gratitude that , out of concern and caution , her senior&amp;rsquo ; s residence is now closed to visitors . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; She is being well looked after . Still it&amp;rsquo ; s disconcerting that I can not see her , hug her and reassure her &amp;mdash ; or more likely have her reassure me &amp;mdash ; that this too shall pass . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; But when this will pass and what the world will be like in its aftermath are the questions driving our fears . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; And , with each passing day , it&amp;rsquo ; s harder not be fearful as Canada&amp;rsquo ; s infection arc is on the steep , epidemic climb . Stricter measures were introduced Monday . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Prime Minister Justin Trudeau told Canadians abroad that it&amp;rsquo ; s time to come home and , literally , stay at home , self-isolating for 14 days and following that with the social distancing measures that the rest of us are supposed to be practising . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; He appealed to Canadians&amp;rsquo ; sense of duty to protect ourselves , our loved ones and our community and to pull together . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;9558;TOOLONG ... title= " Canada 's Prime Minister Justin Trudeau speaks to news media outside his home in Ottawa on Monday . " alt= " Canada 's Prime Minister Justin Trudeau speaks to news media outside his home in Ottawa on Monday . " data-portal-copyright= " PATRICK DOYLE " **28;9594;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1241719 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; Trudeau also told almost everyone else to stay away &amp;mdash ; everyone , that is , except Americans whose infection trajectory has taken off because of its delayed responses , confused messaging and outright lies . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The American exception , the prime minister said , recognizes the two countries&amp;rsquo ; integration . Trudeau didn&amp;rsquo ; t rule out closing the border to all but essential transportation of goods and services . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; But not yet , he said , noting that he&amp;rsquo ; s spoken to U.S. President Donald Trump several times over the past few days and that officials are in close contact . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; It&amp;rsquo ; s not an exaggeration to say that it has left B.C. officials seething . B.C. Premier John Horgan had urged Ottawa to shut down the border to all but essential transportation of goods and services . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; It is our strong view that visitors from the United States should not come to Canada , &amp;rdquo ; B.C. Health Minister Adrian Dix said at the daily coronavirus briefing Monday . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; But if Americans simply can&amp;rsquo ; t live without seeing Butchart Gardens , he said , they&amp;rsquo ; re welcome to come , self-isolate and then see them on Day 15. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Unlike the administrators at Mom&amp;rsquo ; s assisted living residence , Premier John Horgan can&amp;rsquo ; t shut the doors . And so far , he , Dix and provincial health officer Bonnie Henry haven&amp;rsquo ; t been able to convince their federal counterparts to take that drastic step . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; It&amp;rsquo ; s one that the European Union is contemplating &amp;mdash ; a 30-day ban on all non-essential travel &amp;mdash ; after member states including Italy , Spain , Germany and France have placed their own restrictions on travel . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Perhaps the urgency isn&amp;rsquo ; t felt in Ottawa despite the prime minister&amp;rsquo ; s own self-isolation following his wife&amp;rsquo ; s positive test for the coronavirus. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; But here , British Columbia ( pop . 5.1 million ) has already spent nine weeks attempting to limit the damage with testing , social distancing and even daily briefings in the belief that knowledge is the best antidote to fear . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Nine weeks in , there are 103 cases and only four deaths . In stark contrast , the province&amp;rsquo ; s southern neighbour is the epicentre of the American epidemic . Washington ( pop . 7.5 million ) has 904 confirmed cases , 48 deaths and numerous cases of community transmission with 135 new cases reported between Sunday and Monday afternoon . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Separating us from them is Canada&amp;rsquo ; s second busiest border crossing . And uniting us with them is the world&amp;rsquo ; s second largest trade relationship . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Every day , nearly $2-billion worth of goods and services cross the Canada-U.S. border . We sell more to them than we buy and 75 per cent of everything we export goes there . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; But everyone who&amp;rsquo ; s been to a grocery store in the past few days knows exactly how much we depend on them especially for fresh produce at this time of year . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; I&amp;rsquo ; ll leave for another day the issue of how vulnerable we&amp;rsquo ; ve allowed ourselves to become when it comes to food production and processing and how captive we are to just-in-time manufacturing . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Buffeted by fear of the many unknowns , even sneezing , sneezing , sneezing into my elbow as a minor allergy flares causes me to wonder . &amp;hellip ; Could it be ? &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; When the Safeway sign warning that customers are limited to one toilet paper purchase a day made me want to buy some . Just in case . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; There&amp;rsquo ; s only one certainty , the experts tells us each day . If you don&amp;rsquo ; t come into contact with the virus , you won&amp;rsquo ; t get it and you won&amp;rsquo ; t pass it on . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Like most people , I need to work through this pandemic even if it&amp;rsquo ; s at a distance , holed up at home . I need money to keep a roof over my head and enough food and toilet paper in the cupboard to get by . I want some certainty that if I look hard enough , I&amp;rsquo ; ll eventually find what I need . And I&amp;rsquo ; m also doing what I can to keep myself and others safe . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; If it were simple , I&amp;rsquo ; d gladly slam shut the gates and lock us all in until this is over . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; But it were that simple , the drawbridge would already be up . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " dbramham@postmedia.com " target= " blank " rel= " noopener " **36;9624;TOOLONG &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Twitter : @bramhamdaphne &lt;/p&gt; Daphne BramhamTue , 17 Mar 2020 00:37:38 **45;9662;TOOLONG ... &amp;amp ; ChildHealthLifeLocal HealthLocal **45;9709;TOOLONG ... veteran Mountie shares his struggles with PTSD , hoping it will encourage others to seek helphttp : **36;9756;TOOLONG ... &lt;img src= " https : **34;9794;TOOLONG ... title= " Staff Sgt Major John Buis is on medical leave because of PTSD , which he developed over his 43-year policing career . " alt= " Staff Sgt Major John Buis is on medical leave because of PTSD , which he developed over his 43-year policing career . " data-portal-copyright= " Francis Georgian " **28;9830;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1240184 " /&gt; &lt;iframe width= " 100% " height= " 100% " frameborder= " 0 " allowfullscreen= " true " src= " https : **34;9860;TOOLONG ... &gt; &lt;/iframe&gt; &lt;p&gt; It was a warm Sunday evening in April 1979 when John Buis , a 25-year-old Mountie with two years on the job , pulled over a dilapidated Lincoln Continental with Texas plates that had been speeding on Kingsway Avenue in Burnaby. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; He radioed in the licence plate number , but it was 8:30 p.m. on a Sunday night and the computer system was slow , so no information was immediately available . Buis and his partner Jack Robinson called for backup before checking the identification of the seven people who spilled out of the messy , dirty car stopped near Imperial Avenue . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; After Const . Merv Korolek responded to the scene , the three officers searched the car . They made some disturbing finds among the discarded food wrappers and other garbage : ammunition and a rifle scope in the back seat , and a sawed-off rifle in a plastic bag in the trunk . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;9896;TOOLONG ... title= " The suspects ' car . " alt= " The suspects ' car . " data-portal-copyright= " " **28;9932;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1239638 " /&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;9962;TOOLONG ... title= " Some of the weapons found in the car . " alt= " Some of the weapons found in the car . " data-portal-copyright= " " **28;9998;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1239637 " /&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;10028;TOOLONG ... title= " Some of the ammunition found in the car . " alt= " Some of the ammunition found in the car . " data-portal-copyright= " " **28;10064;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1239647 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; Buis returned to his police cruiser to radio for more backup . That&amp;rsquo ; s when the dispatcher told him , 15 minutes after he&amp;rsquo ; d first asked for the plates to be run , that the suspects&amp;rsquo ; vehicle had been stolen in California . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; He alerted the other two cops and then walked over to the group of suspects standing on the sidewalk . Buis , who had not pulled his service revolver out of his holster , explained to one of the passengers , a nervous-looking Gustave Gyulay , that he didn&amp;rsquo ; t want any trouble . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; Just walk over to the car and put your hands on the roof , &amp;rdquo ; Buis said , while placing his hand on the 26-year-old suspect&amp;rsquo ; s shoulder . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Gyulay suddenly stepped backwards , put his hand into the back of his pants , and pulled out a sawed-off shotgun . &amp;ldquo ; Come here , I want you , &amp;rdquo ; he told Buis , according to the police reports . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The young cop backed up quickly but Gyulay ran toward him , firing the shotgun at very close range . The bullet tore into Buis&amp;rsquo ; s thighs . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Buis remembers floating in the air and hitting his head on the ground , his ears ringing from the loud blast . Disoriented and in shock , he lay so still , face-down on the sidewalk , that his co-workers didn&amp;rsquo ; t know if he were dead or alive . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; His partner , Robinson , fired one shot at the suspect , then ran to the cruiser to hit the radio emergency button . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Korolek , who was behind the suspects&amp;rsquo ; car , shouted at Gyulay , &amp;ldquo ; Hold it right there . &amp;rdquo ; The Mountie unleashed five bullets , one that went through Gyulay&amp;rsquo ; s right arm and lodged in his liver , the RCMP report says . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Robinson blasted two more shots at Gyulay , who was struck in the knee and ankle before he reached into Buis&amp;rsquo ; s holster and pulled out the cop&amp;rsquo ; s revolver , using it to fire at the other officers . &amp;ldquo ; The gun was shooting over my left ear as he is using me as a barricade , &amp;rdquo ; Buis recalled . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Gyulay then rammed Buis&amp;rsquo ; s own revolver into the back of his head , and told the other officers to drop their guns or he would kill their colleague . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Buis can still remember the hot muzzle burning his neck and the sound of his revolver being cocked , knowing a bullet was live in the chamber . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The harrowing incident unfolded two years after Buis graduated from the RCMP training depot in April 1977 and began working in Burnaby . As he lay on the ground , he thought of his wife Kellie , a 23-year-old Delta school teacher . He thought of his parents &amp;mdash ; he&amp;rsquo ; d had dinner with them earlier that evening to celebrate his birthday . He thought about the safety of his fellow cops . He thought about the short life he&amp;rsquo ; d had . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Robinson kept his revolver pointed at Gyulay , telling him to drop the gun and not add murder to the mounting number of charges against him . Crouching on the ground , Gyulay tried to get the bullets out of the pouch attached to Buis&amp;rsquo ; s belt , but he dropped the ammunition onto the sidewalk . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; As more officers arrived , Gyulay slowly put the revolver to his own head before throwing it onto the nearby grass . He was arrested for attempted murder , and the following year was sentenced to life in prison . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The shooting was over in a matter of minutes , but for Buis the healing would take a lifetime . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; His right leg was badly damaged &amp;mdash ; his wife said the wound was larger than her two fists held together &amp;mdash ; and his left leg was injured as well . He spent 26 days in the hospital , undergoing two surgeries , including a skin graft , and was off work for 2&amp;frac12 ; months . Since then , he&amp;rsquo ; s had seven more leg surgeries and several knee surgeries to address aftermath injuries , remove dozens of pellets embedded in his skin , reduce his pain and minimize the scarring. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;10094;TOOLONG ... title= " The scars on RCMP officer John Buis 's right leg , two years after the shooting . " alt= " The scars on RCMP officer John Buis 's right leg , two years after the shooting . " data-portal-copyright= " " **28;10130;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1239664 " /&gt; &lt;h3&gt;Injuries far from just physical&lt;/h3&gt; &lt;p&gt; The shooting , though , would not be the last trauma in Buis&amp;rsquo ; s 43-year career . Two of his depot troop mates were killed on the job , his emergency response force boss committed suicide , a young constable on his watch was killed by street racers , and he participated in two peacekeeping tours . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; When people think someone gets shot , they think he recovers and goes back to work . I thought I had dealt with it pretty well , the emotional , psychological stuff . But it is the cumulative things over the years , &amp;rdquo ; said Buis , who is now a staff-sergeant major . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; He thought he had held his life together with tough-guy bravado , but things started to come unglued in 2017 and totally fell apart last year , when psychologists diagnosed him with post-traumatic stress disorder , or PTSD , and a dependency on opioids and alcohol that he had used to try to self-medicate his pain . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; He sought help at Vancouver&amp;rsquo ; s &lt;a href= " http : **34;10160;TOOLONG ... target= " blank " rel= " noopener " &gt;Operational Stress Injury Clinic&lt;/a&gt; , which caters to police and soldiers , and from there attended a nine-week residential treatment program in Saanich. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; He is speaking out today to encourage a larger conversation about mental health among first responders , who are often hesitant to ask for help . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; I think there are a lot of people , particularly members of the RCMP , who if they get into this type of situation don&amp;rsquo ; t really know where to turn . And hopefully we can start talking about the services that are out there for people who may end up in a similar situation to me , &amp;rdquo ; said Buis , who has been on medical leave since October 2018. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; I have a son and his girlfriend in the RCMP and do not want to see either of them go through what I have gone through . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Buis believes he was able to find help because his leg surgeries meant he was already in contact with medical personnel who work with Mounties. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; There has been much debate , though , about whether the RCMP and other first-responder agencies have put enough resources into dealing with PTSD. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;10196;TOOLONG ... title= " IHIT 's Staff Sgt. Jennifer Pound addresses the media at B.C. RCMP headquarters to provide an update on a 2016 Abbotsford school homicide . " alt= " IHIT 's Staff Sgt. Jennifer Pound addresses the media at B.C. RCMP headquarters to provide an update on a 2016 Abbotsford school homicide . " data-portal-copyright= " Gerry Kahrmann " **28;10232;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1239737 " /&gt; &lt;h3&gt;Some officers did n't get help&lt;/h3&gt; &lt;p&gt; Former high-profile RCMP spokeswoman &lt;a href= " https : **29;10262;TOOLONG ? pag ... target= " blank " rel= " noopener " &gt;Staff Sgt. Jennifer Pound maintains a raw blog&lt;/a&gt; that documents her struggle with PTSD , and insists she has not found the help she needs from the Mounties or the health care system . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; One of the most gutting realizations of this journey for me is that once I became injured , the RCMP family I once bought into appeared to no longer value me , &amp;rdquo ; she wrote last September . &amp;ldquo ; If funding is not provided to create more positions for health care employees , then injured first responders will continue to sit on wait-lists with the risk of their injury becoming more difficult to treat . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The assistance offered to former B.C. RCMP spokesman Sgt. Pierre Lemaitre was analyzed at the 2018 &lt;a href= " https : **33;10293;TOOLONG ... target= " blank " rel= " noopener " &gt;inquest&lt;/a&gt; into his 2013 suicide . Lemaitre , as the coroner&amp;rsquo ; s report chronicled , said he had been diagnosed with PTSD &amp;ldquo ; as a result of exposure to traumatic events he experienced while in the line of duty . &amp;hellip ; He was not provided with any critical incident debriefings or meaningful mental health support following these incidents . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The RCMP chief psychologist , Roxane Marois , testified at the inquest that the force had started several mental health programs in the previous decade , including its &lt;a href= " http : **34;10328;TOOLONG ... target= " blank " rel= " noopener " &gt;mental health strategy&lt;/a&gt; , which focuses on awareness , prevention and intervention . &amp;ldquo ; The chief psychologist testified that she was unaware of any tracking mechanism or evidence-based data for the effectiveness of the strategy . She was also unable to provide information on the type and number of officers accessing the support , &amp;rdquo ; the coroner&amp;rsquo ; s report said . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Among the jury&amp;rsquo ; s recommendations was that the RCMP should include mental health assessments as part of mandatory three-year physical assessments for all officers . The RCMP told the coroner that new step would begin this spring . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;10364;TOOLONG ... title= " RCMP Cpl . Pierre Lemaitre answers questions from the press while sitting beside Danya Voth , whose 19-year-old daughter Breann Voth was murdered in 2002 in Coquitlam. " alt= " RCMP Cpl . Pierre Lemaitre answers questions from the press while sitting beside Danya Voth , whose 19-year-old daughter Breann Voth was murdered in 2002 in Coquitlam. " data-portal-copyright= " Colin Price " **28;10400;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1239729 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; Also in response to the coroner&amp;rsquo ; s recommendations , the RCMP said it would hire 14 new staff psychologists . Spokesman Daniel Brien said this week that will bring the number of staff psychologists up to 27. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; He said PTSD is &amp;ldquo ; an increasing concern&amp;rdquo ; within the RCMP , but could not say how many members have that diagnosis because that would require delving into private medical files . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Brien said the RCMP is taking many steps to address work-related stress , including tracking the mental health of cadets after they start working , and an online suicide prevention course . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; There were 25 documented RCMP suicides between January 2014 and December 2019 , involving 15 active members and 10 retirees , Brien said . &lt;a href= " https : **33;10430;TOOLONG ... target= " blank " rel= " noopener " &gt;Postmedia has reported&lt;/a&gt; &amp;nbsp ; that between 2006 and 2014 , there were 31 suicides by serving or retired Mounties. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Certainly , the RCMP is not the only agency grappling with mental health issues . WorkSafeBC has seen a large increase in accepted PTSD-related workplace claims , 641 in 2019 , up from 266 in 2017 . Of the 2019 claims , 21 were for municipal police officers . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; WorkSafeBC created an anti-stigma campaign , &lt;a href= " https : **33;10465;TOOLONG ... target= " blank " rel= " noopener " &gt;Share It , Do n't Wear It&lt;/a&gt; , and held a mental health conference for first responders last year , which was attended by 300 people , 81 of them police officers . A second conference is being planned for January . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Another former Mountie , retired superintendent Russ Grabb , told Postmedia this week that he believes he developed PTSD during his long career , which included an attempt on his life by &amp;mdash ; coincidentally &amp;mdash ; one of the suspects in the Lincoln Continental just days before Buis pulled the same car over . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Grabb is also haunted by the image of the charred bodies of two little girls killed in a Lions Bay house fire in 1977. &amp;ldquo ; The gruesome sights and penetrating smells of that double autopsy haunted me for over 40 years , &amp;rdquo ; Grabb said this week . &amp;ldquo ; ( But ) it was the 1970s , and we were conditioned to just &amp;lsquo ; suck it up&amp;rsquo ; or face eternal derision and shame . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;10500;TOOLONG ... title= " RCMP media relations officer Sgt. Russ Grabb in 1998 pushing RCMP Const . Laurie White , who lost her lower leg after being shot on duty in Kitimat. " alt= " RCMP media relations officer Sgt. Russ Grabb in 1998 pushing RCMP Const . Laurie White , who lost her lower leg after being shot on duty in Kitimat. " data-portal-copyright= " David Clark " **28;10536;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1239722 " /&gt; &lt;h3&gt;Demand for help ' growing quite significantly ' &lt;/h3&gt; &lt;p&gt; The residential program that Buis attended for nine weeks last fall was at Homewood Ravensview in Saanich , which opened in June to address &amp;ldquo ; an unmet need&amp;rdquo ; in Western Canada for in-house treatment of trauma and PTSD among &lt;a href= " https : **33;10566;TOOLONG ... target= " blank " rel= " noopener " &gt;first responders , soldiers and veterans&lt;/a&gt; , said staff psychologist Michael Berry . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; A lot of first responders and military members are coming in with occupational stress injury , which means that they&amp;rsquo ; ve had trauma in the workplace , &amp;rdquo ; Berry said . &amp;ldquo ; The need and the demand for services and treatment is growing quite significantly . I think there is still stigma in those communities . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; He believes the RCMP and other first-responder organizations are moving in the right direction to better address mental health , but there&amp;rsquo ; s still more work to do . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; I think there is still a ways to go yet , &amp;rdquo ; Berry said , but added senior Mounties like Buis speaking publicly will assist others . &amp;ldquo ; To help them feel like it is valid and it&amp;rsquo ; s OK to seek out supportive help . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;10601;TOOLONG ... title= " RCMP Const . Mike Buday with his dog Trooper . Buday was shot dead by trapper Michael Oros in northern B.C. in 1985 . From ' Descent Into Madness : The Diary of a Killer ' , by Vernon Frolick. " alt= " RCMP Const . Mike Buday with his dog Trooper . Buday was shot dead by trapper Michael Oros in northern B.C. in 1985 . From ' Descent Into Madness : The Diary of a Killer ' , by Vernon Frolick. " data-portal-copyright= " " **28;10637;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1239715 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; Through a series of interviews , it was clear that sharing his story has not been easy for Buis , who is approaching retirement . &amp;ldquo ; I&amp;rsquo ; ve had a number of traumatic situations that changed me , &amp;rdquo ; he said . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; In March 1985 , six years after his shooting , Buis heard over the police radio that Mike Buday , his &amp;ldquo ; pit partner&amp;rdquo ; at training depot , had been fatally shot on the job near Teslin Lake in northern B.C. &amp;ldquo ; He was a great young man and when I found out he had been killed , it was very devastating , &amp;rdquo ; he recalled . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Two years later , Buis was a member of the Burnaby RCMP emergency response team when the team leader , Const . Lee Rumball , shot himself in the ERT van parked at the detachment . Earlier in the evening , Rumball&amp;rsquo ; s wife had phoned Buis to ask if he knew where her husband was , and later that night he drove to Abbotsford to deliver the crushing news . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;10667;TOOLONG ... title= " John and Kellie Buis with the RCMP honour guard at their 1977 wedding . Const . Chris Riglar , who was killed on the job , is at the far left . " alt= " John and Kellie Buis with the RCMP honour guard at their 1977 wedding . Const . Chris Riglar , who was killed on the job , is at the far left . " data-portal-copyright= " " **28;10703;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1239703 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; The weeping coming from one of the ( Rumball ) kids , I will never forget , when she said their dad was dead , &amp;rdquo ; Buis recalled . Rumball was the fifth of six suicides with ties to the Burnaby detachment between 1982 and 1988 , he added . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; In September 1991 , Buis was on a ferry returning from Tofino when he read in the newspaper that Const . Chris Riglar &amp;mdash ; another depot friend who had also been in his wedding party &amp;mdash ; had been killed by a drunk driver outside Victoria . &amp;ldquo ; It&amp;rsquo ; s a very awful experience , &amp;rdquo ; said Buis. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; So too was the senseless 2002 death of Richmond Const . Jimmy Ng , killed by two street racers. &amp;ldquo ; It was a very , very tragic incident , &amp;rdquo ; said Buis , who was Ng&amp;rsquo ; s watch commander . &amp;ldquo ; It really hit the people on my watch hard . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;10733;TOOLONG ... title= " Dr. Chris Ng and Therese Ng , the parents of Const . Jimmy Ng , who died in a car crash in 2002. " alt= " Dr. Chris Ng and Therese Ng , the parents of Const . Jimmy Ng , who died in a car crash in 2002. " data-portal-copyright= " Jason Payne " **28;10769;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1239696 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; Among the memories that Buis finds the hardest to shake was an experience during one of his two tours working with peacekeepers in the former Yugoslavia in 1994 and East Timor in 2003-04 . He is haunted by his crew&amp;rsquo ; s sudden evacuation out of Yugoslavia to escape the violence , and his inability to help the citizens he had been protecting . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; We were leaving them behind . And I didn&amp;rsquo ; t realize how much of a dilemma that would be for me , &amp;rdquo ; he said . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;10799;TOOLONG ... title= " Const . John Buis in the Ilok UN Civilian Police Station in the former Yugoslavia in June 1994. " alt= " Const . John Buis in the Ilok UN Civilian Police Station in the former Yugoslavia in June 1994. " data-portal-copyright= " " **28;10835;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1239686 " /&gt; &lt;h3&gt;'I said , " I need help " ' &lt;/h3&gt; &lt;p&gt; Buis said he&amp;rsquo ; s had a remarkable career , with many accomplishments of which he is proud . But in 2017 , he developed a serious infection following yet another surgery on his right leg . He was prescribed the powerful opioid Dilaudid for the pain and spent two weeks in isolation , during which memories of his last long stay in hospital in 1979 brought flashbacks of the shooting . Later , both his knees were replaced as a result of injuries from the shooting , and he also had several unrelated surgeries , which all brought more opioid prescriptions for pain . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; In March 2019 , a surgeon told Buis his daily dosage of Dilaudid was frighteningly high . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; I quit cold turkey . But the pain was still there , so I started drinking . I was still hurting and it was ruining my relationship with my wife . I was just sitting at home feeling sorry for myself , &amp;rdquo ; he said with blunt honesty . &amp;ldquo ; I said , &amp;lsquo ; I need help . &amp;rsquo ; And for me to say that , it was difficult . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; At the end of May 2019 , he stopped drinking and reached out to the &lt;a href= " http : **34;10865;TOOLONG ... target= " blank " rel= " noopener " &gt;Operational Stress Injury Clinic&lt;/a&gt; . The ensuing assessment from the staff psychologist shocked him : He had PTSD , anxiety , depression and alcohol use disorder . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; It was like cold water being thrown in my face , &amp;rdquo ; he said . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; In recommending he attend in-patient therapy , the doctor wrote : &amp;ldquo ; The cumulative effect of his many traumatic experiences have caused symptoms that have lasted decades . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;10901;TOOLONG ... title= " RCMP Staff Sgt. Major John Buis is on medical leave because of PTSD , which he developed over his 43-year policing career . " alt= " RCMP Staff Sgt. Major John Buis is on medical leave because of PTSD , which he developed over his 43-year policing career . " data-portal-copyright= " Francis Georgian " **28;10937;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1239679 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; He agreed to go to Ravensview , but even as Buis drove through the gates last September , he heard a nagging voice asking : &amp;ldquo ; Do I really need this ? &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; It turns out he did . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; He was able to confront the demons from his past and some pent-up disappointments , such as the two officers who saved his life in 1979 never receiving recognition for their actions . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; When he completed the program in December , his discharge papers noted that &amp;ldquo ; during his career with the RCMP , the culture was to not express weakness . He did not consider the need for receiving therapy . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; That has changed , and he hopes that by sharing his personal journey &amp;mdash ; which continues today as he sees a therapist once a week and attends a recovery program &amp;mdash ; he can encourage others in a similar situation to speak up . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; I&amp;rsquo ; m hoping years from now , that the stigma attached to asking for mental health help will be completely erased , &amp;rdquo ; he said , adding that may require better health funding so first responders can access sufficient resources . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; I know that PTSD in our armed forces and first responders is significant . I hope that in the future , or starting now , that more attention is put to it . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " **29;10967;TOOLONG " **36;10998;TOOLONG &lt;/p&gt; &lt;hr&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;em&gt;&lt;a href= " **27;11036;TOOLONG " &gt;CLICK HERE&lt;/a&gt; to report a typo. &lt;/em&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;em&gt;Is there more to this story ? NewsNationalNewsChild killer Allan Schoenborn wins right to community accesshttp : **34;11179;TOOLONG ... &lt;img src= " https : **34;11215;TOOLONG ... title= " Allan Schoenborn. " alt= " Allan Schoenborn. " data-portal-copyright= " TBA " **28;11251;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1238868 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; Notorious B.C. child killer Allan Schoenborn will be detained for another year in the Forensic Psychiatric Hospital in Coquitlam , but the &lt;a href= " http : **27;11281;TOOLONG " target= " blank " rel= " noopener " &gt;B.C . Review Board&lt;/a&gt; said Friday he can have unescorted passes into the community at the director&amp;rsquo ; s discretion . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Less than a day after conducting his annual hearing , a three-person panel issued the order saying its actual decision and reasons will be released later . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; Mr. Schoenborn recognizes he has much work to do moving forward , &amp;rdquo ; his Vancouver lawyer , Rishi Gill , said . &amp;ldquo ; He is grateful to have the ability to proceed with his treatment plan and he appreciates the support of his treatment team . He understands the public interest and potential concerns of those following the case and is working toward making sure the public can be ultimately confident he is not a danger . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; In April 2008 Schoenborn stabbed and smothered his daughter and two sons to death in their Merritt family trailer . He then fled into the bush , eluding a police dragnet for 10 days before emerging dishevelled and incoherent . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The now-52-year-old was found not criminally responsible because of a mental disorder on three counts of first-degree murder on Feb. 22 , 2010 , and confined indefinitely under the care of the board subject to yearly review . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; He claimed he killed his kids &amp;mdash ; Kaitlynne , 10 , Max , 8 , and Corden , 5 , &amp;mdash ; because he believed they were being sexually abused and he wanted to save them from the crushing humiliation . Prosecutors insisted he was out for revenge on his wife , Darcie Clarke , who died of heart issues May 30 last year . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Dave Teixeira , a spokesman for the Clarke family , said they weren&amp;rsquo ; t surprised by the ruling . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; The B.C. Review Board has no regard for public safety and the well-being of victims of crime in the province &amp;mdash ; this highlights that fact , &amp;rdquo ; he said . &amp;ldquo ; This is a man who is more concerned with getting out and getting away than in getting healthy . The review board is complicit in allowing him to do this . It&amp;rsquo ; s very disappointing but not unexpected . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Schoenborn has been incarcerated since his arrest , but at his hearing last year the board agreed he should be permitted escorted community access at the director&amp;rsquo ; s discretion &amp;mdash ; and he has had a score of trips without incident . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; His doctors told the panel he should remain detained for another year but was ready to be considered for unescorted community access &amp;mdash ; recreational activities and the like &amp;mdash ; to prepare him for ultimate release . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Born in Winnipeg , the second of three kids , Schoenborn developed alcohol and drug addictions early in life and began exhibiting signs of mental illness at age 19 . After dropping out of high school in Grade 10 , his life became a series of drunk-driving incidents and petty crime until he met his wife in 1993. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; But while he settled down , maintaining a stable relationship was impossible . He soon developed paranoid delusions that she was cheating on him and that their children were fathered by another man . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Before the slayings Schoenborn was estranged from his family , homeless and hearing voices . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Over the last year he has had about a score of escorted trips from the Colony Farm institution to nearby parks , then coffee shops and shopping centres . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Robert Lacroix , a forensic psychiatrist , told the panel Schoenborn was making progress . He has started counselling for substance use , completed a &amp;ldquo ; social communications skills&amp;rdquo ; course and has attended a cognitive behavioural therapy for psychosis program . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; At best , the review was told he will gradually move toward community reintegration during which his coping skills will need constant evaluation and support . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Meanwhile , Teixeira said the Clarke family and its supporters will continue to push for legislative change because the review board and the **26;11310;TOOLONG system is broken . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; The hospital is under no obligation to notify the community , the police , the family of the victims or anyone that he&amp;rsquo ; s on these outings , &amp;rdquo ; he emphasized . &amp;ldquo ; Also , should Schoenborn not return , they&amp;rsquo ; re under no obligation to report it to anyone . So it&amp;rsquo ; s very , very disappointing he&amp;rsquo ; s going to be given this level of freedom even if it is at the discretion of the director . It&amp;rsquo ; s a very sad time for the family for sure . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " **29;11338;TOOLONG " target= " blank " rel= " noopener " **36;11369;TOOLONG &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " http : //twitter.com/ianmulgrew " target= " blank " rel= " noopener " **31;11407;TOOLONG ... &lt;/p&gt; &lt;hr&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;em&gt;&lt;a href= " **27;11440;TOOLONG " &gt;CLICK HERE&lt;/a&gt; to report a typo. &lt;/em&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;em&gt;Is there more to this story ? " data-portal-copyright= " TBA " **28;11654;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1238868 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; Notorious B.C. child killer Allan Schoenborn is a tough sell for anyone who believes in the possibility of rehabilitation and the need to have compassion for those living with mental illness . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; His doctors think that while he should remain detained for another year , he is ready to be considered for unescorted community access &amp;mdash ; some recreational activity , lattes at Starbucks &amp;mdash ; to prepare him for ultimate release . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; He&amp;rsquo ; s actually functioning at a higher level than most patients , &amp;rdquo ; Vancouver lawyer Rishi Gill said after an annual hearing Thursday by the B.C. Review Board on Schoenborn&amp;rsquo ; s continued confinement . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; He&amp;rsquo ; s lucky he&amp;rsquo ; s getting the resources he&amp;rsquo ; s getting on the public dime. &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Schoenborn acknowledged to the three-member panel he had more work to do to win greater freedom and said he would try his best to meet any requirements imposed . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; In April 2008 , Schoenborn stabbed and smothered his daughter and two sons to death in their Merritt family trailer. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; He then fled into the bush , eluding a police dragnet for 10 days before emerging disheveled and incoherent &amp;mdash ; the only person with a worse mugshot than Charlie Manson or Nick Nolte. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The now 52-year-old was found not criminally responsible because of a mental disorder on three counts of first-degree murder on Feb. 22 , 2010 , and confined indefinitely under the care of the B.C. Review Board . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;11684;TOOLONG ... title= " Kaitlynne , Max and Cordon were murdered by their father Allan Schoenborn in 2008. " alt= " Kaitlynne , Max and Cordon were murdered by their father Allan Schoenborn in 2008. " data-portal-copyright= " Les Bazso " **28;11720;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 324266 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; He claimed he killed his kids &amp;mdash ; Kaitlynne , 10 , Max , 8 , and Corden , 5 , &amp;mdash ; because he believed they were being sexually abused and he wanted to save them from the crushing humiliation . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; He has been incarcerated since , but at his review last year the board agreed he should be permitted escorted community access at the director&amp;rsquo ; s discretion &amp;mdash ; and he has had 19 trips without incident . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Gill said his client was managed by a team that included a psychiatrist , psychologist , and occupational therapist and they would remain in control if the board approved the possibility for greater liberty . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; He can not ask for a pass into the community on his own , his team has to agree it&amp;rsquo ; s appropriate and recommend it , &amp;rdquo ; Gill emphasized . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; This is a very closely monitored type of situation . It&amp;rsquo ; s to calibrate his ability to be out in public so that they can figure out what the eventual end goal is , where is he in the context of our hope based on the law that we have to get him ready to re-integrate him into society ? &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Born in Winnipeg , the second of three kids , Schoenborn&amp;rsquo ; s childhood was unremarkable &amp;mdash ; his parents divorced when he was about 11 and he initially moved to the Lower Mainland with his mother . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; At 14 , he moved to Camrose to live with his father . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; He completed Grade 9 , developed a thirst for alcohol and a taste for drugs , and became a roofer with a history of petty crime &amp;mdash ; B&amp;amp ; Es , mischief , assault , drunk driving , and flouting legal orders &amp;mdash ; until 1993 when he met his wife . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; But Schoenborn came to fear she was unfaithful and frequently questioned the paternity of his children &amp;mdash ; signs of his growing psychiatric illness . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; In 1987 when he was 19 , Schoenborn had been hospitalized with auditory hallucinations in Camrose and claimed to have spoken to Jesus . He was discharged but received no psychiatric follow-up . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; In 1999 , he crashed his car while rushing his 15-month-old daughter to hospital for fear she had been sexually assaulted by his wife and her supposed lover . As a result , he was admitted to Royal Columbian Hospital for more than a month of psychiatric care before he left , against medical advice . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Just before the slayings , Schoenborn was again suspicious about his children being groomed for prostitution , was hearing voices and becoming more and more paranoid . He was living in a tent or his car in the Lower Mainland , bankrupt . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Prosecutors insisted he killed the children to get revenge on his wife , Darcie Clarke , who died of undisclosed causes on May 30 last year . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; In 2011 , escorted absences were approved for Schoenborn , but the Crown had the decision revoked. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Escorted absences were again approved by the board in 2015 , triggering a two-year fight by prosecutors to have him declared a &amp;ldquo ; high-risk . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; But then-B.C . Supreme Court Justice Martha Devlin concluded Schoenborn&amp;rsquo ; s progress , mental condition and the opinions of experts provided no reason to believe he posed a serious enough threat to warrant the label . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; However , he was not approved for an escorted pass because of anger management and interpersonal challenges until last year . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Since then , Schoenborn has had &amp;ldquo ; staff supported&amp;rdquo ; trips , at first to parks , then coffee shops and shopping centres . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Robert Lacroix , a forensic psychiatrist , said that reflected his progress . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; In the last year , Schoenborn has started counselling for substance use , completed a &amp;ldquo ; Social Communications Skills&amp;rdquo ; course and he has attended a Cognitive Behavioural Therapy for Psychosis program . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Although forensic security officers initially accompanied Schoenborn&amp;rsquo ; s designated staff , now they occur without security and with other patients . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Potential destinations for unescorted leave could include community rehabilitative programs , recreational outings or occupational programs , the therapists said . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; While at times irritable and abrasive , Schoenborn is said to cooperate with ward routine and rules , but remains a lightning rod for some patients who taunt and target him . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; Imagine if you were under this level of scrutiny , would you only have a handful of incidents in a year where you wished you&amp;rsquo ; d acted differently ? &amp;rdquo ; Gill said . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Schoenborn&amp;rsquo ; s illness is apparently in &amp;ldquo ; full remission&amp;rdquo ; and he reputedly has improved personal insight . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; His team worries about two situations if he is released . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The first involves violence resulting from a psychotic episode triggered by delusions of jealousy or paranoia if Schoenborn stopped taking his meds or relapsed into substance use . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; But such symptoms , the experts assured , would evolve over a period of months and were not likely to go undetected in a structured setting or with regular psychiatric follow-up . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The second scenario involved violence sparked by his &amp;ldquo ; reactive anger&amp;rdquo ; when provoked . Schoenborn is engaged in psychotherapy to help exert self-control . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; But his only support is his mother . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; At best , the review was told he will require a gradual progression toward community reintegration during which his coping skills will need constant evaluation and support . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; This is the healthiest I&amp;rsquo ; ve ever seen him and I&amp;rsquo ; ve known him for 12 years now , &amp;rdquo ; Gill said . &amp;ldquo ; He&amp;rsquo ; s not ready for release , but we&amp;rsquo ; re not asking for that . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Dave Teixeira , a family spokesman who attended the review with Darcie&amp;rsquo ; s brother Mike , said : &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; The family and I are hopeful there will be no more additional privileges , but we&amp;rsquo ; re also somewhat understanding that this may go through , and so what could happen is over the next three , six , 12 months the director here at Colony Farm could allow Schoenborn into our community in about a 15-minute radius of this hospital &amp;mdash ; so that means elementary schools , soccer pitches , Coquitlam Centre Mall , downtown Coquitlam , where he will be allowed to roam about . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The panel reserved its decision . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " **29;11750;TOOLONG " target= " blank " rel= " noopener " **36;11781;TOOLONG &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " http : //twitter.com/ianmulgrew " target= " blank " rel= " noopener " **31;11819;TOOLONG ... &lt;/p&gt; Ian MulgrewFri , 13 Mar 2020 01:25:45 **45;11852;TOOLONG ... Mulgrew : Money probe requires secrecyhttp : **33;11899;TOOLONG ... &lt;img src= " https : **34;11934;TOOLONG ... title= " The commission of inquiry into money-laundering in B.C. is being led by Austin Cullen . " alt= " The commission of inquiry into money-laundering in B.C. is being led by Austin Cullen . " data-portal-copyright= " Jason Payne " **28;11970;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1237338 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; An inscrutable B.C. Court of Appeal decision closing the door on the public in a securities investigation has raised a legal quandary that will face the Cullen Commission into money-laundering. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The province&amp;rsquo ; s high bench took the rare step on March 4 of sealing and anonymizing a case because of the nature of financial investigations and the potentially devastating effects on targeted but innocent individuals and companies . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; It is called &amp;ldquo ; Party A v. British Columbia ( Securities Commission ) &amp;rdquo ; , although it involves the full Sesame Street gang &amp;mdash ; &amp;ldquo ; Party A , Party B , Party C , Party D , and Party E , &amp;rdquo ; which is about all I can tell you . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; In the opaque &lt;a href= " https : **33;12000;TOOLONG ... target= " blank " rel= " noopener " &gt;oral judgment&lt;/a&gt; published Wednesday , Justice Mary Saunders explained why the unusual cloaking procedures were necessary . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; These are the same conundrums that will dog former B.C. Supreme Court associate chief justice Austin Cullen later this year at his inquiry&amp;rsquo ; s evidentiary hearings &amp;mdash ; trying to get to the bottom of misconduct in public is fraught with difficulty because of the damage that can be done to innocent parties . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; From the start , Cullen has maintained he is strongly committed to ensuring the commission respects the rights of individuals , companies and agencies and protects their privacy , legal interests and reputations . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; But those issues are not easily addressed , as shown in this appeal that arose out of a 2019 Securities Commission investigation . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Under the financial policing system , an investigation precedes a &amp;ldquo ; notice of hearing , &amp;rdquo ; an indictment outlining specific allegations parties must answer and explain . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Investigations can be carried out without a notice of hearing ever being issued , just as police investigations can end without charges being laid . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; And like a police inquiry , just the fact the investigation occurred may harm individual reputations and the capital markets in situations where no wrongdoing may ever be alleged . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; As a result , not surprisingly , people who think they might be mentioned at the Cullen Commission have already lawyered up &amp;mdash ; an apparently wise move given that at its public meetings , half-baked accusations were flung around without censure or correction . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; In this case , the alphabet cartel wanted the securities investigation and any mention it took place sealed and anonymized &amp;mdash ; and the commission agreed . So did Saunders . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Proceedings in the Supreme Court dealing with a constitutional issue arising from the same investigation , Saunders noted , are not &amp;ldquo ; sealed&amp;rdquo ; because that file itself does not involve inherently sensitive information . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Nonetheless , efforts have been made to anonymize those proceedings , too , she added . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; There is always concern on the part of this court when orders are sought that would seal a file , &amp;rdquo ; Saunders acknowledged . &amp;nbsp ; &amp;ldquo ; The principle of the open court is important and we do not lightly seal a file , the act of which has the effect of foreclosing public knowledge of the contents of the file . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Quoting a financial regulatory expert , the justice said public knowledge that an investigation was conducted would significantly harm a venture capital business and the personal reputations of those involved : &amp;ldquo ; It is also possible that persons could draw connections to other companies and individuals related to the applicants and damage the business or reputations of those other companies . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; What heightens the unfairness , in this case , is that the disclosure of the issuance of a freeze order will leave the public with the erroneous impression that the applicants have engaged in some kind of wrongdoing when no such inference can or should be drawn at this stage . In addition , in my experience , there is almost nothing the applicants&amp;rsquo ; will be able to say or do to counteract the prejudice they will undoubtedly suffer by this premature and misleading disclosure . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; In my opinion , the integrity of this investigation , the capital markets in general and the applicants , in particular , will all unfairly suffer harm from premature disclosure of this investigation . To that extent , in my opinion , it would be contrary to the public interest in the capital markets for these proceedings to proceed in a public setting . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; She issued the extraordinary sealing order . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Cullen has a similar Gordian knot &amp;mdash ; the mere accusation of money-laundering tarnishes , no matter the reality , while marketplace repercussions and the damage to the innocent can&amp;rsquo ; t be ignored . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; But since he is charged with conducting a public inquiry , I don&amp;rsquo ; t think Cullen&amp;rsquo ; s got the same leeway as Saunders to respect the presumption of innocence . It&amp;rsquo ; s going to be harder for him to close the door on the hearsay and speculation that has permeated the money-laundering discussion . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Especially given the **26;12035;TOOLONG ; s and the public&amp;rsquo ; s desire for identifiable culprits . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " **29;12063;TOOLONG " target= " blank " rel= " noopener " **36;12094;TOOLONG &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " http : //twitter.com/ianmulgrew " target= " blank " rel= " noopener " **31;12132;TOOLONG ... &lt;/p&gt; Ian MulgrewWed , 11 Mar 2020 23:32:58 **45;12165;TOOLONG ... drop hits bottom line for Vancouver airport , businesseshttp : **30;12212;TOOLONG ... &lt;img src= " https : **34;12244;TOOLONG ... title= " Amy Wilson of the Lattimer Gallery at Vancouver International Airport . Institute of Technology . She said Tuesday was the first time she was wearing a mask on an airplane. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; While the Tiedes and others said the virus was &amp;ldquo ; a non-issue&amp;rdquo ; on their vacation , other travellers have decided to stay home , and airlines have cancelled flights to international destinations because of the virus , of which there are 39 cases in B.C. , including one death , and 93 Canada wide . There are more than 100,000 cases globally . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; As a result , the international departures hall at Vancouver International Airport has been quieter than usual , and many people are wearing face masks . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; From behind the counter at Lattimer Gallery YVR , manager Amy Wilson has a perfect view of the comings and goings in the terminal . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; Since January , basically , just as things started to kind of appear on the news , we just saw reduced passenger flow through the airport . A lot of that had to do with cancellation of flights through to China , &amp;rdquo ; Wilson said . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The airport did not have recent figures for passenger volumes , but said in an emailed statement that flights from Mainland China to YVR decreased by 50 per cent in February , and it anticipates a similar lower number of flights for March . An official was unable to provide a specific number of flights coming from China . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;12412;TOOLONG ... title= " Face masks at Vancouver International Airport . " alt= " Face masks at Vancouver International Airport . " data-portal-copyright= " NICK PROCAYLO " **28;12448;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1236450 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; As of Tuesday there had been no decrease in the number of flights from anywhere else internationally , but that day Air Canada announced that it would be immediately suspending flights between Italy and Canada until May 1 . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The Public Health Agency of Canada has recommended that Canadians avoid all cruise ship travel due to the outbreak , and travel health notices have been issued for China , France , Germany , Hong Kong , Iran , Japan , northern Italy , Singapore , South Korea and Spain . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; In the statement , airport CEO Craig Richmond said they are forecasting a three- to five-per-cent drop in passenger numbers for 2020 , down from last year&amp;rsquo ; s record of 26.4 million passengers . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The airport is also expecting a five- to 10-per-cent decline in revenue for this year thanks to less income from landing fees and less spending at the airport itself . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; However , we are confident in the airport&amp;rsquo ; s ability to weather the current economic environment . We have been through similar challenges in the past , including the global economic downturn in 2008-2009 and SARS in 2002-2003 , and these experiences are reflected in our responsible approach to growth , &amp;rdquo ; Richmond said in the statement . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The reduction in flights and passengers is also hitting the businesses in the airport . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; Like all the retailers at the airport , we&amp;rsquo ; re very dependent on the flow of traffic coming through the airport , &amp;rdquo ; said Wilson . &amp;ldquo ; So as we&amp;rsquo ; re seeing a decrease in the number of customers coming into the airport , obviously the times we would normally be busy with our high-capacity flights are less busy than they normally would be . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; At Absolute Spa , which has five locations in the airport , stylist Bella Wegmuller said business has slowed significantly . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; Passengers are scared to travel , &amp;rdquo ; she said . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Pharmasave at YVR is doing brisk business in hand sanitizer , wipes and face masks , and as of Tuesday was also sold out of zinc , which a clerk said customers believe will protect them from the virus , though that claim is unproven. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The businesses that spoke to Postmedia noted that the airport is taking cleanliness and safety seriously , and checking in regularly with them . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; They take safety of the passengers as the most important thing , &amp;rdquo ; said Wegmuller. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The airport has implemented passenger screening measures as recommended by the Public Health Agency of Canada , such as asking questions on customs kiosks , and stepped up cleaning of handrails , washrooms and other terminal areas that are touched often . &lt;/p&gt; Health officials confirm seven new cases in **25;12661;TOOLONG &lt;/ul&gt; Jennifer SaltmanWed , 11 Mar 2020 03:58:08 **45;12688;TOOLONG ... **26;12735;TOOLONG fallout from coronavirus set to squeeze B.C. budgethttp : **34;12763;TOOLONG ... &lt;img src= " https : **34;12799;TOOLONG ... title= " The global economic drop caused by the coronavirus , seen here on March 9 at the New York Stock Exchange , will eventually impact B.C. 's provincial budget . " alt= " The global economic drop caused by the coronavirus , seen here on March 9 at the New York Stock Exchange , will eventually impact B.C. 's provincial budget . " data-portal-copyright= " TIMOTHY A. CLARY " **28;12835;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1234965 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; VICTORIA &amp;mdash ; The worldwide economic crunch caused by the coronavirus epidemic will eventually drag down B.C.&amp;rsquo ; s budget , but business and labour leaders are urging the province to push through the downturn and spend whatever is necessary in the crisis . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Steep losses this week in stock markets , the collapse of oil prices , and the curtailment of cruise ships and travel will hit B.C. through reductions in corporate and personal tax revenues , in hotel tax revenue , and in export revenue , said Ken Peacock , chief economist at the B.C. Business Council . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The business council has revised its&amp;nbsp ; projected growth of B.C.&amp;rsquo ; s gross domestic product to 1.4 per cent for 2020 , down from two per cent projected in January . The provincial budget pegged GDP growth at 1.9 per cent , meaning the province is already off target . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; There&amp;rsquo ; s really no data on economic impact so far , &amp;rdquo ; said Peacock , citing the risk of cancellations of conferences , sporting events and other large-scale public gatherings . &amp;ldquo ; But we will almost certainly be revising our look down again . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Premier John Horgan said the province will face &amp;ldquo ; significant challenges&amp;rdquo ; to its economy in the short-term as a result of the coronavirus , especially because of to B.C.&amp;rsquo ; s close economic ties to Asian countries hardest hit by the COVID-19 disease . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; We have what is now a pandemic and we have extraordinary challenges within markets around the world , &amp;rdquo ; Horgan told the Surrey Board of Trade on Tuesday . &amp;ldquo ; We&amp;rsquo ; ve seen catastrophic falls within the Dow and Toronto exchanges . But all governments can do is prepare for the worst and hope for the best . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; British Columbia is an open economy with a diverse population , so it&amp;rsquo ; s not a surprise we would have a significant number of cases here , among the highest in the country . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The 2020 provincial budget , which begins April 1 , projects a $227 million surplus on $60 billion in spending , with a $300 million forecast allowance and $600 million contingency fund . That $900 million fiscal cushion is &amp;ldquo ; very prudent , &amp;rdquo ; said Horgan . However , government also draws money to fight seasonal forest fires from the same contingency fund . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The budget earmarked billions more for new capital spending , bringing the three-year plan to $22.9 billion in projects &amp;mdash ; some of which could be fast-tracked to stimulate the provincial economy and generate jobs . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; We can anticipate economic disruption , &amp;rdquo ; said Horgan. &amp;ldquo ; The challenge from government is we can&amp;rsquo ; t predict from moment to moment where those consequences will be . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Finance Minister Carole James had to introduce new taxes and cut spending to achieve the small surplus projection. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;iframe src= " visualisation/1296452 " &gt;&lt;/iframe&gt; ... &lt;p&gt; With the economic downturn , and pressure on the health care system , the government should stop worrying about trying to post a surplus , said Peacock . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; Right now is not the time to be worried about deficits , &amp;rdquo ; he said . &amp;ldquo ; And fortunately for B.C. we are in a very strong fiscal position . We have low debt loads , and government absolutely can borrow some money and run a deficit for 2020 if it requires devoting money to health care . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The province should also use that fiscal room to help pay for sick days for employees who are being told by public health experts not to come to work when ill , said Laird Cronk , president of the B.C. Federation of Labour . Some workers have paid sick days in collective agreements or contracts , but thousands more do not and need government help , he said . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; What we don&amp;rsquo ; t want is workers having the untenable decision of saying do I self-isolate and disclose I&amp;rsquo ; m not feeling well and not go to work and risk possibly not having not having a job to come back to , &amp;rdquo ; said Cronk. &amp;ldquo ; Or even if I do have a job to come back to I&amp;rsquo ; d lose 14 days of pay and lose my rent . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Prime Minister Justin Trudeau is expected to announce federal support measures for the economy and unemployment insurance on Tuesday . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; B.C. has little revenue in the budget from oil production and is far less hurt by the collapse in oil prices than Alberta . The $40 million projected for oil production in B.C. in 2020 is based on an American spot market price that had dropped almost in half as of Tuesday . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; However , natural gas is a major economic driver for B.C. Every 25 cent drop in the price of natural gas on the global energy market costs B.C. $30 to $40 million in lost revenue . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Tourism is also a significant part of the provincial economy . Health Minister Adrian Dix has urged people to reconsider travel on cruise ships , which account for more than $3.2 billion in economic activity annually in Canada &amp;mdash ; much of which is centred on popular ports of call like Vancouver and Victoria . On Tuesday , B.C.&amp;rsquo ; s chief medical health officer , Dr. Bonnie Henry , said the start of the cruise ship season should be delayed . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; A few channels the economy will be impacted through most immediately are airlines , tourism and the related accommodation sector , &amp;rdquo ; said Peacock . &amp;ldquo ; That&amp;rsquo ; s going to a big hit . It was a big hit in SARS and it will be a big hit now . Tourism is even more important to the B.C. economy than it was in 2003 . It&amp;rsquo ; s a growth engine . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The extent of the economic impact to B.C. also depends on any drop in household income , jobs and spending caused by the virus . A one percentage point drop in 2020 B.C. household income growth costs the provincial treasury up to $115 million , and one percentage point drop in provincial sales tax equates to $25 million in lost revenue , for example . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The B.C. government could stimulate the economy by rolling back some of its taxation penalties on real estate and property ownership , allowing that sector to rise again and buffer the province from declines elsewhere , said Andrey Pavlov , a professor of finance at the Simon Fraser University&amp;rsquo ; s Beedie School of Business . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; Roll back all these measures , roll back the speculation and vacancy tax , the school tax , rent controls , rental only zoning , roll that back so that we can allow arguably the most important sector in our economy to flourish and that will help protect and insulate us from overall economic declines , &amp;rdquo ; said Pavlov. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Short of that , Pavlov urged people to not overreact to the economic declines caused by the virus . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; It has been said time and time again , do not sell in a panic , &amp;rdquo ; he said . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Finance Minister James was not available for an interview Tuesday but in a statement her ministry said it is continually monitoring the effect of the virus and working with other provinces to mitigate the impact . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; At the moment we expect that the economic impacts will be temporary , and we know that B.C.&amp;rsquo ; s economy is well diversified and resilient as we continue with the largest infrastructure investment in the province&amp;rsquo ; s history , &amp;rdquo ; said her ministry . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; B.C.&amp;rsquo ; s economy is well diversified but not immune to any slowdown in the global economy . The full extent and impact felt from COVID-19 may not be known for a while , but we are monitoring the situation and working with economic partners across the country . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " **26;12865;TOOLONG " **33;12893;TOOLONG &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " http : **27;12928;TOOLONG " &gt;t ... &lt;/p&gt; Rob ShawWed , 11 Mar 2020 00:35:59 **45;12957;TOOLONG ... **26;13004;TOOLONG Bramham : Canada 's other public health crisis also needs urgent attentionhttp : **31;13032;TOOLONG ... &lt;img src= " https : **34;13065;TOOLONG ... title= " Sharene Shuster 's son , Jordan Hunter Carhoun , died of a fentanyl overdose in August 2018 . She was one of more than 50 mothers who marked their children 's death at Vancouver 's Jericho Beach in November . She was one of more than 50 mothers who marked their children 's death at Vancouver 's Jericho Beach in November . " data-portal-copyright= " Francis Georgian " **28;13101;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1233640 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; There is a very real and deadly health crisis in B.C. from which two people died yesterday and two more will likely die today , tomorrow and the days after that . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; It&amp;rsquo ; s not COVID-19 , and no news conference was hastily called to talk about it . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Most of those dead and dying are blue-collar guys in what should be the prime of their lives . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; This is the reality as B.C. lurches into the fifth year of an opioid overdose crisis . It&amp;rsquo ; s a seemingly unending emergency that by the end of 2019 had already killed 5,539 people here and more than 13,900 across Canada . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Five years in , this crisis has become normalized , with the only certainty as we face another day is that first responders are now better at resuscitating victims because , year over year , the calls have only continued to increase . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Last week , Prime Minister Justin Trudeau appointed his top ministers to a committee tasked with responding to the COVID-19 crisis . At that point , Canada had only 30 confirmed cases . Of the 21 B.C. cases , four of the patients have fully recovered . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Not to belittle the concerns about COVID-19 becoming a global pandemic , but with nearly 14,000 dead already , no committee &amp;mdash ; high-level or otherwise &amp;mdash ; has yet been struck to devise a national addictions strategy that would deal not only with opioids , but also the biggest killer , which is alcohol . A 2019 report by the Canadian Institute for Health Information found that 10 Canadians die every day from substance use , and three-quarters of those deaths are alcohol-related. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; During the 2019 election , the issue flared briefly after Conservatives placed ads &amp;mdash ; mainly through ethnic media &amp;mdash ; claiming that Trudeau&amp;rsquo ; s Liberals planned to legalize all drugs , including heroin . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Already beleaguered , Trudeau not only denied it , he quickly disavowed the resolution overwhelmingly passed at the party&amp;rsquo ; s 2018 convention that called on the Canadian government to treat addiction as a health issue , expand treatment and harm reduction services , and decriminalize personal-use possession of all drugs , with people diverted away from the criminal courts and into treatment . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Trudeau disavowed it again this week when a Liberal backbencher&amp;rsquo ; s private member&amp;rsquo ; s bill was put on the order paper . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;13131;TOOLONG ... title= " Liberal member of Parliament Nathaniel Erskine-Smith ( in front ) pictured in 2018. " alt= " Liberal member of Parliament Nathaniel Erskine-Smith ( in front ) pictured in 2018. " data-portal-copyright= " Adrian Wyld/The Canadian Press " **28;13167;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 919119 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; Depending on how you read Bill C-236 , it&amp;rsquo ; s either calling for decriminalization or legalization . Regardless , the fact that Nathaniel Erskine-Smith&amp;rsquo ; s bill will be debated at least gets it on the political agenda because unless there are some major changes , Canadians are going to continue dying at these unacceptably high rates that have already caused the national life expectancy to drop . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Erskine-Smith , an Ontario MP from the Beaches-East York riding , favours a Portugal-style plan of which decriminalization plays only a small part . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; But parliamentary rules forbid private member&amp;rsquo ; s bills from committing the government to any new spending , so he said his bill could only narrowly focus on decriminalization. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The slim bill says charges could be laid &amp;ldquo ; only if &amp;hellip ; the individual can not be adequately dealt with by a warning or referral ( to a program agency or service provider ) &amp;hellip ; or by way of alternative measures . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Erskine-Smith disagreed with the suggestion that it gives too much discretionary power to police &amp;mdash ; especially since in B.C. , it&amp;rsquo ; s prosecutors , not police , who determine whether charges are laid . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Still , what he proposes is quite different from what happens in Portugal . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; There , police have no discretionary power . People found with illicit drugs are arrested and taken to the police station where the drugs are weighed , and the person is either charged with possession and sent to court or diverted to the Commission for the Dissuasion of Drug Use to meet with social workers , therapists and addictions specialists who map out a plan . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Since private members&amp;rsquo ; bills rarely pass , Erskine-Smith doesn&amp;rsquo ; t hold out much hope for his . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; It created a firestorm on social media , with some recovery advocates pitted against advocates for harm reduction , including full legalization. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;h2 class= " cat-head short " **40;13197;TOOLONG ... &lt;ul class= " relatedlinks " &gt; &lt;li&gt;&lt;a href= " https : **33;13239;TOOLONG ... deaths decrease in B.C. , but officials say safer drug supply **27;13274;TOOLONG &lt;li&gt;&lt;a href= " https : **33;13303;TOOLONG ... Bramham : It 's not enough to just keep overdose victims **26;13338;TOOLONG &lt;li&gt;&lt;a href= " https : **33;13366;TOOLONG ... mothers highlight devastating impact of opioid crisis in **30;13401;TOOLONG &lt;/ul&gt; &lt;p&gt; Federal Conservatives also repeated their trope that drug legalization is part of Trudeau&amp;rsquo ; s secret agenda . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Meanwhile , Alberta&amp;rsquo ; s United Conservative government inflamed some harm-reduction advocates with the release of a report on the adverse social and economic impacts of safe consumption sites , even though it didn&amp;rsquo ; t recommend shutting them down . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The report acknowledged that they play an important role in a continuum of care , but it also called for beefed-up enforcement to lessen the chaos that often surrounds them . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The committee questioned some data provided to them that suggested Lethbridge &amp;mdash ; population 92,730 &amp;mdash ; may be the world&amp;rsquo ; s most-used injection site . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The committee also questioned why some operators report all adverse events , including non-life-threatening ones as overdoses , leaving the impression that without the sites &amp;ldquo ; thousands of people would have fatally overdosed. &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Among its recommendations are better data collection using standardized definitions as well as better tracking of users to determine whether they are being referred to other services . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; More than a year ago , Canadians overwhelmingly told the Angus Reid Institute that they supported mandatory treatment for opioid addiction . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Nearly half said they were willing to consider decriminalization . Nearly half also said that neither Ottawa nor the provinces were doing enough to ease the epidemic . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; It seems Canadians are eager for change even if they&amp;rsquo ; re not yet certain what it should look like . The only ones who seem reluctant are the politicians . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " **29;13433;TOOLONG " target= " blank " rel= " noopener " **36;13464;TOOLONG &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " https : **27;13502;TOOLONG " target= " blank " rel= " noopener " **29;13531;TOOLONG &; ... &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;em&gt;&lt;a href= " **27;13562;TOOLONG " &gt;CLICK HERE&lt;/a&gt; to report a typo. &lt;/em&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;em&gt;Is there more to this story ?
