Todd : Inequality , like Vancouver 's , gives rise to anxiety , depressionhttp : **30;13752;TOOLONG ... &lt;img src= " https : **34;13784;TOOLONG ... title= " Luxury sports cars are part of the daily scene on Metro Vancouver streets . ' There are studies that show that people in more unequal areas are more likely to spend money on flashy stuff , particularly flashy-looking cars , ' says British epidemiologist Richard Wilkinson . " alt= " Luxury sports cars are part of the daily scene on Metro Vancouver streets . ' There are studies that show that people in more unequal areas are more likely to spend money on flashy stuff , particularly flashy-looking cars , ' says British epidemiologist Richard Wilkinson . " data-portal-copyright= " NICK PROCAYLO " **28;13820;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1231222 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; Inequality is bad for your mental health . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Famed British epidemiologist Richard Wilkinson couldn&amp;rsquo ; t be coming to a more appropriate city to deliver his message that material inequality has powerful , negative psychological effects . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Metro Vancouver could be the poster child for a society with sharp disparities in wealth , which Wilkinson has shown makes people more prone to such things as heart disease , anxiety and drug use . The rich or established in this city have sparkling homes while the rest agonize over getting by in one of the most unaffordable housing markets in the world . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Exacerbating the wealth gap , Metro Vancouver&amp;rsquo ; s median wages are ninth worst of Canada&amp;rsquo ; s 10 largest cities . Metro Vancouver ranks a dismal 50th for wages when compared to North American cities , according to Andy Yan , head of Simon Fraser University&amp;rsquo ; s City Program . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Meanwhile , despite the struggles of the city&amp;rsquo ; s lower and middle classes , Metro Vancouver has won the dubious honour of being dubbed the &lt;a href= " http : **34;13850;TOOLONG ... target= " blank " rel= " noopener " &gt; " supercar capital " of the world&lt;/a&gt; , with the number of Aston Martins , Bugattis and Rolls Royces running far out of proportion to the population . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The influential co-author of the &lt;a href= " https : **33;13886;TOOLONG ... target= " blank " rel= " noopener " &gt;The Inner Level : How More Equal Societies Reduce Stress , Restore Sanity and Improve Everyone 's Well-Being&lt;/a&gt; has worked with Kate Pickett for more than a decade to map out just how much the realities and perceptions of inequality contribute to fragile egos , addiction , depression , obesity and violence . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Compared to other democratic countries , Wilkinson&amp;rsquo ; s research shows , Canada is in the middle of the pack in the gap between rich and poor , less divided by money than the U.S. , but &lt;a href= " http : **34;13921;TOOLONG ... target= " blank " rel= " noopener " &gt;far less equal than Norway , Denmark , Austria and Japan&lt;/a&gt; . Still , the Canadian Centre for Policy Alternatives says Canada&amp;rsquo ; s wealthiest 87 families collectively own the same amount as the lowest-earning 12 million Canadians . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;13957;TOOLONG ... title= " Narcissism levels rise in unequal societies , says British epidemiologist Richard Wilkinson , yet low self-esteem is more likely , including feeling compelled to self-medicate with alcohol and drugs . " alt= " Narcissism levels rise in unequal societies , says British epidemiologist Richard Wilkinson , yet low self-esteem is more likely , including feeling compelled to self-medicate with alcohol and drugs . " data-portal-copyright= " JohnHoulihan.com " **28;13993;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1231368 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; Metro Vancouverites will be able to hear about the wide-ranging downsides of economic hierarchies when Wilkinson , professor emeritus at the University of Nottingham&amp;rsquo ; s Medical School , gives a free lecture at the Queen Elizabeth Theatre at 7 p.m. on April 2 , as part of SFU&amp;rsquo ; s Public Square Community Summit . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Both Wilkinson and Pickett , a professor of epidemiology at the University of York , have been in-demand speakers since their bestseller The Spirit Level was published in 2009 , winning multiple awards . It provided devastating evidence that both those on low incomes , and perhaps surprisingly the rich , are all hurt by inequality . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; In a telephone interview from his home in York , England , Wilkinson said he&amp;rsquo ; s been to Canada and Vancouver several times and generally finds the country &amp;ldquo ; clean and modern and not as hard-edged as the U.S.&amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Wilkinson talked about how the &lt;a href= " http : **34;14023;TOOLONG ... target= " blank " rel= " noopener " &gt;prevalence of luxury sports cars&lt;/a&gt; in Metro Vancouver and elsewhere can be excruciating for those who witness them . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;14059;TOOLONG ... title= " Despite many displays of opulence in Metro Vancouver , this chart shows that median household incomes in the city rank a dismal 50th among North American cities . ( Source : Andy Yan , Simon Fraser University City Program ) " alt= " Despite many displays of opulence in Metro Vancouver , this chart shows that median household incomes in the city rank a dismal 50th among North American cities . ( Source : Andy Yan , Simon Fraser University City Program ) " data-portal-copyright= " " **28;14095;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1231063 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; There are studies that show that people in more unequal areas are more likely to spend money on flashy stuff , particularly flashy-looking cars , &amp;rdquo ; Wilkinson said , noting supercars are few and far between in his hometown , located two hours north of London . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; I remember reading how sports cars are a bit of a nuisance , because you can&amp;rsquo ; t park them by the side of the road because people deliberately scratch them . That shows resentment . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The perception of &lt;a href= " http : **34;14125;TOOLONG ... target= " blank " rel= " noopener " &gt;extreme inequality&lt;/a&gt; that goes along with everything from high-end cars to glittering jewellery outlets to overbuilt mansions , Wilkinson said , can cause people to feel inferior and lack in confidence , which can lead them to withdraw and feel depressed . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; However , others , as we argue in the book , pick themselves up . For their own self-advancement they start to flaunt their professional achievements , instead of being modest about them , &amp;rdquo ; said the expert in the so-called social determinants of health . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; If you&amp;rsquo ; re worried about how you&amp;rsquo ; re seen and judged in terms of status , then you either feel overcome with insecure self-worth or you respond the opposite way and try to show yourself off in a very positive light , like by buying expensive cars . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " http : **34;14161;TOOLONG ... target= " blank " rel= " noopener " &gt;Narcissism levels rise in unequal societies&lt;/a&gt; , says Wilkinson , yet low self-esteem is more likely , including feeling compelled to self-medicate with alcohol and drugs . Which makes one wonder whether it&amp;rsquo ; s more than a coincidence that Metro Vancouver is one of the centres of the opioid crisis . The city accounts for more than its share of the 2.5 people in the province dying each day of overdoses , &lt;a href= " http : **34;14197;TOOLONG ... target= " blank " rel= " noopener " &gt;four out of five being men . &lt;/a&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Wilkinson finds Canada&amp;rsquo ; s little-discussed gender breakdown on overdose deaths intriguing , since the current focus in Britain is on the rising number of drug fatalities among women . The grim subject deserves further research , he suggested , since evidence suggests it is more common for women who are stressed to &amp;ldquo ; eat for comfort . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;h2 class= " cat-head short " **40;14233;TOOLONG ... &lt;ul class= " relatedlinks " &gt; &lt;li&gt;&lt;a href= " http : **34;14275;TOOLONG ... Todd : Crisis of wealth inequality simmers during **29;14311;TOOLONG &lt;li&gt;&lt;a href= " http : **34;14342;TOOLONG ... Todd : Five thinkers offer fresh takes on race , identity and **29;14378;TOOLONG &lt;li&gt;&lt;a href= " http : **34;14409;TOOLONG ... Todd : Death , taxes and the terrible injustice of Metro Vancouver 's housing **27;14445;TOOLONG &lt;/ul&gt; &lt;p&gt; There are so many correlations between inequity and ill health that Wilkinson , 76 , wonders why other researchers didn&amp;rsquo ; t pick up on the theme much earlier . &amp;ldquo ; It seems to be something we don&amp;rsquo ; t like to recognize , the feeling of being ill at ease . &amp;rdquo ; He notes studies suggest 50 per cent of Canadians have struggled with some form of mental illness by the age of 40. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Asked whether being a member of an ethnic , gender or sexual-orientation group can exacerbate a sense of inequality , Wilkinson says sometimes they can be &amp;ldquo ; markers for low social status . &amp;rdquo ; But , he says , &amp;ldquo ; &lt;a href= " http : **34;14474;TOOLONG ... target= " blank " rel= " noopener " &gt;we 're less racist than we used to be&lt;/a&gt; &amp;rdquo ; and stigmas surrounding gender &amp;ldquo ; are not as bad as they were . &amp;rdquo ; It is material differences that are most crucial for establishing social hierarchies . **39;14510;TOOLONG &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; What can be done about economic inequality ? &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Wilkinson and Pickett have felt pressured to try to answer such questions since they first published The Spirit Level . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Even though Wilkinson emphasizes he&amp;rsquo ; s &amp;ldquo ; not an economist , &amp;rdquo ; the final section of their latest book contains ideas on how to achieve more materially equal societies and a more environmentally sustainable world . The authors criticize the growth mantra of capitalism . And they recommend &amp;ldquo ; expanding democracy into the economy , &amp;rdquo ; including through mandatory employee representation on company boards . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; While the proposed solutions of Wilkinson and Pickett are leading to much worthwhile debate about the future of societies , there remains no reason to doubt their hard-earned research reveals the world , and Metro Vancouver , have distinct inequality problems . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " **26;14551;TOOLONG " target= " blank " rel= " noopener " **33;14579;TOOLONG &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " https : **25;14614;TOOLONG " target= " blank " rel= " noopener " **31;14641;TOOLONG ... &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;em&gt;&lt;a href= " **27;14674;TOOLONG " &gt;CLICK HERE&lt;/a&gt; to report a typo. &lt;/em&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;em&gt;Is there more to this story ? " data-portal-copyright= " JONATHAN HAYWARD " **28;15260;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1228971 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; British Columbia Indigenous Relations Minister Scott Fraser acknowledged there was a disagreement over the natural gas pipeline going through traditional territory . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; We are developing a protocol here &amp;hellip ; to recognize rights and titles for the future , &amp;rdquo ; he said . &amp;ldquo ; I ask for some space and calm to continue that good work , and allow the Wet&amp;rsquo ; suwet&amp;rsquo ; en people to review the arrangement and to endorse it . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Chief Woos , one of the Wet&amp;rsquo ; suwet&amp;rsquo ; en hereditary leaders , called the draft a milestone for everyone involved but said the &amp;ldquo ; degree of satisfaction is not what we expected . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; He stressed that the hereditary chiefs remain opposed to the pipeline in their traditional territory . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; We are going to be continuing to look at some more conversations with B.C. and of course with the proponent and further conversation with the RCMP , &amp;rdquo ; Woos said . &amp;ldquo ; It&amp;rsquo ; s not over yet . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; A joint statement released by representatives of Wet&amp;rsquo ; suwet&amp;rsquo ; en Nation , the province and the federal government acknowledged that they had not come to an agreement on the pipeline . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Shortly after the proposal was announced , Coastal GasLink issued a statement saying it would resume construction activities in the Morice River area , which is near the Unist&amp;rsquo ; ot&amp;rsquo ; en Healing Centre , on Monday . It also said the company is committed to talking with all Indigenous groups along its route . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The company had agreed to pause construction during the talks between the hereditary chiefs and the ministers , which began Thursday . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; RCMP had also pledged to cease patrols along the Morice West Forest Service Road during the discussions . The Mounties did not respond to requests for comment Sunday . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The dispute over the pipeline has spurred solidarity protests across the country that have disrupted passenger and freight train service over the last three weeks . Police have recently moved to dismantle some of the blockades. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The Wet&amp;rsquo ; suwet&amp;rsquo ; en are governed by both a traditional hereditary chief system and elected band councils . A majority of its councils have approved the pipeline , but some of the Wet&amp;rsquo ; suwet&amp;rsquo ; en hereditary chiefs oppose it running through their traditional territory . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Gary Naziel , a Wet&amp;rsquo ; suwet&amp;rsquo ; en hereditary subchief who wants the pipeline built , said he hasn&amp;rsquo ; t seen the proposed arrangement . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; What&amp;rsquo ; s done behind closed doors hasn&amp;rsquo ; t been shared yet , &amp;rdquo ; he said . &amp;ldquo ; But it will be settled in the Feast Hall &amp;mdash ; all this stuff that&amp;rsquo ; s been going on has to come to Feast Hall . We will deal with it there . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Naziel said no date has been set to discuss the proposal , but he noted it&amp;rsquo ; s a good first step and he&amp;rsquo ; s satisfied that the pipeline is going ahead . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; The projects are going forward , so that way we can get the benefits for our education and language , &amp;rdquo ; he said . &amp;ldquo ; If everybody is satisfied , that&amp;rsquo ; s fine by me . Internally we need to fix things in the Wet&amp;rsquo ; suwet&amp;rsquo ; en Nation . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; We already know we had titles and rights to this land . We know we own this land . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The dispute also involves other unsettled land rights and title issues , including who has the right to negotiate with governments and corporations , the fact that the land is not covered by a treaty and remains unceded , and a 1997 court case that recognized the hereditary chiefs&amp;rsquo ; authority and the exclusive right of the Wet&amp;rsquo ; suwet&amp;rsquo ; en peoples to the land but did not specify the boundaries . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Lawyer Peter Grant , who represented the Wet&amp;rsquo ; suwet&amp;rsquo ; en and neighbouring Gitxsan First Nation , said the proposal is not a treaty . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; It&amp;rsquo ; s a draft arrangement , but I think it&amp;rsquo ; s very powerful , &amp;rdquo ; he said . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The secretary of the Mohawk Nation of Kahnawake said activists have decided to maintain their rail blockade on the territory south of Montreal , at least for now . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Kenneth Deer said the Mohawks want more clarification on the agreement between the Wet&amp;rsquo ; suwet&amp;rsquo ; en hereditary chiefs and the federal government before making a final decision . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; It&amp;rsquo ; s a big decision to decide to take down the barricade or not , and they want to make sure they have everything before they make a decision , &amp;rdquo ; he said Sunday outside the entrance to the barricade , where Mohawk flags flew from a tent and pointed wooden shelter protected by low concrete barriers . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Deer , who said he&amp;rsquo ; s been in contact with hereditary chiefs , said good things came out of the meeting in B.C. , as well as &amp;ldquo ; some things that were not so good . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; He said the agreement includes discussions on who are custodians of the land , as well as a recognition of the hereditary chiefs , which he described as &amp;ldquo ; significant . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; However , the pipeline is not resolved , and that&amp;rsquo ; s a very big issue , not only for the Wet&amp;rsquo ; suwet&amp;rsquo ; en chiefs but for everybody , &amp;rdquo ; &lt;li&gt;&lt;a href= " https : **33;15332;TOOLONG ... experts say injunctions not effective in Indigenous-led land **29;15367;TOOLONG &lt;li&gt;&lt;a href= " https : **33;15398;TOOLONG ... very good start ' : Talks between Wet'suwet'en chiefs and federal , provincial ministers to resume **27;15433;TOOLONG &lt;/ul&gt; Hina Alam , The Canadian PressMon , 02 Mar 2020 14:29:02 **45;15462;TOOLONG ... NewsNationalPoliticsIan Mulgrew : Marathon medicare trial finally endshttp : **36;15509;TOOLONG ... &lt;img src= " https : **34;15547;TOOLONG ... title= " Dr. Brian Day in 2013. " alt= " Dr. Brian Day in 2013. " data-portal-copyright= " Jenelle Schneider Jenelle Schnei " **28;15583;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1224981 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; The interminable constitutional trial over the provision of private health care in B.C. &amp;mdash ; dubbed The Flying Dutchman of the B.C. Supreme Court by lawyers &amp;mdash ; finally made it back to harbour Friday . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; In many ways , what should have been an intellectual cruise involving a few months of written argument and data from the medical system became a veritable Royal Commission that has generated a library of evidence . It involved 194 days of proceedings over 3 1/2 years that mocked timely justice and badly bruised the belief that the courts can act as an effective brake on bad government by providing a remedy to unconstitutional law-making. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The length of time and cost of the case are an argument that the courts are no longer capable of efficiently resolving such thorny questions of social policy . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; As intervener lawyer Joseph Arvay said : &amp;ldquo ; &lt;span style= " font-family : -apple-system , BlinkMacSystemFont , ' Segoe UI ' , Roboto , Oxygen-Sans , Ubuntu , Cantarell , ' Helvetica Neue ' , sans-serif ; " &gt;This case would appear to be , at least in my experience , the most complex Charter case I 've ever seen . And one that truly does test the institutional competence of the court . " &lt;/span&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; At a time when more and more Canadians think the country isn&amp;rsquo ; t working , the dysfunctional legal system must be considered a primary reason . Indigenous people have run into the same problem trying to hold governments to account through litigation &amp;mdash ; they too have found themselves bogged down in endless process that pours millions-of-dollars into lawyers&amp;rsquo ; pockets . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The duration brought its own risks and demands on memory &amp;mdash ; the constitutional challenge of two provisions of the Medicare Protection Act was nearly derailed late last year when B.C. Supreme Court Justice John Steeves required health care . This week Steeves couldn&amp;rsquo ; t remember what prevented the government from enforcing the law . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; There is in place a consent order allowing private surgical services to continue , &amp;rdquo ; plaintiffs&amp;rsquo ; lawyer , Robert Grant , explained &amp;mdash ; issued by Justice Janet Winteringham following an injunction she granted in November 2018 after Victoria amended the law and planned enforcement , though its validity was in question . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; Did I sign ? that ( order ) ? &amp;rdquo ; the justice asked . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; No , it carries on from Justice ? Winteringham , &amp;rdquo ; Grant replied . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; So , it has to do with the amendments , as I say , that occurred during my trial , &amp;rdquo ; Steeves said . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;h2 class= " cat-head short " **40;15613;TOOLONG ... &lt;ul class= " relatedlinks " &gt; &lt;li&gt;&lt;a href= " https : **33;15655;TOOLONG ... health care battle in judge 's hands but expected to land in Canada 's top **26;15690;TOOLONG &lt;/ul&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; Exactly , &amp;rdquo ; Grant said . &amp;ldquo ; And what it did is effectively to ? confirm they won&amp;rsquo ; t be employed until your lordship ? ruled , so that allowed the status quo to continue . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; Maybe ( government lawyer Jonathan ) Penner can give the minister my ? compliments for making my job easier , &amp;rdquo ; the justice quipped. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Two private clinics and a handful of patients launched the litigation roughly a decade ago because the constraints on dual practice by doctors and private health insurance would force private clinics and diagnostic centres across the province to close . No evidence or data was offered by either government to support the assertion that the private clinics cause harm to the public system and B.C. has not measured the impact or effect of the clinics that have existed for a generation . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; They would have welcomed an opportunity for an impartial objective empirical study , &amp;rdquo ; Grant said . &amp;ldquo ; It might have made this litigation unnecessary as it would have confirmed that private surgeries did not have any adverse effects on public surgeries . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The Vancouver lawyer accused the government of grossly mischaracterizing and misrepresenting evidence in closing statements he said were little more than fearmongering . He pointed out B.C. has had de facto private health care for 20 years and the sky hasn&amp;rsquo ; t fallen . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; To end that status quo , he added , would make the public health system even more overcrowded as the 65,000 private surgeries done annually join already historically long waiting lists . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; Nobody gets ? ahead in the public queue by having private ? surgery , &amp;rdquo ; Grant explained . &amp;ldquo ; What happens is you leave the queue . ? You&amp;rsquo ; re not jumping the queue , you&amp;rsquo ; re leaving the ? queue . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Instead of relevant data , the federal and provincial governments resorted to fervid rhetoric about the prospect of U.S.-style health care and the poor languishing in dirty beds at the mercy of greedy , unscrupulous physicians . At one point they accused a respected neurosurgeon of having &amp;ldquo ; scaled back public work because he wanted more time to smell the roses and read a book . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; This is an egregious mischaracterization of the evidence , &amp;rdquo ; Grant told Steeves. &amp;ldquo ; In fact ( the doctor ) suffered a family tragedy . His wife developed terminal cancer &amp;mdash ; and he needed to scale back his public on-call commitments as he couldn&amp;rsquo ; t be operating all night due to his family&amp;rsquo ; s needs &amp;hellip ; With his wife&amp;rsquo ; s illness and passing , he could not do this with four children . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Grant swept the broader accusations aside too : &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; If there was one shred of ? evidence that doctors practising ? in the private system , just one piece , one example ? of a doctor performing private pay surgery , ? shirking a commitment to the public system or ? causing any problem at all for the public system , ? we can be certain they would have called that ? evidence , but they didn&amp;rsquo ; t. &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; He urged Steeves to draw an adverse inference from the government&amp;rsquo ; s failure to call a single doctor or senior administrator to give evidence about problems the public system had experienced as a result of private surgeries . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; The evidence in this trial shows thousands of British Columbians wait too long past government-mandated medically maximum acceptable waits for their condition , risking progression of disease and in some cases shortened lifespan or death , &amp;rdquo ; Grant concluded . &amp;ldquo ; In evidence in this trial , is the fact that in one year , in just one health region in B.C. , Fraser Health , 308 patients died waiting for medically necessary surgery . B.C. patients need a &amp;lsquo ; safety valve&amp;rsquo ; . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Before the courtroom emptied , Steeves said : &amp;ldquo ; I&amp;rsquo ; m looking forward to completing my judgment and setting my name on it , and , once I&amp;rsquo ; ve done that , I&amp;rsquo ; ll join the rest of the world watching the progress of this case with great interest . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; He is expected to take several months , perhaps longer , on his ruling . Appeals are expected to follow , which means a final decision could be two , three or more years away . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Outside of court , Dr. Brian Day , the face of the litigation , said he was relieved that the trial was over . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;span class= " s1 " &gt; " Suffering patients -- the more than 30,000 a year who wait past the government 's own maximum acceptable wait times , and the 18-a-week who die on public wait lists in B.C. -- need the justice system to rescue themselves from their plight , " he said . &lt;/span&gt; &lt;span class= " s1 " &gt; " It 's astonishing that we are the only country on earth that outlaws private health insurance . " &lt;/span&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " **29;15718;TOOLONG " target= " blank " rel= " noopener " **36;15749;TOOLONG &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " http : //twitter.com/ianmulgrew " target= " blank " rel= " noopener " **31;15787;TOOLONG ... &lt;/p&gt; Ian MulgrewSat , 29 Feb 2020 01:43:29 **45;15820;TOOLONG ... **29;15867;TOOLONG Todd : Toronto and Vancouver breaking North American records for immigration growthhttp : **34;15898;TOOLONG ... &lt;img src= " https : **34;15934;TOOLONG ... title= " New immigrants to New York City account for only 0.8 per cent of its annual growth . That 's in contrast to Metro Vancouver , which grew by 1.6 per cent in 2019 as a result of immigration . Toronto expanded by almost two per cent from immigration . " alt= " New immigrants to New York City account for only 0.8 per cent of its annual growth . That 's in contrast to Metro Vancouver , which grew by 1.6 per cent in 2019 as a result of immigration . Toronto expanded by almost two per cent from immigration . " data-portal-copyright= " STAN HONDA " **28;15970;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1225500 " /&gt; &lt;iframe width= " 100% " height= " 100% " frameborder= " 0 " allowfullscreen= " true " src= " https : **34;16000;TOOLONG ... &gt; &lt;/iframe&gt; &lt;p&gt; Greater Toronto and Metro Vancouver are growing far faster through immigration than any other North American city . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The two Canadian metropolises are absorbing many more immigrants per capita than the United States&amp;rsquo ; top immigrant-receiving cities , such as New York , Miami , Los Angeles and Washington , D.C. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The rapid growth of &lt;a href= " http : **34;16036;TOOLONG ... target= " blank " rel= " noopener " &gt;Toronto and Vancouver&lt;/a&gt; through immigration comes as the federal Liberals in 2019 welcomed the largest number of new immigrants to the country in more than a century at 341,000. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The concentrated flow into Toronto and Vancouver is occurring as the province of Quebec has reduced its immigration levels by 20 per cent , leading to fewer moving last year to Montreal ( 34,620 ) . The two Canadian cities&amp;rsquo ; immigration numbers also coincide with tightening border rules in the U.S. , which has long allowed in &lt;a href= " http : **34;16072;TOOLONG ... target= " blank " rel= " noopener " &gt;three times fewer immigrants per capita than Canada . &lt;/a&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Greater Toronto and Metro Vancouver , the latter of which experienced a record 13-per-cent rise in new immigrants in 2019 , are proving to be distinctly powerful magnets for foreign-born newcomers , even within their own provinces . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Although it&amp;rsquo ; s often said Canada is a &amp;ldquo ; big country , &amp;rdquo ; most immigrants focus on heading to the country&amp;rsquo ; s biggest cities . Sixteen of 20 newcomers to British Columbia in 2019 headed directly for Metro Vancouver , while one of 20 set up home in the Greater Victoria region . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Vancouver real-estate marketer Bob Rennie and associate Andrew Ramlo told the Independent Contractors and Businesses Association last week that a projected &lt;a href= " http : **34;16108;TOOLONG ... target= " blank " rel= " noopener " &gt; " demographic crunch " &lt;/a&gt; involving immigrants will bring one million more people into Metro Vancouver in the next 20 years . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; That will require building 450,000 new homes &amp;mdash ; the equivalent of &amp;ldquo ; another Vancouver , Burnaby , New West and Coquitlam , &amp;rdquo ; said the real-estate marketers . Other analysts say it&amp;rsquo ; s worth monitoring the impact of such high in-migration on not only local housing , but on transit and the labour market . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;16144;TOOLONG ... title= " The Vancouver skyline . The Independent Contractors and Businesses Association was told last week that a projected ' demographic crunch ' involving immigrants will bring one million more people into Metro Vancouver in the next 20 years . " alt= " The Vancouver skyline . The Independent Contractors and Businesses Association was told last week that a projected ' demographic crunch ' involving immigrants will bring one million more people into Metro Vancouver in the next 20 years . " data-portal-copyright= " Francis Georgian " **28;16180;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1225730 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; Jack Jedwab , president of Metropolis Canada , says Metro Vancouver , despite its relatively small size of 2.6 million , is projected to be the fifth highest immigrant-receiving city in North America in total numbers after only New York , Toronto , Miami and Los Angeles . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; A Postmedia analysis of Jedwab&amp;rsquo ; s data on North American cities revealed that Metro Vancouver and Greater Toronto take in a much higher per capita proportion of immigrants each year than any major U.S. city . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The record number of new immigrants to Greater Toronto in 2019 ( 117,000 ) boosted the city&amp;rsquo ; s population by 1.9 per cent . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The rising immigrant population of Metro Vancouver ( 40,020 ) hiked the West Coast metropolis&amp;rsquo ; s population by 1.6 per cent . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The recent arrival of 168,000 immigrants to New York City in one year makes the Big Apple the top immigrant-receiving city in North America in gross numbers . But the population of the New York census region of 20 million grew through immigration by only a small margin compared to Vancouver and Toronto : 0.8 per cent . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The second-largest immigrant-receiving metropolis in the U.S. , the Miami region of 6.2 million , grew by just 1.2 per cent in a year via 80,000 new immigrants . Los Angeles , with more than 12 million people , grew by a tiny 0.6 per cent through the arrival of 70,032 new immigrants . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The U.S.&amp;rsquo ; s other top immigrant-receiving metropolises &amp;mdash ; Washington , D.C. , Chicago , Houston , the San Francisco area and Dallas/Fort Worth &amp;mdash ; all also expanded by less than 0.6 per cent annually as a result of immigrants . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The speedier rates of immigration growth for Toronto and Vancouver were achieved without taking into account the arrival of temporary foreign-born newcomers to each city , particularly international students , whose overall numbers in 2019 ( 642,000 ) have doubled since 2015. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;16210;TOOLONG ... title= " The Toronto downtown skyline . The record number of new immigrants to Greater Toronto in 2019 ( 117,000 ) boosted the city 's population by 1.9 per cent . " alt= " The Toronto downtown skyline . The record number of new immigrants to Greater Toronto in 2019 ( 117,000 ) boosted the city 's population by 1.9 per cent . " data-portal-copyright= " Ernest Doroszuk " **28;16246;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1225734 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; Greater Toronto and Metro Vancouver took in far more new international students and &lt;a href= " http : **34;16276;TOOLONG ... target= " blank " rel= " noopener " &gt;foreign guest workers&lt;/a&gt; in 2019 than they did immigrants . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; In Metro Vancouver , the volume of &lt;a href= " https : **33;16312;TOOLONG ... target= " blank " rel= " noopener " &gt;non-permanent foreign-born residents&lt;/a&gt; who moved in ( 101,000 ) was more than double the number of immigrants . Yet analysts point out both permanent and non-permanent newcomers have an impact on housing , transit , wages and &lt;a href= " http : **34;16347;TOOLONG ... target= " blank " rel= " noopener " &gt;rental accommodation&lt;/a&gt; . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Another geographical imbalance in immigration patterns reveals itself when figures are broken down by territory and province . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; B.C ( mostly Metro Vancouver ) took in as many immigrants last year as all of Saskatchewan , Manitoba , Nova Scotia , Nunavut , Yukon , Prince Edward Island , Newfoundland and New Brunswick combined . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; New Brunswick officials are lamenting they can not hold onto the meagre 5,000 to 6,000 or so a year who initially move to their Maritime province . A team of researchers this month &lt;a href= " https : **33;16383;TOOLONG ... target= " blank " rel= " noopener " &gt;found 50 per cent of immigrants leave New Brunswick&lt;/a&gt; after five years . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Data on 2019 immigration levels also reveals striking changes in the origins of the 341,000 immigrants who arrived in Canada this year , compared to the 271,000 who came in 2015 , the first year of Justin Trudeau&amp;rsquo ; s government . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Jedwab&amp;rsquo ; s data shows the proportion of immigrants coming to Canada from India has doubled since 2016 , to the point that that giant South Asian country&amp;rsquo ; s citizens now account for one in four of all new immigrants . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;h2 class= " cat-head short " **40;16418;TOOLONG ... &lt;ul class= " relatedlinks " &gt; &lt;li&gt;&lt;a href= " https : **33;16460;TOOLONG ... site graphically shows immigration and foreign-student trends in Vancouver and **28;16495;TOOLONG &lt;li&gt;&lt;a href= " http : **34;16525;TOOLONG ... Todd : A tale of two costly housing markets : Toronto and **30;16561;TOOLONG &lt;li&gt;&lt;a href= " http : **34;16593;TOOLONG ... Todd : Canada versus U.S. on immigration **27;16629;TOOLONG &lt;/ul&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;span style= " font-family : -apple-system , BlinkMacSystemFont , ' Segoe UI ' , Roboto , Oxygen-Sans , Ubuntu , Cantarell , ' Helvetica Neue ' , sans-serif ; " &gt;The number of immigrants from the Philippines has almost dropped in half since 2015 , to 27,815 , with one analyst saying it 's largely because the government has significantly reduced its intake of workers under the caregiver program . &lt;/span&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;span style= " font-family : -apple-system , BlinkMacSystemFont , ' Segoe UI ' , Roboto , Oxygen-Sans , Ubuntu , Cantarell , ' Helvetica Neue ' , sans-serif ; " &gt;Meanwhile , 30,260 came from China last year , compared to 19,460 in 2015 . T&lt;/span&gt; he volume of immigrants who hailed from Nigeria ( 12,595 ) , Eritrea ( 7,025 ) and Brazil ( 5,290 ) has expanded more than three times in five years . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; What categories of immigrants are coming ? Some are highly skilled . Since 2015 there has been a four-fold increase in &amp;ldquo ; software engineers and designers&amp;rdquo ; moving to B.C. ( 1,045 came last year ) , according to data obtained by Jedwab. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; There have also been big jumps in immigrants arriving through semi-skilled and low-skilled immigration categories . In the past four years B.C. , particularly Metro Vancouver , has had increases of more than 300 per cent in &amp;ldquo ; restaurant and food service managers , &amp;rdquo ; &amp;ldquo ; retail managers , &amp;rdquo ; &amp;ldquo ; public relations managers , &amp;rdquo ; &amp;ldquo ; transport truck drivers , &amp;rdquo ; &amp;ldquo ; administrative officers&amp;rdquo ; and &amp;ldquo ; administrative assistants . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The number of Metro Vancouver immigration stories that can be mined from such data is unlimited . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " **26;16658;TOOLONG " target= " blank " rel= " noopener " **33;16686;TOOLONG &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; **25;16721;TOOLONG &lt;a href= " https : **25;16748;TOOLONG " class= " twitter-follow-button " data-show-count= " false " &gt;Follow @DouglasTodd&lt;/a&gt; **25;16775;TOOLONG &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;em&gt;&lt;a href= " **27;16802;TOOLONG " &gt;CLICK HERE&lt;/a&gt; to report a typo. &lt;/em&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;em&gt;Is there more to this story ?
