NewsNationalStaff BlogsWorldIan Mulgrew : Reconciliation in danger of derailment by a minority opposed to rule of lawhttp : **37;16945;TOOLONG ... &lt;img src= " https : **34;16984;TOOLONG ... title= " Police officers make an arrest during a raid on a Tyendinaga Mohawk Territory camp next to a blockaded railway crossing in Tyendinaga , Ont. , on February 24 , 2020. " alt= " Police officers make an arrest during a raid on a Tyendinaga Mohawk Territory camp next to a blockaded railway crossing in Tyendinaga , Ont. , on February 24 , 2020. " data-portal-copyright= " CARLOS OSORIO " **28;17020;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1225196 " /&gt; &lt;iframe width= " 100% " height= " 100% " frameborder= " 0 " allowfullscreen= " true " src= " https : **34;17050;TOOLONG ... &gt; &lt;/iframe&gt; &lt;p&gt; The mob opposing the Coastal GasLink pipeline refuses to acknowledge the complicated legal reality of First Nations reconciliation or Aboriginal rights . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Meanwhile , our politicians look like they don&amp;rsquo ; t have a clue or any confidence in what is being done to settle Indigenous grievances and the cops appear more interested in filing overtime than clearing blockades so the country can function and people get to work . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; In its &lt;a href= " https : **33;17086;TOOLONG ... target= " blank " rel= " noopener " &gt;latest ruling&lt;/a&gt; on Aboriginal rights involving the Innu in Labrador , the Supreme Court of Canada reiterated on Friday its past pronouncements , but few noticed . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The court has made it clear &amp;mdash ; Indigenous sovereignty was extinguished and the federal government calls the shots in Canada , even for Aboriginal people . &amp;nbsp ; Still , the high bench emphasized Section 35 of the Constitution specifically recognizes Aboriginal and treaty rights . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; There are about one million First Nations people across the country ( not counting the 65,000 Inuit or 600,000 M&amp;eacute ; t is ) &amp;mdash ; &amp;nbsp ; and most are covered by a treaty that together with the constitution defines their relationship with governments . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; In B.C. , however , the colonial administration stopped signing treaties , leaving most of B.C.&amp;rsquo ; s roughly 250,000 First Nations today without a deal . &amp;nbsp ; Worse , politicians for most of B.C. history supported Indigenous policies that could have been drafted by the U.S. 7 **25;17121;TOOLONG Cavalry under Custer. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; I think that era ended in 1991 when B.C. Chief Justice Allan McEachern rejected the Gitxsan and Wet&amp;rsquo ; suwet&amp;rsquo ; en Aboriginal claims in a 400-page judgment that was staggering in its tone-deaf condescension. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; McEachern cribbed a Thomas Hobbes quote to describe Indigenous lives as &amp;ldquo ; nasty , brutish , and short . &amp;rdquo ; He then pointed out they didn&amp;rsquo ; t invent the wheel or have horses . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Indigenous people had many reasons to be angry at how they were being treated ; McEachern was a lightning rod . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Within a year , a new NDP government precipitated a sea-change in Victoria . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; It abandoned the long-held anti-Indigenous-rights stance , leading the B.C. Court of Appeal to reject McEachern&amp;rsquo ; s decision and order a new trial . And premier Mike Harcourt launched a process to sign modern treaties . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; In 1997 , the Supreme Court of Canada also &lt;a href= " https : **33;17148;TOOLONG ... target= " blank " rel= " noopener " &gt;tossed McEachern 's insulting ruling&lt;/a&gt; &amp;nbsp ; and sent the Gitxsan and Wet&amp;rsquo ; suwet&amp;rsquo ; en case back for a retrial . A generation later , that retrial has not taken place . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Instead , the Gitxsan and Wet&amp;rsquo ; suwet&amp;rsquo ; en bought into the promise of the nascent reconciliation movement . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Unfortunately , the &lt;a href= " http : //www.bctreaty.ca/ " target= " blank " rel= " noopener " &gt;B.C. treaty-making process&lt;/a&gt; has not been as successful as hoped &amp;mdash ; &amp;nbsp ; some of the province&amp;rsquo ; s 200 bands refused to participate or angrily withdrew and there have been too few agreements given the hundreds of millions in tax money incinerated . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The lack of progress has been frustrating because of the cost and years it takes to reach a pact or get a legal decision involving Indigenous rights . The discontent is exacerbated when most rulings are hard to fathom . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The Supreme Court of Canada says Aboriginal title isn&amp;rsquo ; t the same as owning property ; Aboriginal title belongs to a group , not an individual . &amp;nbsp ; It&amp;rsquo ; s also for the benefit of future generations , not just the current one . So it can&amp;rsquo ; t simply be sold and individuals can&amp;rsquo ; t make decisions about it , except maybe in circumstances the court hasn&amp;rsquo ; t envisioned yet . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; In other words : &amp;ldquo ; It&amp;rsquo ; s complicated ! &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Yet the treaty-making continues and both sides are still pursuing solutions outside the courtroom so the politicians and Indigenous people shape the future , not some judge . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; As everyone knew it would be , the process of negotiating reconciliation has been fraught with difficulty , failure and false starts , especially in B.C. given its despicable record of First Nations relations and the lack of treaties . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The issues we confront are not new &amp;mdash ; they were before the Supreme Court in 2013 , in a B.C. logging dispute . &lt;span style= " font-family : -apple-system , BlinkMacSystemFont , ' Segoe UI ' , Roboto , Oxygen-Sans , Ubuntu , Cantarell , ' Helvetica Neue ' , sans-serif ; " &gt;In that unanimous decision , the high bench said Indigenous individuals could not exercise " community " rights by blocking a road without authorization from their band . &lt;/span&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; ( R ) ights may sometimes be assigned to or exercised by individual members of Aboriginal communities , and entitlements may sometimes be created in their favour , &amp;rdquo ; Justice Louis LeBel wrote for the court . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; To legitimize civil disobedience , he wrote , would be &amp;ldquo ; tantamount to condoning self-help remedies and would bring the administration of justice into disrepute . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; It may well be , &amp;rdquo ; LeBel added , &amp;ldquo ; that , in appropriate circumstances , individual members can assert certain Aboriginal or treaty rights , as some of the interveners have proposed . But I would not , on the occasion of this appeal and at this stage of the development of the law , try to develop broad categories for these rights and to slot each right in the appropriate one . This is an important issue , but a definitive pronouncement in this regard can not be made in the circumstances of this case . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; That&amp;rsquo ; s why it&amp;rsquo ; s complicated . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; LeBel believed that some community members might &amp;ldquo ; have a greater interest in the protection of the rights on their traditional family territory than do other members of the First Nation , and that this connection gives them a certain standing to raise the violation of their particular rights . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Some might argue that opens the door for , say , a handful of hereditary chiefs to assert their clan interests to block a pipeline . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Perhaps , but all the judges insisted that if Indigenous people believed their rights were being breached , they should ask for a judicial review rather than mounting barricades . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; In the 8-0 , landmark 2014 &lt;a href= " https : **33;17183;TOOLONG ... target= " blank " rel= " noopener " &gt;Tsilquot'in judgment , &lt;/a&gt; now-retired chief justice Beverley McLachlin confirmed that First Nations had rights , though government could still expropriate or place easements on Indigenous land . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The Tsilquot&amp;rsquo ; in did not win their claim lock , stock and barrel , but rather a much smaller area of about 1,700 square kilometres &amp;mdash ; less than the size of Metro Vancouver . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Indigenous control of the land , insofar as they can benefit from it , the court explained , was constrained by the community nature of their rights and the need to look after the interest of future generations , which again was subjected to judicial review given the fiduciary obligations involved . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; As long as the government negotiated in good faith , carried out meaningful consultation and , presumably , was willing to cut a reasonable cheque &amp;mdash ; a mine , pipeline or industrial development could proceed . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Most importantly , although tortoise-like and expensive , the Tsilquot&amp;rsquo ; in decision indicated the process could work . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; McLachlin called it the new &amp;ldquo ; governing ethos &amp;hellip ; of reconciliation . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Over the last few weeks , the protesters have mocked that hope and the authenticity of the country&amp;rsquo ; s difficult but progressing reconciliation efforts . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The majority of the Wet&amp;rsquo ; suwet&amp;rsquo ; en say they back the GasLink pipeline , are satisfied with the consultations and happy with the outcomes . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Yet their rights are being ignored , ordinary commuters are having their lives disrupted , court injunctions are being flouted and the economy bruised . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; These blockades are not just an inconvenience &amp;mdash ; they are an insidious attempt to derail reconciliation by a minority opposed to the rule of law . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " **29;17218;TOOLONG " target= " blank " rel= " noopener " **36;17249;TOOLONG &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " http : //twitter.com/ianmulgrew " target= " blank " rel= " noopener " **31;17287;TOOLONG ... &lt;/p&gt; Ian MulgrewWed , 26 Feb 2020 21:00:44 **45;17320;TOOLONG ... Bramham : It 's not enough to just keep overdose victims alivehttp : **35;17367;TOOLONG ... &lt;img src= " https : **34;17404;TOOLONG ... title= " Paramedics transport a woman suspected of an overdose. " alt= " Paramedics transport a woman suspected of an overdose. " data-portal-copyright= " Dan Janisse " **28;17440;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1224488 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; There was some good news in the 2019 data from the B.C. Coroners Service . Overdose deaths in the province declined for the first time since fentanyl-tainted drugs hit the streets and a public health emergency was declared in 2016. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The decrease was significant &amp;mdash ; down 36 per cent from 2018 &amp;mdash ; even though the death toll remains heartbreakingly high . As B.C. enters its fifth year of the crisis , nearly three British Columbians are dying every day . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; It does mean that all of the money poured into this crisis &amp;mdash ; for naloxone kits , the training for paramedics , medical professionals and laypeople in how to use naloxone , more supervised consumption sites , and more people now on prescriptions for drugs like methadone and Suboxone to staunch addicts&amp;rsquo ; opioid cravings &amp;mdash ; is keeping more people alive . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; But that&amp;rsquo ; s really where the good news ends . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Alarmingly , the number of 911 calls has continued to climb . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Paramedics and other first responders took more than 24,000 calls last year , with calls spiking to more than 130 overdose alerts on &amp;ldquo ; cheque days&amp;rdquo ; or &amp;ldquo ; welfare Wednesdays. &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Being revived from an overdose or living with an opioid addiction comes at a high cost . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Opioids affect the receptors in the brain , causing breathing to become dangerously slow , which in turn slows the heart and sometimes causing cardiac arrest . When the hearts doesn&amp;rsquo ; t pump at capacity , less oxygenated blood makes it to the brain . Without oxygen , brain cells die &amp;mdash ; and they don&amp;rsquo ; t regenerate. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; It&amp;rsquo ; s called toxic brain injury . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Within the coming weeks or months , the B.C. Centre for Disease Control will release data on the prevalence of brain injury among opioid users , including those who have been successfully restored to life with naloxone. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; We know that many hundreds of people will need a lifetime of care , &amp;rdquo ; said Dr. Perry Kendall , who raised the alarm during the coroner&amp;rsquo ; s news conference earlier this week . &amp;ldquo ; It will be a tremendous burden . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; It&amp;rsquo ; s far from the only one . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The burden carried by first responders is different and no less costly . They are burning out and checking out of the system , unable to cope physically , mentally or emotionally with the constant stress of being called to deal with all the overdoses. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;h2 class= " cat-head short " **40;17470;TOOLONG ... &lt;ul class= " relatedlinks " &gt; &lt;li&gt;&lt;a href= " https : **33;17512;TOOLONG ... overdose deaths decrease , but officials say safer supply **27;17547;TOOLONG &lt;/ul&gt; &lt;p&gt; This is not to say that harm-reduction measures aren&amp;rsquo ; t working . No one disputes that they are keeping many people alive . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; But until now , little attention has been focused on the quality of their lives , post-overdose. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Five years into the public health emergency , Chief Coroner Lisa Lapointe said B.C. still doesn&amp;rsquo ; t have a comprehensive system that includes prevention , treatment and recovery . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The lack of a seamless system is particularly problematic and even deadly for people in rural areas and those coming out of jails and prisons , according to Dr. Nel Wieman , senior medical officer at the First Nations Health Authority . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The numbers back that up . The death rate in the Northern Health Authority , at 22.5 per 100,000 , trails Vancouver Coastal , which has the highest rate , by a mere half a percentage point . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Regardless of where they live , Lapointe said families frequently tell coroners how their loved ones managed through detox only to come out and die while on the waiting list for a recovery bed . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The problem isn&amp;rsquo ; t necessarily that there aren&amp;rsquo ; t enough treatment beds . On most days , some lie empty because the government only funds treatment for welfare recipients . Everyone else has to pay their own way . And except for those with generous employee benefits , many can&amp;rsquo ; t afford treatment that comes at a cost of $900-plus a day . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Lapointe also decried the lack of provincial treatment standards . Different operators have different approaches . Some aren&amp;rsquo ; t evidence-based . Some are strictly abstinence-based and refuse to accept people on drug therapies such as methadone and Suboxone , even though without that , they are more vulnerable to overdose if they relapse. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Decriminalization is touted by some as the answer . Without fear of criminal charges , the theory is that people would be more willing to seek help . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; They point to Portugal , where decriminalization was brought in as part of a massive overhaul of its drug treatment system . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; But decriminalization has only worked there because Portugal also boosted spending on the other three pillars &amp;mdash ; prevention , enforcement and treatment . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Here , the crucial elements are missing . With a minority government in Ottawa , the Liberals already have enough problems on their plate to risk raising the controversial idea of decriminalization. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Meanwhile , most provinces , including B.C. , haven&amp;rsquo ; t invested enough in the infrastructure to put a Portugal-style model in place . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; This week , Mental Health and Addictions Minister Judy Darcy agreed that there are enormous gaps in B.C.&amp;rsquo ; s fragmented system . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; When the New Democrats were elected less than three years ago , she said the drug treatment system had been neglected for so long that it was not able to cope with regular tasks , let alone a public health emergency . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The government is taking steps to fix that . But whether it&amp;rsquo ; s moving fast enough is a conversation that both the coroner and chief medical health officer are pushing British Columbians to have because the lives of many loved ones depend on it . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " **29;17576;TOOLONG " **36;17607;TOOLONG &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Twitter : @bramhamdaphne &lt;/p&gt; Daphne BramhamWed , 26 Feb 2020 00:12:09 **45;17645;TOOLONG ... &amp;amp ; ChildHealthLocal HealthLocal **42;17692;TOOLONG Mulgrew : Home is more than where the heart ishttp : **38;17736;TOOLONG ... &lt;img src= " https : **34;17776;TOOLONG ... title= " " alt= " " data-portal-copyright= " PhotoMelon/Getty Images/iStockphoto " **28;17812;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1221941 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; Home may be where the heart is , but ask a lawyer and you&amp;rsquo ; ll get a complicated answer and a six-figure bill . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Still , a B.C. Court of Appeal registrar , Tim Outerbridge , slashed Helen Sato&amp;rsquo ; s legal bill by 30 per cent &amp;mdash ; rolling back her legal costs to about $89,000 from $135,000 in a dispute about where her late brother Hiroyuki considered home . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; In &lt;a href= " https : **33;17842;TOOLONG ... target= " blank " rel= " noopener " &gt;his assessment&lt;/a&gt; of the invoices , Outerbridge said Helen Sato was overcharged in the appeal of a five-day inheritance trial that turned only on the question of where her brother Hiroyuki Sato was &amp;ldquo ; domiciled&amp;rdquo ; when he died . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; In making this reduction , I recognize that this was an appeal of significant importance and accept that it was appropriate for the appellant to deploy significant resources given its high-stakes nature , &amp;rdquo ; Outerbridge wrote . &amp;nbsp ; &amp;ldquo ; However , at its core , this appeal had a narrow focus on the facts and law , a modest record , and very little procedural difficulty . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; At one point , though , he said six lawyers worked a total of 225.6 hours at an effective hourly rate of $450 an hour on the issues &amp;mdash ; and Helen Sato lost . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Although her brother Hiroyuki lived with his wife , worked and had friends in Luxembourg , and hadn&amp;rsquo ; t lived in Canada since 1999 , the courts concluded he was &amp;ldquo ; domiciled&amp;rdquo ; in B.C. and his sisters and their children were disinherited. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; This meant his estate of about $2 million went to his Japanese wife , Makiko. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The case drew legal attention because the applicable provincial law at the time held that Sato&amp;rsquo ; s 2013 marriage revoked his 2011 will . Under Luxembourg law , the will would have remained valid . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; B.C. law has since been changed and , after the &lt;i&gt;Wills , Estates and Succession Act &lt;/i&gt; came into effect on March 31 , 2014 , a marriage no longer revokes a will . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Hiroyuki &amp;ldquo ; Rex&amp;rdquo ; Sato , who immigrated from Japan in 1969 , was a global banker whose career saw him working and living in Toronto , Vancouver , the Cayman Islands , Tokyo and Guernsey . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; He moved to Luxembourg in 2009 and , for income tax purposes , the Canada Revenue Agency had considered Sato a non-resident since 1999. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; During a visit to family in B.C. in May 2011 , he wrote a will leaving the bulk of his estate to his two sisters , naming Helen as executrix , and their children . He apparently never returned to the province . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; In April 2013 , he married Makiko in Luxembourg , where he kept his personal effects , filed taxes and had inquired into whether he would be eligible for an old age pension . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; But in 2014 he was diagnosed with cancer . Sato died in Japan in 2015 while receiving medical treatment . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Still , B.C. Supreme Court Justice Gordon Funt concluded Sato was &amp;ldquo ; domiciled&amp;rdquo ; in B.C. at the time of his marriage because he asserted to the CRA when he left the country that he would return and intended to retire here . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Funt was bound by &lt;a href= " https : **33;17877;TOOLONG ... target= " blank " rel= " noopener " &gt;a 1959 case&lt;/a&gt; in which the Supreme Court of Canada said a person&amp;rsquo ; s &amp;ldquo ; domicile&amp;rdquo ; was determined by a two-pronged test : Did Sato reside in Luxembourg and did he intend to make it his permanent home indefinitely ? &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; While Sato lived in Luxembourg , Funt said he did not intend to make it his permanent home . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; He called the &amp;ldquo ; Key Document&amp;rdquo ; a CRA **27;17912;TOOLONG form filed by Sato when he moved to the Caymans in which he stated he planned to retire in Canada . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Helen appealed , saying Funt interpreted and applied the high bench&amp;rsquo ; s test too narrowly and &amp;ldquo ; in a manner incompatible with fairness , public policy , and modern conceptions of a person&amp;rsquo ; s intention to settle . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; But the &lt;a href= " https : **33;17941;TOOLONG ... target= " blank " rel= " noopener " &gt;Court of Appeal agreed&lt;/a&gt; with Funt &amp;mdash ; the will was invalid under the old provincial law . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; ( T ) he deceased was not particularly fluent in French , which was the principal language in Luxembourg . He also found that the fact that the plaintiff was Japanese , and not from Luxembourg , militated against finding that the deceased intended to retire there . &amp;hellip ; There was no written document reflecting an intention to change his previously expressed intention to retire in Canada . &amp;hellip ; While the deceased had made several statements in the past about a desire to retire in Japan , those did not support the proposition that the deceased intended to reside in Luxembourg permanently . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The appeal was heard in a day and involved limited factual disputes , no transcript evidence , and no interlocutory applications , Outerbridge noted in his ruling . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; ( Mark ) Weintraub , Q.C . ( of Clark Wilson LLP ) had primary carriage of this appeal and is experienced in the field of estates and trusts , having been called to the bar in 1983 , &amp;rdquo ; the registrar added . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; He was not counsel at trial and worked with three other partners on this file , ranging in years of call from 1997 to 2010 and hourly rates from $385 to $575 . He employed one associate , called to the bar in 2017 , with a rate of between $240 and $295 . A sixth outside counsel argued the appeal , called to the bar in 1986 , who billed 28.3 hours of time for preparation and attendance at a rate of $550 per hour , eventually reducing his invoice to $15,000 , exclusive of taxes . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; I accept Makiko Sato&amp;rsquo ; s argument that the use of six counsel resulted in some duplication of effort . &amp;hellip ; While I agree it may have been reasonable to involve additional counsel for some aspects of the appeal , particularly to provide an impartial view of the issues , I am unable to agree that the level of responsibility justifies the full amount of time billed . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The Court of Appeal ordered costs borne by Sato&amp;rsquo ; s estate , blaming the litigation on his failure to clarify his will after being diagnosed with cancer . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " **29;17976;TOOLONG " target= " blank " rel= " noopener " **36;18007;TOOLONG &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " http : //twitter.com/ianmulgrew " target= " blank " rel= " noopener " **31;18045;TOOLONG ... &lt;/p&gt; Ian MulgrewFri , 21 Feb 2020 23:17:41 **45;18078;TOOLONG ... NewsNationalAgainst the odds , a Canadian woman is granted asylum in the U.S. under the UN Convention Against Torturehttp : **33;18125;TOOLONG ... &lt;img src= " https : **34;18160;TOOLONG ... title= " Her abuser pursued her for years , frequently attacking her . Canadian police did little . In the end , she fled the country " alt= " Her abuser pursued her for years , frequently attacking her . Canadian police did little . In the end , she fled the country " data-portal-copyright= " Brittany Greeson " **28;18196;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1221052 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;WARNING : &lt;/strong&gt; &amp;nbsp ; &lt;em&gt;This story contains graphic accounts of sexual violence that may be disturbing to some readers . &lt;/em&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;hr&gt; &lt;p&gt; For 17 years , she criss-crossed Canada trying to flee the man who raped and tortured her and coerced her into prostitution and sexual slavery . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Regardless of what she did or where she went , her tormentor and the gang that he ran with were never far behind . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; When she became pregnant with his child , he beat her badly enough that police were called . No charges were laid . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Over the years , he was twice arrested and convicted , but never jailed for assault or for breaching no-contact orders as part of his probation . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; He spray-painted one of her homes with racist epithets , torched another and dropped off an eviscerated rat at yet another . Police were called . No charges were laid . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; It all ended 12 years ago when , against incredible odds , the United States granted her asylum under the United Nations Convention Against Torture . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; She didn&amp;rsquo ; t have a lawyer , only two law students who with a professor&amp;rsquo ; s help took it on as a class project . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; U.S. Immigration Judge Kenneth Josephson relied on court precedents in concluding that domestic violence constitutes persecution , noting , &amp;ldquo ; If the government is unable or unwilling to control persecution , it matters not who inflicts it . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; There was no meaningful assistance provided to her , &amp;rdquo ; he said , according to a transcription of his oral decision . He noted that she had made more than 30 attempts to get help from police and spent time in more than a dozen different transition houses across Canada and the United States . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; Obviously , Canada is a democratic , first-world country , &amp;rdquo ; Josephson said . &amp;ldquo ; While it is rare for a citizen of Canada to seek asylum , it is not rare to have claims presented on the basis of domestic violence . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The judge also leaned heavily on Lisa Rupert&amp;rsquo ; s affidavit describing how women are treated by Canadian police and courts . Rupert is the YWCA&amp;rsquo ; s vice-president of housing services and violence protection in Vancouver . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Between 2003 and 2016 , only 79 of 276 Canadian applicants &lt;a href= " https : **33;18226;TOOLONG ... target= " blank " rel= " noopener " &gt;were given asylum&lt;/a&gt; , according to the U.S. Justice Department . Because the reasons for decisions aren&amp;rsquo ; t tracked , a spokesman said it&amp;rsquo ; s not known how many were escaping domestic violence or gangs . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Because Rachel is still deemed by Canadian police to be at high risk , Rachel is a pseudonym . For her protection , other identifying details have been deliberately omitted or altered from the mountains of documents that she has meticulously saved over the years . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; At our first meeting , Rachel insisted on one thing : &amp;ldquo ; This story is not about him ( the perpetrator ) or the people he is involved with . &amp;hellip ; They get enough publicity for being the creepy people who they are . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The story , she said , is about the failure of the Canadian police and courts to protect her and others like her . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Rachel is furious with Canada . She bitterly points to the country&amp;rsquo ; s boast that it is a world leader when it comes to women&amp;rsquo ; s rights . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; Between the RCMP and the court system , they dropped the ball and slid me down a million crevices , and then they did everything they could to cover it up . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Now in her mid-50s , she lives at the edge of poverty in subsidized housing , scraping by on part-time and temporary work to supplement a $212-a-month disability pension from the U.S. government . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; I want the Canadian government to acknowledge what happened and repair as much of the damage as they can , &amp;rdquo ; she said during one of many conversations over the past six months . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Rachel has paid dearly for her safety . It&amp;rsquo ; s cost her everything she&amp;rsquo ; s ever had and nearly everyone she&amp;rsquo ; s known and loved . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; She can never return to Canada . If she were to come even for a visit , she might be denied re-entry to the U.S. because the reason she was given asylum is that she&amp;rsquo ; s at risk if she returns home . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Rachel has had to reinvent herself in a place where no one knew her or why she was there . She&amp;rsquo ; s had to do it without any credentials , because her hard-earned college certificates are in her old name , and without job references because contacting Canadian employers risks having her new identity exposed . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; She&amp;rsquo ; s struggled with the effects of the trauma and abuse she&amp;rsquo ; s endured , as well as guilt over the pain her life has caused her children . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Exile has also alienated her from Canada&amp;rsquo ; s safety net , including health care , social assistance and the Canada Pension Plan . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; That&amp;rsquo ; s in addition to what she lost earlier when fear forced her to give up permanent custody of one of her children , cut off contact with her elderly parents , abruptly leave jobs and sell the family home she inherited from her parents in order to finance her fugitive life . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; After 12 years in hiding , Rachel yearns for home . Canada Day , Canadian Thanksgiving and even Boxing Day trigger memories of happier times and thoughts about what might have been . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; When she contemplated visiting Canada earlier this year , Canadian police advised her that she would be at high risk even if she only came for a few days . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The United States is the only place on the continent where she is safe . The man who hunted and abused her can&amp;rsquo ; t cross the border because of his criminal convictions . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; But even now , she&amp;rsquo ; s extremely cautious , fearing he&amp;rsquo ; ll find her again . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Meantime , her abuser has carried on . He&amp;rsquo ; s served jail time for forcibly entering a home and assaulting another woman . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;h3&gt;FATEFUL FIRST MEETING&lt;/h3&gt; &lt;p&gt; Nearly 30 years ago , the vivacious , single , 20-something mom was singing with a band in a bar and attracted the unwanted attention of a guy who was never going to take no for an answer . It changed her world forever . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; After she rebuffed him at the bar , he surreptitiously followed her home that night . The stalking had begun . He&amp;rsquo ; d turn up at odd places . When she refused to go to his house for a barbecue with her child , he called repeatedly until she finally relented . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; She thought that might be the end of it . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; It was only the beginning . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; His home was a grow-op . When she realized that , she grabbed her child and fled . He grabbed a rifle and fired a shot at her . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The phone was already ringing when she walked in the door of her home . She knew too much , he said . If she made trouble , his gang would kill her and her family . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Rachel changed her phone number , moved and quit her job . But a few weeks later , he was standing over her in her bedroom with a knife . He raped her repeatedly , pressing a pillow into her face to muffle her screams so she wouldn&amp;rsquo ; t wake her child . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; It went on for three days before he agreed that the child should be allowed to go stay with her father . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Over the next few weeks whenever she left the room , he went with her , carrying the switchblade knife . He began inviting some of his friends over . The more compliant she was , the more freedom he gave her . She began plotting her escape to a friend&amp;rsquo ; s house in another community . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; But he found out , took her car keys and her money and assaulted her . A few weeks later , he coerced her into taking him with her and the violence escalated . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; He punished minor slights by locking her in the basement . In her U.S. immigration affidavit , Rachel wrote that he started humming the music from Psycho. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The RCMP report from one of the assaults that sent Rachel to hospital includes her statement describing how he wrapped a sheet around her neck and choked her before he lunged at her with a large knife . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; She was thrown against a wall , thrown to the ground and kicked , according to the RCMP victim assistance supplementary report . He kept repeating that he was going to kill her . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; When police interviewed Rachel about the assault , they didn&amp;rsquo ; t want to hear about anything that had gone before that , she told the immigration judge . They refused to listen when she tried to tell them about how he&amp;rsquo ; d coerced her into living with him , tortured and beaten her before . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Instead , they were the first of many to describe him as her boyfriend and suggest the violence was the result of her bad choices . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Although he was arrested , they didn&amp;rsquo ; t detain him . They escorted him out of town as if it were all part of a Wild West movie . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; It was no movie . A few days later , Rachel was released from hospital . As she was scrambling to pack the car and leave , he came out from behind the garage , grinning . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; Where are we going now ? &amp;rdquo ; he asked . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; A month later and in another town , he beat her until she was unconscious . Once again , police weren&amp;rsquo ; t interested in what had happened before , only what had happened that night . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; He was charged with aggravated assault , but he later pleaded guilty to assault and was sentenced to nine months of probation and ordered to attend anger-management classes . There was no restraining order . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; That night , he found her and raped her . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;18261;TOOLONG ... title= " As part of a relentless campaign to control her , Rachel 's tormentor coerced her into prostitution and made her audition for a porn film . His abuse was only beginning . " alt= " As part of a relentless campaign to control her , Rachel 's tormentor coerced her into prostitution and made her audition for a porn film . His abuse was only beginning . " data-portal-copyright= " CARL DE SOUZA " **28;18297;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1221409 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; Within that first year , he coerced her into prostitution and made her audition for a porn film . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; He also got her pregnant . When she refused to have an abortion , he assaulted her . Police came , but no charges were laid . A month after the child was born , he breached the order , robbed and assaulted her , burning her with a cigarette and punching her in the jaw . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; Strongly recommend that the accused be released only if a restraining order is put into effect , &amp;rdquo ; the attending officer wrote . &amp;ldquo ; No contact direct or indirect as accused harassing victim by repeated phone calls . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Also in the report is the accused&amp;rsquo ; s comment : &amp;ldquo ; She&amp;rsquo ; ll pay for this . She will know how this feels . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Why police responded as they did , why he was never jailed for breaching no-contact orders and why he was never jailed at all are all questions that haunt Rachel and remain unanswered . Police don&amp;rsquo ; t comment on individual cases and , aside from their decisions , judges don&amp;rsquo ; t comment at all . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; For 17 years , Rachel describes her life as a cat-and-mouse chase . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; I thought he&amp;rsquo ; d eventually give up and move on . I didn&amp;rsquo ; t think it would be a 17-year problem or that I would eventually have to leave the country , &amp;rdquo ; she told me. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; I kept thinking , &amp;lsquo ; Now , the police will do something . Now , it&amp;rsquo ; s going to stop . &amp;rsquo ; &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; But the timeline chronicling her torment runs to eight pages . He&amp;rsquo ; d breach the orders . She&amp;rsquo ; d escape to a shelter and he&amp;rsquo ; d find her . He&amp;rsquo ; d beat her ; police would be called . Only twice were restraining orders issued . He was never sent to jail . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; When he couldn&amp;rsquo ; t catch and assault her , he&amp;rsquo ; d vandalize her home or threaten her employers . When he couldn&amp;rsquo ; t find her , he&amp;rsquo ; d threaten her parents . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; One summer , she and her child lived off the grid in a tent bought at Zellers . When the $300 that she&amp;rsquo ; d hidden from him ran out , she begged a telephone operator to find the number for a women&amp;rsquo ; s shelter and put her through . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Less than a month later , her relentless and well-connected abuser found them there . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Another time and in a different shelter , a gang-connected woman wheedled her way in to deliver the message that he was watching . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Rachel relinquished permanent custody of her child from a previous relationship as a protection from the violence that permeated her life . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Later , exhausted from the threats and running , Rachel asked the child protection ministry to take the child that she&amp;rsquo ; d had with her abuser into temporary care on the condition that the child&amp;rsquo ; s father not be contacted . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; But a social worker broke that agreement and contacted Rachel&amp;rsquo ; s abuser even though his name is not on the child&amp;rsquo ; s birth certificate . Because of that breach of privacy , Rachel very nearly ended up having to share custody with the man who was making her life hell . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Not only would it have meant regular contact with him , Rachel could never have got asylum in the U.S. With a custody order in place , she could have been charged with abduction if she had taken the child out of the country without his permission . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Instead , his custody attempt was the impetus for her exile . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;h3&gt;REPEATED PLEAS FOR HELP&lt;/h3&gt; &lt;p&gt; Over the years , Rachel has approached the Canadian government for help . She&amp;rsquo ; s kept every email and letter , along with names and phone numbers of the various officials she&amp;rsquo ; s spoken to . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Initially , she asked for compensation for the house she was forced to sell at below market price in 1997 to finance her fugitive life . When it sold again recently , it was for $1.4 million . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Last fall , she tried to get help accessing disability benefits under the Canada Pension Plan , which she paid into from the time she started a part-time job as a high-school student . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; To get benefits , she needs a birth certificate and social insurance number . Rachel believes it&amp;rsquo ; s too risky to apply for CPP under her old name , so she needs new documents . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; After a flurry of email exchanges and phone calls , nothing has happened , just as nothing happened in the 1990s when Rachel begged police to give her a new identity . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Among the problems is Canada&amp;rsquo ; s disjointed system , name changes and birth certificates are provincial . Social insurance numbers and CPP are federal . Each requires a separate application . Each application costs money that Rachel can ill afford . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; But even before she can apply , Rachel would have to apply to be allowed to apply from outside Canada . That&amp;rsquo ; s a whole other process . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; For nearly 30 years , Rachel has been told there&amp;rsquo ; s another problem with getting her name changed in Canada . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; When she was in her late teens , Rachel defrauded a telephone company of $2,000 worth of telephone service by using a fake name . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; It was kid stuff , poor-people stuff , &amp;rdquo ; she said . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; She pleaded guilty and was sentenced to three years&amp;rsquo ; probation with 200 hours of community service and the requirement that she repay the money . She tried , but couldn&amp;rsquo ; t manage to do all of that . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Even with the support of her probation officer , the judge refused to amend the probation order and clear the way for a later pardon , or what&amp;rsquo ; s now called a record suspension . Because of her record , her only safe choice was an extreme one . Flight . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The United States gave her a waiver before granting her asylum and a new identity . Why shouldn&amp;rsquo ; t Canada do that for her now ? &lt;/p&gt; &lt;h3 style= " text-align:left ; " &gt;STILL AT RISK&lt;/h3&gt; &lt;p&gt; Rachel has lived in fear for half her life . She still struggles to accept that for as long as her assailant is alive , her life is at risk . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Violence , threats and coercion forced her into hiding , into exile and into poverty that affected not only her but her children . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Unable to return to her country of birth , she missed major milestones in her children&amp;rsquo ; s lives . She is unable to visit her parents&amp;rsquo ; graves . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; But among the facts of her life that Rachel finds most galling is that her punishment for defrauding a phone company of $2,000 was three times as long as any sentence her assailant ever received for nearly killing her . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; It&amp;rsquo ; s cruel and absurd . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " **29;18327;TOOLONG " **36;18358;TOOLONG &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " http : **27;18396;TOOLONG " target= " blank " rel= " noopener " **29;18425;TOOLONG &; ... &lt;/p&gt; &lt;hr&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;em&gt;&lt;a href= " **27;18456;TOOLONG " &gt;CLICK HERE&lt;/a&gt; to report a typo. &lt;/em&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;em&gt;Is there more to this story ? We 'd like to hear from you about this or any other stories you think we should know about . Email &lt;a href= " **28;18485;TOOLONG " **35;18515;TOOLONG &lt;/em&gt; &lt;/p&gt; Daphne BramhamFri , 21 Feb 2020 14:00:04 **45;18552;TOOLONG ... NewsNationalWomenDaphne Bramham : After decades of promises , has Canada finally reached a tipping point ? http : **34;18599;TOOLONG ... &lt;img src= " https : **34;18635;TOOLONG ... title= " Supporters gather in Victoria to support Wet'suwet'en chiefs who oppose the Coastal GasLink pipeline . " alt= " Supporters gather in Victoria to support Wet'suwet'en chiefs who oppose the Coastal GasLink pipeline . " data-portal-copyright= " KEVIN LIGHT " **28;18671;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1219348 " /&gt; &lt;iframe width= " 100% " height= " 100% " frameborder= " 0 " allowfullscreen= " true " src= " https : **34;18701;TOOLONG ... &gt; &lt;/iframe&gt; &lt;p&gt; Countless words have been written in voluminous reports with hundreds of recommendations regarding the promise that Canada&amp;rsquo ; s relationship with Indigenous people will be transformed . Often years in the making and costing millions of dollars , the reports are encyclopedic , in both the good and the dull sense . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Warmly welcomed by politicians , the full reports with their many volumes are rarely read by them or by anyone else , including journalists and experts offering sage commentary . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The excuses are easy : The reports are too long . We are too busy to read more than the executive summaries . And how much easier it is to reduce it all to a simple debate over whether using the word genocide is appropriate to describe the murders and disappearances of so many Aboriginal women . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; For every recommendation , promises are made to give due consideration . But after public attention has inevitably and quickly pivoted to the latest Instagram-able issues , it has been easy for politicians to decide the recommendations are too costly in dollar terms , political capital , or both . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Canada has wasted so many opportunities . Why is anyone surprised by the disruption to national rail service , or by protests that have kept legislators and others from their work ? &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The only surprise is that the tipping point finally forcing a resolution to generations of talks seems to be the Wet&amp;rsquo ; suwet&amp;rsquo ; en hereditary chiefs&amp;rsquo ; refusal to allow a natural gas pipeline to run through their traditional territory . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Every little thing that makes a difference &amp;mdash ; to paraphrase the subtitle of Malcolm Gladwell&amp;rsquo ; s book The Tipping Point . He argues that there are three essential requirements to tip the balance and force change : &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; There must be strong leaders with influential and charismatic friends , which is a good description of the Wet&amp;rsquo ; suwet&amp;rsquo ; en chiefs and climate activist Greta Thunberg , who has accused Canada of violating children&amp;rsquo ; s rights because of its continued support for oil and gas development . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; There has to be a &amp;ldquo ; stickiness factor&amp;rdquo ; that compels an idea or phenomenon to stick in people&amp;rsquo ; s minds and influence their future behaviour . That might , in this case , include small things such as the now-ubiquitous acknowledgements at every gathering of whose unceded territory people are meeting on . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The final element is context , and for this , I&amp;rsquo ; ll stick to only the most recent . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Eight years ago , Idle No More shut down multiple border crossings , blocked railway lines , disrupted traffic with flash mobs and garnered international attention . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; It petered out when then-Prime Minister Stephen Harper promised high-level dialogue , not with Idle No More&amp;rsquo ; s female founders and leaders , but with then-Assembly of First Nations Chief Shawn Atleo. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Five years ago , the Truth and Reconciliation Commission&amp;rsquo ; s 11 pages of recommendations provided a roadmap for overcoming the legacy of residential schools . &amp;nbsp ; Prime Minister Justin Trudeau committed to implementing all 94 recommendations in December 2015 . The government&amp;rsquo ; s progress is tracked online at &lt;a href= " http : **34;18737;TOOLONG ... &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; That raised Indigenous peoples&amp;rsquo ; expectations , as did the appointment of Jody Wilson-Raybould as justice minister . But many hopes were dashed with the government&amp;rsquo ; s purchase of the Trans Mountain Pipeline and its subsequent re-approval , as did **25;18773;TOOLONG ; s demotion and eventual ouster from the Liberal caucus following her testimony about how Trudeau tried to convince her not to prosecute SNC-Lavalin. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Which brings us to the gas pipeline going through Wet&amp;rsquo ; suwet&amp;rsquo ; en territory that is backed by John Horgan&amp;rsquo ; s NDP government &amp;mdash ; the same government that less than a year ago promised to bring B.C. laws in line with the UN Declaration on the Rights of Indigenous People . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Adding to the frustrations are 1,200 murdered and missing women , health outcomes that lag other Canadians by a wide margin , and 27 years of B.C. treaty negotiations that have yielded only eight finalized agreements , involving only 38 per cent of the province&amp;rsquo ; s Indian Act bands . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; And , yes , there&amp;rsquo ; s climate change that also disproportionately affects Indigenous people . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Senator Murray Sinclair , who chaired the Truth and Reconciliation Commission , wrote in a recent blogpost that what is needed to resolve the Wet&amp;rsquo ; suwet&amp;rsquo ; en pipeline dispute are &amp;ldquo ; positive acts . &amp;rdquo ; But he is not optimistic . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; Frankly , given Canada&amp;rsquo ; s intransigence , and the rising sense of injustice felt by Indigenous leadership throughout the country , I do not like where this is heading . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; It is a rich irony that among the strongest voices deriding protesters as radical activists and urging police to take action against them are themselves using the spectre of western separatism to get their way on pipeline approvals. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Canada is in the midst of an existential crisis with the intersection of Indigenous rights , climate change and the economy . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; There is no simple solution . Certainly , it can&amp;rsquo ; t be solved by company-sought court injunctions enforced by police , or by any other court rulings . It hasn&amp;rsquo ; t worked before in Canada and it hasn&amp;rsquo ; t worked in other democratic societies . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Canada&amp;rsquo ; s future depends on federal , provincial , Indigenous , business and community leaders as well as its citizens talking in good faith and with respect about what Canada should be and could be , not only a decade from now , but a century from now . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " **29;18800;TOOLONG " **36;18831;TOOLONG &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Twitter : @bramhamdaphne &lt;/p&gt; Daphne BramhamThu , 20 Feb 2020 17:33:57 **45;18869;TOOLONG ... **34;18916;TOOLONG Mulgrew : B.C. cannabis police ' blink , ' and reverse their stance on evidencehttp : **32;18952;TOOLONG ... &lt;img src= " https : **34;18986;TOOLONG ... title= " Trees of Eden Grown co-owner Alex Robb recently got notice from B.C. The CSU alleges that Trees of Eden Island Grown sold pot between May 29 and July 31 , 2019 without a retail licence . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Until Postmedia&amp;rsquo ; s report Friday on one of the first fines imposed under the law , the CSU policy provided disclosure only after those who were impugned rejected the plea deal . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Now , they will be granted disclosure and have 45 days to decide whether to contest the notice or waive their entitlement to a hearing and pay the reduced penalty . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; Acceptance of a waiver after 45 days and up to the date that director ( Jamie Lipp ) conducts the administrative hearing will be at the discretion of the director , &amp;rdquo ; Romanuik added . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;19088;TOOLONG ... title= " Trees of Eden Grown co-owner Alex Robb recently got notice from B.C. It also was later raided and closed just before Labour Day . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Nevertheless , Bradbury pointed out 642 Cannabis was on the list of those about to reopen as an approved retail store while Robb had received one of the first fines. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; He also personally submitted an application for a licence back in Sept. 2018 , and is also still waiting . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The entire program raises more questions than answers , he said : &amp;ldquo ; In fact , there are few answers when one asks about licensing information . Seems like a very uneven playing field ! &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Bradbury relates the same experience as other applicants : they are constantly asked to provide additional information and documents are regularly lost . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Initially , Bradbury&amp;rsquo ; s entire application was lost yet his credit card was charged $5,000 , he said . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; He had to revise a site plan as it was outlined in blue ink not black ; then , he had to redo an organization chart because it was missing a name though the title was self-explanatory . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; You purchase a building , install security and monitoring equipment , upgrade that building , insure it , absorb additional costs and wait months and months for your number to be selected for a licence &amp;hellip ; this is just wrong , &amp;rdquo ; he insisted . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; We hear concerns about potential money laundering . For a small store in the Otter Point area of Sooke ? It pales by comparison to downtown Victoria or the casinos across B.C. One has to question how and where some of the large dispensaries that operated prior to legalization and then closed for legalization secured their products . It is time someone does an expos&amp;egrave ; on how the government licensing authority in this industry uses a pick-and-choose approach in processing applications . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Sounds like a job for the provincial money-laundering inquiry . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " **29;19154;TOOLONG " target= " blank " rel= " noopener " **36;19185;TOOLONG &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " http : //twitter.com/ianmulgrew " target= " blank " rel= " noopener " **31;19223;TOOLONG ... &lt;/p&gt; Ian MulgrewWed , 19 Feb 2020 16:25:54 **45;19256;TOOLONG ... BusinessCannabis LegalizationCannabis Small **38;19303;TOOLONG Todd : The morality of religion , driverless cars and everything elsehttp : **36;19343;TOOLONG ... &lt;img src= " https : **34;19381;TOOLONG ... title= " Azim Shariff is a social psychology prof at UBC who is studying morality around driverless cars . " alt= " Azim Shariff is a social psychology prof at UBC who is studying morality around driverless cars . " data-portal-copyright= " Mike Bell " **28;19417;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1216609 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; Azim Shariff is becoming known as a go-to expert on the ways morality relates to religion , driverless cars , IQs , organ transplants , artificial intelligence and how we view people who are &amp;ldquo ; busy . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; That doesn&amp;rsquo ; t mean the University of B.C. social psychologist comes across as at all moralistic &amp;mdash ; even when I asked how he exposed the big mistake in one of the decade&amp;rsquo ; s most &amp;ldquo ; surprising&amp;rdquo ; and widely cited studies on how religion shapes morality . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Shariff lead a team that discovered a high-profile University of Chicago research project had incorrectly concluded that children in Christian and Muslim households are more stingy than youngsters in non-religious households . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; In doing so , Shariff also uncovered some favourable findings about the children of Canada . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Since his own research had suggested a religious upbringing often increases kindness towards others , he led four colleagues in asking for access to Jean Decety&amp;rsquo ; s extensive data on the generosity of 1,200 children in six countries . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Shariff&amp;rsquo ; s group discovered there had been a coding error : The Chicago team&amp;rsquo ; s calculations in 2015 of how children performed in sharing coloured stickers with one another had , in effect and without being too technical about it , mixed up nationalities with religiosity. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; What&amp;rsquo ; s worse , as a few international media outlets reported in 2019 , the Chicago team&amp;rsquo ; s eventual retraction didn&amp;rsquo ; t receive a fraction of the scientific and media coverage that trumpeted the original negative findings about religion . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; In addition , virtually no media outlet has publicized a key result of the corrected data : That Canadian children emerged as the most sharing . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The Canadian children were found to be more generous than those from South Africa , Turkey , the United States , Jordan and China . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;h2 class= " cat-head short " **40;19447;TOOLONG ... &lt;ul class= " relatedlinks " &gt; &lt;li&gt;&lt;a href= " http : **34;19489;TOOLONG ... Todd : Is it OK to ask , ' Where are you from ? ' &lt;/a&gt;&lt;/li&gt; &lt;li&gt;&lt;a href= " http : **34;19525;TOOLONG ... Todd : Higher education has given up on the meaning of **25;19561;TOOLONG &lt;/ul&gt; &lt;p&gt; Shariff doesn&amp;rsquo ; t want to overemphasize the results , however . He says the children weren&amp;rsquo ; t too far apart based on nationality and , anyways , no one knows what factors produced the Canadian children&amp;rsquo ; s relatively so-called &amp;ldquo ; pro-social behaviour . &amp;rdquo ; For instance , many offspring in China are their parents&amp;rsquo ; only children , Shariff said , which is often associated with being self-focused. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Shariff doesn&amp;rsquo ; t have an axe to grind either for or against religion or any country . And he&amp;rsquo ; s wary of those who do . He&amp;rsquo ; s appreciating the chance to collaborate with the Chicago group to expand their research , which will now test the moral behaviour of children in 12 nations . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Born in Toronto to a Muslim family from India , the 38-year-old associate professor and Canada Research Chair says he drifted away from the family faith in his early teens . But , while studying psychology at the University of Toronto , he came intrigued by religion and ethical reasoning . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Teaching at UBC he&amp;rsquo ; s noticed many students crave to be taught morality , even in a &amp;ldquo ; paternalistic&amp;rdquo ; way . Today&amp;rsquo ; s young people seem to be reacting against , he says , the s &lt;a href= " http : **34;19588;TOOLONG ... target= " blank " rel= " noopener " &gt;o-called " values-free " education&lt;/a&gt; typically offered by secular colleges and universities . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Shariff&amp;rsquo ; s own original research has found when people &amp;mdash ; including Christians , Muslims , Jews , atheists &amp;shy ; and agnostics &amp;mdash ; are asked to think about religion , they become more generous not only to members of their own group , but also to those outside their group . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; And a new potentially controversial international study lead by Britain&amp;rsquo ; s Cory Jane Clark , in which Shariff took part , found &lt;a href= " https : **33;19624;TOOLONG ... target= " blank " rel= " noopener " &gt;religiosity and high-IQs were important factors in reducing violence&lt;/a&gt; , since each is associated with self-control . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; After looking at data across many countries ( not named in the paper ) , the scholars theorize that religion is more effective in reducing violence in societies with relatively lower average IQs . They don&amp;rsquo ; t want people to jump to conclusions , however . Shariff notes IQs are determined both by genetics and environment , and a society&amp;rsquo ; s average IQ tends to correlate to levels of education . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Never mind that there are many things beyond religiosity and IQ that influence morality , including the presence of police , taxation measures and secular philosophy , says Shariff. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; In contrast to ancient religions , another thing increasingly swaying our ethics is high technology . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; That&amp;rsquo ; s particularly the case with the kind of algorithms created to make life-altering medical ethics decisions about who gets priority for a donated organ and about the moral programming of self-driving cars . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Every time a driver makes a decision about how close to veer toward a bicyclist or whether to tailgate , he or she is making a moral choice , Shariff says . So he and colleagues have been researching whether the algorithmic decisions of driverless cars are compatible with wider human morality . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; MIT researcher Edmond Awad collaborated with Shariff and others to create a web-based experimental driving platform called &lt;a href= " http : //moralmachine.mit.edu/ " target= " blank " rel= " noopener " &gt;Moral Machine&lt;/a&gt; , which has now been played by several million people . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Moral Machine throws virtual drivers into stark moral dilemmas , such as having to choose between crashing into innocent pedestrians or saving their own lives . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;19659;TOOLONG ... title= " Moral Machine is an online experimental platform that asks participants about the moral choices they would make while driving . In this test the driver must decide between running down some elderly males or swerving and killing herself . " alt= " Moral Machine is an online experimental platform that asks participants about the moral choices they would make while driving . In this test the driver must decide between running down some elderly males or swerving and killing herself . " data-portal-copyright= " " **28;19695;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1215033 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; Shariff says the kinds of ethical responses that driverless car algorithms &amp;mdash ; and lawyers worried about legal liability &amp;mdash ; come up with to these somewhat &amp;ldquo ; cartoonish&amp;rdquo ; scenarios are not necessarily those of empathetic humans . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; That said , they&amp;rsquo ; re finding the morality of people can change by country . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; When Moral Machine asked online drivers to choose between mowing down a business executive or a homeless man , Shariff and colleagues found people in egalitarian Nordic countries were just as likely to protect the homeless person . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; But citizens of highly unequal countries , such as those in South America , were more protective of the business executive . Another Moral Machine experiment found Asians more likely than North Americans to save elderly pedestrians , not just young ones , from harm . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Since researchers are discovering humans show a startling range of responses to driving dilemmas , the question arises : Who will be granted the authority to program the life-and-death decision-making of self-driving vehicles ? &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; On the subject of robots , Shariff has also been looking into the economic morality associated with working , particularly if many are going to lose their jobs to automation . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; His experiments show that most of us , from Europe to Korea , view busyness as &amp;ldquo ; a moral signal of character , &amp;rdquo ; even when such people are not doing anything meaningful . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; A key question Shariff is working on is how society will view the masses of people who could be replaced by Artificial Intelligence , such as truck drivers , who might end up living on a basic income supplied by taxpayers : &amp;ldquo ; Will we think they are valuable members of society ? &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The moral decisions linked with everything we interact with are profound &amp;mdash ; and Shariff and his adventurous colleagues are helping us pay attention to their powerful consequences . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " **26;19725;TOOLONG " **33;19753;TOOLONG &lt;/p&gt; &lt;a href= " https : **25;19788;TOOLONG " class= " twitter-follow-button " data-show-count= " false " &gt;Follow @DouglasTodd&lt;/a&gt; Douglas ToddTue , 18 Feb 2020 16:33:26 **45;19815;TOOLONG ...
