" data-portal-copyright= " Geoff Robins/AFP via Getty Images/File " **28;19932;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1196325 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; A Prince George man may face a rare third trial after an appeal throwing out his second conviction underscored the need to train judges in sexual misconduct cases . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; After two expensive , emotional trials &amp;mdash ; one before a jury &amp;mdash ; the province&amp;rsquo ; s high bench found both justices made serious errors despite the attention in recent years on the need for care and sensitivity in dealing with these prosecutions plagued by myths and stereotypes . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Spurred by concerns raised by the #MeToo movement and former CBC-host Jian Ghomeshi&amp;rsquo ; s high-profile , controversial 2016 trial , the federal Liberals last week introduced Bill C- &lt;span style= " font-family : -apple-system , BlinkMacSystemFont , ' Segoe UI ' , Roboto , Oxygen-Sans , Ubuntu , Cantarell , ' Helvetica Neue ' , sans-serif ; " &gt;5 &lt;/span&gt; &lt;span style= " font-family : -apple-system , BlinkMacSystemFont , ' Segoe UI ' , Roboto , Oxygen-Sans , Ubuntu , Cantarell , ' Helvetica Neue ' , sans-serif ; " &gt;to ensure newly appointed superior court judges are properly trained for sexual cases . &lt;/span&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; It also would require the Canadian Judicial Council to report on efforts to provide similar training to sitting judges and amend the Criminal Code to ensure judges provide reasons on the record for sexual offences . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The bill mirrors previous legislation introduced by former Conservative MP Rona Ambrose . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; There are some issues &amp;hellip ; that really are above politics , &amp;rdquo ; she said , supporting the proposed changes while standing with Minister for Women and Gender Equality Maryam Monsef and Justice Minister David Lametti. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Her bill passed the House with bipartisan support before moldering in the Senate . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; This case exemplified the issues . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; B.C. Supreme Justice Lance Bernard , the second trial judge , edited his reasons after they were delivered &amp;mdash ; although the three-justice panel did not address that ground of appeal , deciding he erred more significantly . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; In my view , reversible error in law is reflected in the edited version of the reasons for judgment , &amp;rdquo ; &lt;a href= " https : **33;19962;TOOLONG ... target= " blank " rel= " noopener " &gt;said Justice Gregory Fitch&lt;/a&gt; , supported by colleagues Mary Saunders and Christopher Grauer. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; As a consequence , it is unnecessary for this court to address the extent to which trial judges can edit their oral reasons for judgment to properly dispose of this appeal . In these circumstances , the issue is best left to be explored another day . &amp;hellip ; I am of the view that a new trial is required &amp;hellip ; &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; In February 2015 , at the first trial , a jury found Paul Peter Veeken guilty of sexually interfering with a young girl . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; But the appellate bench tossed that decision , saying B.C. Supreme Court Justice Elliot Myers failed to properly instruct the jury in that trial . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The unanimous decision concluded the jury&amp;rsquo ; s reasoning might have been improperly influenced by testimony from the girl&amp;rsquo ; s mother . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; The jury should have been instructed that what R.&amp;rsquo ; s mother said she was told ( about Veeken ) was hearsay and was not proof of the truth of what she had been told , &amp;rdquo ; &lt;a href= " https : **33;19997;TOOLONG ... target= " blank " rel= " noopener " &gt;Justice David Frankel wrote&lt;/a&gt; . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; More importantly , the jury should have been specifically instructed that it must not rely on that evidence to conclude Mr. Veeken was a person of bad character who was , therefore , more likely to have committed the offence . &amp;hellip ; Simply instructing the jury to &amp;lsquo ; ignore&amp;rsquo ; the evidence was not sufficient to ensure a fair trial . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Veeken , now 47 , was re-tried &amp;mdash ; this time before a judge alone . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; After the second trial , in December 2018 , Bernard found Veeken guilty again of inappropriately and deliberately touching the girl over the course of about two years starting when she was 10 , often under the guise of tickling or playing with her . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; When testifying , Veeken admitted to hosting get-togethers for groups of children at a family cabin where they would use a hot tub and watch movies &amp;mdash ; with no other adults present . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; He acknowledged providing candy and ice cream along with toys and children&amp;rsquo ; s movies . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The girl complained to her parents about Veeken in 2013 after being warned at a babysitting course about men and conduct of concern . They went to police . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Bernard reputedly concluded Veeken groomed the girl for his own sexual gratification while &amp;ldquo ; hiding in plain view&amp;rdquo ; by using the other children as a &amp;ldquo ; smokescreen of sorts . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; In December 2018 , he said the victim was a &amp;ldquo ; naive young girl&amp;rdquo ; enthralled by Veeken : &amp;ldquo ; It was a child&amp;rsquo ; s dream that sadly turned into a nightmare . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The appeal division , however , said his verdict was fatally flawed . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; The judge was certainly entitled to reject the appellant&amp;rsquo ; s evidence and find that it did not raise a reasonable doubt about his guilt , &amp;rdquo ; Fitch concluded . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; The difficulty in this case arises because of the judge&amp;rsquo ; s conclusion that he could neither accept the appellant&amp;rsquo ; s evidence nor find that it raised a reasonable doubt in the absence of independent and reliable evidence supporting his account . To approach the issue this way was to erroneously shift the burden to the accused to adduce evidence that confirmed his denial of wrongdoing &amp;hellip ; it taints his reasons as a whole . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Veeken&amp;rsquo ; s lawyer Scott Wright described the case as &amp;ldquo ; basically he said , she said . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; It&amp;rsquo ; s tough ( to explain ) because the trial court reasons have not been released yet from the second trial and they shed a bit more light on the other grounds of appeal , &amp;rdquo ; he said . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; It&amp;rsquo ; s challenging and it&amp;rsquo ; s a tough spot for trial judges to be in &amp;mdash ; to articulate why it is you believe somebody or disbelieve somebody . So , certainly there&amp;rsquo ; s some sympathy there for the process they have to undertake isn&amp;rsquo ; t easy to articulate something that is sometimes just a &amp;lsquo ; feel&amp;rsquo ; thing in terms of accepting what somebody is telling you . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Sentenced to two years in jail and one year&amp;rsquo ; s probation , Veeken has served his custodial time , Wright added , so he didn&amp;rsquo ; t think a new trial necessary . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;span class= " s1 " &gt; " The ( B.C. Prosecution Service ) is reviewing the decision carefully , " said spokesman Dan McLaughlin . " No final decision has yet been made regarding the next steps in the prosecution . " &lt;/span&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " **29;20032;TOOLONG " target= " blank " rel= " noopener " **36;20063;TOOLONG &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " http : //twitter.com/ianmulgrew " target= " blank " rel= " noopener " **31;20101;TOOLONG ... &lt;/p&gt; Ian MulgrewMon , 17 Feb 2020 21:40:32 **45;20134;TOOLONG ... to evacuate Canadians from quarantined Princess Diamond cruise shiphttp : **36;20181;TOOLONG ... &lt;img src= " https : **34;20219;TOOLONG ... title= " People wearing masks stand near the cruise ship Diamond Princess at Daikoku Pier Cruise Terminal in Yokohama , south of Tokyo , Japan February 6 , 2020. " alt= " People wearing masks stand near the cruise ship Diamond Princess at Daikoku Pier Cruise Terminal in Yokohama , south of Tokyo , Japan February 6 , 2020. " data-portal-copyright= " KIM KYUNG-HOON " **28;20255;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1208481 " /&gt; &lt;iframe width= " 100% " height= " 100% " frameborder= " 0 " allowfullscreen= " true " src= " https : **34;20285;TOOLONG ... &gt; &lt;/iframe&gt; &lt;p&gt; The federal government says it will evacuate Canadians quarantined aboard a cruise ship docked in Japan . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; They&amp;rsquo ; ve been stuck on the Diamond Princess cruise ship for 10 days amid an outbreak of the novel coronavirus , which has so far infected 15 Canadians on board . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; A statement released Saturday night says the government has chartered a plane to bring the Canadians to southern Ontario , where they&amp;rsquo ; ll be quarantined for another 14 days . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Global Affairs says the passengers will be screened before boarding the plane , and those who exhibit symptoms of COVID-19 will be transferred to the Japanese health-care system . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; It says the &amp;ldquo ; extraordinary circumstances&amp;rdquo ; facing Canadians aboard the ship have driven it to evacuate. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Experts have questioned if the close quarters have contributed to the spread . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;h2 class= " cat-head short " **40;20321;TOOLONG ... &lt;ul class= " relatedlinks " &gt; &lt;li&gt;&lt;a href= " https : **33;20363;TOOLONG ... case of coronavirus reported in British **29;20398;TOOLONG &lt;li&gt;&lt;a href= " https : **33;20429;TOOLONG ... things to know about coronavirus in **25;20464;TOOLONG &lt;li&gt;&lt;a href= " https : **33;20491;TOOLONG ... is a website that tracks and displays the spread of the coronavirus in real **25;20526;TOOLONG &lt;/ul&gt; The Canadian PressSun , 16 Feb 2020 03:52:58 **45;20553;TOOLONG ... TravelLocal NewsNationalDouglas Todd : What 's with the British names for new luxury buildings ? http : **30;20600;TOOLONG ... &lt;img src= " https : **34;20632;TOOLONG ... title= " In Metro , developers tends to choose English or Latinate names for luxury buildings ... because it works with buyers , including those from Asia " alt= " In Metro , developers tends to choose English or Latinate names for luxury buildings ... because it works with buyers , including those from Asia " data-portal-copyright= " " **28;20668;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1214200 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; A contender for most striking name for a new Vancouver condominium building may be The Empire at the Park . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; In the world of luxury housing , the names of tony apartment buildings are often considered the &lt;a href= " http : **34;20698;TOOLONG ... target= " blank " rel= " noopener " &gt;most effective way to market them&lt;/a&gt; to buyers , both local and global , who are looking for exclusivity and prestige . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Yet The Empire at the Park , also known as The Empire at Queen Elizabeth Park , could not be more reflective of British heritage , even colonialism . For a globalized , cosmopolitan city like Vancouver , it seems counterintuitive that the names of many &lt;a href= " http : **34;20734;TOOLONG ... target= " blank " rel= " noopener " &gt;new luxury buildings&lt;/a&gt; are often British , with others linked to Latin languages . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The Vancouver luxury market targets foreign buyers as much as it does domestic ones . And whatever developers are doing with English-sounding and Latinate names seems to be working . The stylish units of The Empire at the Park , at Cambie and 29th , which run in the $1.2 million to $1.6 million range , sold out quickly . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; When it comes to marketing high-end British Columbia housing , is it possible the Britishness of the place is part of its brand appeal , even in a city where more than four out of 10 people have Asian roots ? English and familiar Latin-based names are prevalent in most new costly condo buildings , including the many flying up along Cambie Street . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The Chelsea , named after a posh neighbourhood in London , is selling units for $3.3 million . A two-bedroom condo in the Cambie Collection&amp;rsquo ; s Grayson building , built by the very British-sounding Pennyfarthing Development Corp. , goes for about $1.5 million . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Mansion Global Magazine says the trend to using names as brands really took off in London in the 1990s , when &amp;ldquo ; a lot of overseas money came into the city , and people were all of a sudden demanding luxury apartment buildings that didn&amp;rsquo ; t previously exist . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The name of London&amp;rsquo ; s tony Lancaster building , for instance , plays on English heritage . One Hyde Park emphasized its uniqueness . The new Ritz-Carlton and Versace apartments in London capitalize on Western luxury brands , which helped make them the rage in the Middle East and Asia . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;h2 class= " cat-head short " **40;20770;TOOLONG ... &lt;ul class= " relatedlinks " &gt; &lt;li&gt;&lt;a href= " http : **34;20812;TOOLONG ... Todd : Is the English language privileged ? &lt;/a&gt;&lt;/li&gt; &lt;li&gt;&lt;a href= " http : **34;20848;TOOLONG ... Todd : Are ' iconic ' buildings good or bad for Vancouver ? &lt;/a&gt;&lt;/li&gt; &lt;li&gt;&lt;a href= " http : **34;20884;TOOLONG ... Vancouver 's renters pummelled by demovictions and **35;20920;TOOLONG &lt;/ul&gt; &lt;p&gt; Many people in Asian countries value both the English language and Western products , says economic consultant Jean-Noel Kapferer . In his book , Abundant Rarity : The Key to Luxury Growth , Kapferer writes about how &amp;ldquo ; Asian consumers feel safer buying prestigious Western brands with which individuals around them are familiar . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Whether appealing to buyers in the West or East , access to green space also helps explain how the most popular name for North American buildings is &amp;ldquo ; Park , &amp;rdquo ; suggesting a special location with pastoral charm . Apartment Guide Magazine says &amp;ldquo ; Park&amp;rdquo ; has been used in the names of more than 1,400 apartment and condominium blocks , with &amp;ldquo ; Village&amp;rdquo ; clicking in at 1,200. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;20957;TOOLONG ... title= " Promotional image for Empire at the Park condo complex , on Cambie near Queen Elizabeth Park " alt= " Promotional image for Empire at the Park condo complex , on Cambie near Queen Elizabeth Park " data-portal-copyright= " Screen shot from Empire at the P " **28;20993;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1213048 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; Vancouver&amp;rsquo ; s luxury developers are crazy about the name &amp;ldquo ; Park . &amp;rdquo ; In addition to Empire at the Park , there is Park House , on 59th near Cambie , where prices run to $4 million a unit . Dwellings in Ivy in the Park , next to UBC at Wesbrook Village , go for as much as $3.7 million . A top spot at Balpark , near the Langara golf course , is $4 million . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; Oak&amp;rdquo ; is also fashionable for expensive housing , says Apartment Guide . In that vein , Vancouver has Terraces at Oak Park , at 41st and Oak Street , where units cost $1.6 million . And Westbank Corp . continues to erect hundreds more elite condos at Oakridge , where some sell for $9.9 million . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; There are more inventive names , however , including The ARC , from Concord Pacific . It&amp;rsquo ; s marketing evokes the &lt;a href= " http : **34;21023;TOOLONG ... target= " blank " rel= " noopener " &gt;iconic&lt;/a&gt; Arc de Triomphe in Paris , even while the word also hints at the biblical story of Noah and the Earth&amp;rsquo ; s creatures finding refuge in an ark . However the name is interpreted , The ARC&amp;rsquo ; s False Creek condo units are selling for as much as $5.7 million . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; In a province where activists and politicians often press for more places and buildings to be given traditional First Nations names , there are few new high-end buildings that do so . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The exception is &lt;a href= " https : //lelemliving.com/ " target= " blank " rel= " noopener " &gt;Lelem , &lt;/a&gt; also known as Cedar Walk , which is on University Boulevard going toward the UBC campus , where the Musqueam Indian Band is marketing classy units for anywhere from $779,000 to $3.9 million , at an average price of $1,399 a square foot . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Indigenous names are not common among the pricey condominium complexes of Metro Vancouver , including for the thousands of stylish units going up on the University Endowment Lands . UBC is creating a &amp;ldquo ; master-planned&amp;rdquo ; community at Wesbrook Village with buildings called Eton ( after the posh English school ) and Ultima ( a Latin superlative ) . Some upmarket complexes at UBC are named after famous Europeans , such as The Residences at Nobel Park , where the prices range from $2.9 million to $3.9 million . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Vancouver realtor Bryan Yan , who specializes in luxury homes for Regent Park Realty Inc. ( named after a royal park in London ) , recognizes Western-sounding names and brands often come with a certain cachet in parts of the world , particularly as &lt;a href= " http : **34;21059;TOOLONG ... target= " blank " rel= " noopener " &gt;English evolves into a global lingua franca. &lt;/a&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Yet Yan , who speaks six languages including Mandarin , Cantonese and German , also thinks condominium complexes in a global city like Vancouver get English names because &amp;ldquo ; English is just a beautiful language . &amp;rdquo ; Developers aim to capitalize on English or familiar European names , Yan says , that are easy for anyone , regardless of their mother tongue , to pronounce . He notes most Indigenous names are hard for many people to pronounce . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The popularity of English names is illustrated by the way a young woman in England is making millions of dollars helping Chinese parents come up with English names for their babies . Building on the fascination people have for Western culture , Beau Jessup has an online company that has helped &lt;a href= " https : **33;21095;TOOLONG ... target= " blank " rel= " noopener " &gt;give 600,000 Chinese children English names . &lt;/a&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Yan is also among the many realtors and marketers aware that people from the West and East can be drawn to easy-to-pronounce but still exotic-sounding Italian , French and Latinate names . Like The Marquis , The Contessa or The Mirabel ( all Vancouver buildings ) , which sound familiar to most . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The University of Hong Kong&amp;rsquo ; s Stephen Matthews , of the linguistics department , has pointed out the allure of English for some in Asia goes back to colonial times &amp;ldquo ; when it seemed desirable or prestigious to have an English name , &amp;rdquo ; in part to show you can do business with foreigners . Now , however , Matthews says , &amp;ldquo ; The English language in Hong Kong is no longer a symbol of British influence , but part of people&amp;rsquo ; s identity . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Allen Adamson , an American brand specialist , says a pronouncable , memorable and evocative building names will rapidly move a developer &amp;ldquo ; to the 50-yard line when it comes to marketing a property . &amp;rdquo ; And in Metro Vancouver , it&amp;rsquo ; s clear an English or Latin name is helping many builders score touchdowns. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; dtodd@postmedia.com &lt;/p&gt; &lt;a href= " https : **25;21130;TOOLONG " class= " twitter-follow-button " data-show-count= " false " &gt;Follow @DouglasTodd&lt;/a&gt; Douglas ToddFri , 14 Feb 2020 19:03:30 **45;21157;TOOLONG ... &amp;amp ; SellingColumnistsLocal NewsNationalStaff BlogsTrans Mountain confident court orders will protect against pipeline protestshttp : **32;21204;TOOLONG ... &lt;img src= " https : **34;21238;TOOLONG ... title= " Bronwen Boulton-Scott , Debbie Mowat , Katie Welch and Shelly McKerchar lead a group of seven dancers in a Red Brigade protest against the Trans Mountain pipeline twinning project in Kamloops on Oct. 7 , 2019. Kamloops This WeekThu , 13 Feb 2020 23:56:14 **45;21444;TOOLONG ... NewsNationalDaphne Bramham : Lack of addictions treatment for youth needs urgent fixhttp : **37;21491;TOOLONG ... &lt;img src= " https : **34;21530;TOOLONG ... title= " Elliot Eurchuk in a family photo . " alt= " Elliot Eurchuk in a family photo . " data-portal-copyright= " HO " **28;21566;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1212698 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; As B.C. heads into the fifth year of a public health emergency due to the high number of opioid overdose deaths , Vancouver Island still doesn&amp;rsquo ; t have a single residential-treatment for youth . Provincewide , the number of youth beds and services lag demand . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; For youth who do get one of those precious treatment beds , their transition back to community-based services is badly planned and poorly managed . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Had all of that been in place , 16-year-old Elliot Cleveland Eurchuk might have survived his addiction rather than being counted among the 4,850 British Columbians to have died between January 2016 and Oct. 31 , 2019. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; But the teen&amp;rsquo ; s legacy could be &amp;mdash ; should be &amp;mdash ; that Health Minister Adrian Dix and Premier John Horgan making addictions treatment as much of a priority as harm reduction . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " https : **33;21596;TOOLONG ... target= " blank " rel= " noopener " **29;21631;TOOLONG from the coroner&amp;rsquo ; s inquest into Eurchuk&amp;rsquo ; s 2018 death released on Monday provide some direction : More acute-care beds for youths including a residential treatment centre in Victoria ; more and better access to addictions services ; and resources for early detection of mental-health and substance-use disorders among youth . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; For more , the government ought to dig out its copies of the 2018 &lt;a href= " https : **33;21662;TOOLONG ... target= " blank " rel= " noopener " &gt;report&lt;/a&gt; from the B.C. Centre on Substance Use that recommended a &amp;ldquo ; full , evidence-based continuum of care including building an effective and coordinated addiction treatment and recovery system that has traditionally been lacking . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; That report also singled out the need for youth-specific services and treatment including residential care . It also recommended &amp;ldquo ; recovery high schools&amp;rdquo ; where not only are drugs and alcohol are strictly prohibited , treatment and services are part of the curriculum . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Men aged 19 to 59 make up the overwhelming majority of the people who have died of opioid overdoses . But , an average of 18 youths have died in each of the past four years . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The recommendations aren&amp;rsquo ; t only aimed at preventing youth from dying . They&amp;rsquo ; re aimed at providing treatment to prevent their addictions from becoming entrenched . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; In addition to the recommendations , the coroner&amp;rsquo ; s report provides a glimpse of the other opioid crisis far away from Vancouver&amp;rsquo ; s Downtown Eastside. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;h2 class= " cat-head short " **40;21697;TOOLONG ... &lt;ul class= " relatedlinks " &gt; &lt;li&gt;&lt;a href= " https : **33;21739;TOOLONG ... teen overdose inquest recommends treatment **33;21774;TOOLONG &lt;/ul&gt; &lt;p&gt; Eurchuk knew about harm reduction services , but he didn&amp;rsquo ; t get his drugs tested , didn&amp;rsquo ; t go to safe injection sites , didn&amp;rsquo ; t seek treatment or replacement therapies such as methadone or Suboxone. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; He&amp;rsquo ; d started using cannabis in November 2015 when he was 13 . After injuring his shoulder wrestling a year later , he began self-medicating , buying hydromorphone from a classmate at Oak Bay High School . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; In December 2016 , he broke his jaw playing soccer and , after surgery , was prescribed hydromorphone for the pain . Two months later , he had the first of two surgeries on his shoulder and was prescribed another opioid , Tramacet , for the pain . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; After reinjuring his shoulder that fall , Eurchuk was given another prescription for Tramacet . He was also suspended from school , accused of selling drugs to classmates. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; After a second shoulder operation that October , Eurchuk got a five-day prescription for Oxycodone , followed up by a prescription for Tramacet. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; In the final months of his life , Eurchuk was routinely using opioids to the point that when he was hospitalized in early 2018 for a serious infection , he got a day pass and got fentanyl and cocaine while he was out . He went into cardiac arrest in the hospital on his return . &lt;img src= " https : **34;21809;TOOLONG ... title= " " alt= " " data-portal-copyright= " " **28;21845;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " " /&gt; **25;21875;TOOLONG &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; He was home briefly in February before being readmitted under the Mental Health Act . Discharged after a week , Eurchuk was in the emergency room of Vancouver&amp;rsquo ; s St. Paul&amp;rsquo ; s Hospital in March because of decreased consciousness and released after a few hours . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; On his final day , Eurchuk bought a two-day supply from a street dealer , used with a friend early in the evening and was heavily intoxicated by the time they parted ways . As the evening wore on , people who saw him described him as everything from fine to agitated to disoriented . He was last seen at midnight . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The teen died on the morning of April 10 at home from a heart attack , fluid in the lungs and aspiration caused by &amp;ldquo ; mixed intoxication&amp;rdquo ; from fentanyl , cocaine , heroin and methamphetamine. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Attempts to revive him with naloxone , chest compressions , suction and a defibrillator failed . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; While the government will provide a written response to the coroner&amp;rsquo ; s recommendations in the coming weeks , last summer it committed $2.4 million over three years to addictions and mental health programs . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; It has opened four youth detox beds in Victoria . There are eight Foundry Centres across the province providing comprehensive supports with three more being developed . &amp;nbsp ; And , this spring , a 20-bed treatment facility in Chilliwack is scheduled to open . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; There is no guarantee that better acute-care treatment , earlier interventions and more comprehensive community services will save the lives of every addicted youth or that they would have saved Eurchuk. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Addiction is , after all , a chronic , relapsing condition . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Elliot Eurchuk was just a kid and there are others like him . They deserve the best chance possible to grow up to be healthy adults . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " **29;21902;TOOLONG " **36;21933;TOOLONG &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Twitter : @bramhamdaphne &lt;/p&gt; Daphne BramhamWed , 12 Feb 2020 23:12:30 **45;21971;TOOLONG ... &amp;amp ; ChildHealthLocal HealthLocal **45;22018;TOOLONG drop cars and deliver groceries to help travellers from China self-quarantinehttp : **25;22065;TOOLONG ... &lt;img src= " https : **34;22092;TOOLONG ... title= " A basket of groceries on its way to a family that has self-quarantined after returning to Metro Vancouver from China . " data-portal-copyright= " " **28;22128;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1210687 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; The messaging app WeChat is connecting travellers arriving in Metro from China with volunteers willing to drop cars off at the airport and deliver groceries to their homes , so the travellers can quarantine themselves for two weeks . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; It&amp;rsquo ; s a grassroots action based on two weeks being considered the maximum incubation period for the deadly coronavirus that emerged in Wuhan , China . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; I just want to offer my help , rather than people feeling like they have to get into a taxi , &amp;rdquo ; said Frank Kong , a local photographer who joined a WeChat group started at the beginning of the week . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; He explained how it matched him up with a family that also lives in Burnaby. &amp;ldquo ; I met the wife and drove their car and dropped it off to her husband at the airport . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; He was coming in from Shanghai . &amp;ldquo ; I saw him from a distance . He knew his car . I waved bye , &amp;rdquo ; said Kong . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; He then got a ride home from the airport by another driver , also connected to him by the WeChat group . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; On Friday , he was back at the airport to do another car drop-off , this time for a passenger arriving from another city in mainland China via Hong Kong . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;h2 class= " cat-head short " **40;22158;TOOLONG ... &lt;ul class= " relatedlinks " &gt; &lt;li&gt;&lt;a href= " https : **33;22200;TOOLONG ... couple describes life trapped in Wuhan as B.C. reports first case of coronavirus **25;22235;TOOLONG &lt;li&gt;&lt;a href= " https : **33;22262;TOOLONG ... at sea : Aboard the Diamond Princess in Yokohama **28;22297;TOOLONG &lt;/ul&gt; &lt;p&gt; There are various groups popping up on WeChat. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Li Li , a teacher who lives in Vancouver and uses the WeChat handle lilyxmca , started the one that matched Kong . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; It started when she saw a petition on WeChat calling on the Canadian government to ban all flights from China to Canada . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; It had been signed by thousands of people , including many people with Chinese Canadian names , but Li , who moved to Vancouver from Xiamen in China&amp;rsquo ; s eastern Fujian province 13 years ago , didn&amp;rsquo ; t like the idea . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; I thought , why cut off people who live here ? &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; But she , too , is worried about the continuing spread of the coronavirus . She had some more elaborate ideas , but friends convinced her to start with a simple offer to drop off cars and deliver groceries so travellers could more easily avoid public spaces such as taxis , transit , supermarkets and malls. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; There was an immediate response and Li said there are about 50 &amp;ldquo ; helpers&amp;rdquo ; across Metro Vancouver that she has now divided into mini-groups for different areas with team leaders to coordinate matches . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; On Thursday , Canadian health officials asked anyone arriving in Canada from Hubei province , which surrounds Wuhan and is the centre of the outbreak in China , to voluntarily observe a 14 day quarantine even if they have no symptoms . But many travellers to Vancouver from China had already been doing that , perhaps on the advice issued Jan. 28 by China&amp;rsquo ; s embassy in Canada . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Li said even before Ottawa escalated its response this week , she had received more than 100 requests for help as families deal with travellers returning from &amp;ldquo ; all over China . Guangzhou . Beijing . Shanghai . Hong Kong . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; On Friday night , volunteers dropped off cars for two families with members returning from Hunan province in central China and Sichuan province in the southwest . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Canada has not suspended flights from China , but the federal government is advising against all non-essential travel to China and telling any Canadians in China to leave using commercial flights as soon as possible . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; By contrast , &amp;ldquo ; at least 14 countries and territories , from the U.S. to India to Hong Kong have now introduced some form of China-related travel restrictions as policy-makers try to contain the spread of a virus that has killed more than 560 people and infected nearly 30,000 , &amp;rdquo ; according to Bloomberg. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; China has really praised Canada , and most Chinese ( Canadian ) people here felt so good ( to see that ) . Like Kong and others who are getting involved by driving , delivering or just circulating calls for volunteers , she said going through that experience in China or Hong Kong motivated her to take responsibility . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; We know it will get worse . We want to do meaningful things . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; jlee-young@postmedia.com &lt;/p&gt; Joanne Lee-YoungSat , 08 Feb 2020 03:08:49 **45;22327;TOOLONG ... NewsNationalWorldIan Mulgrew : Key tossed for another Indigenous offenderhttp : **32;22374;TOOLONG ... &lt;img src= " https : **34;22408;TOOLONG ... title= " Since April 2010 the Indigenous inmates have increased by 43.4 per cent ( or 1,265 ) , whereas the non-Indigenous incarcerated population has declined by 13.7 per cent ( or 1,549 ) , according to the office of the Correctional Investigator of Canada . " alt= " Since April 2010 the Indigenous inmates have increased by 43.4 per cent ( or 1,265 ) , whereas the non-Indigenous incarcerated population has declined by 13.7 per cent ( or 1,549 ) , according to the office of the Correctional Investigator of Canada . " data-portal-copyright= " Rattankun Thongbun " **28;22444;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1202572 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; Despite the scandalous over-representation of Indigenous people in our country&amp;rsquo ; s prison system , the B.C. Court of Appeal turned a deaf ear to a plea from a First Nations man whose life was shaped two generations before he was born . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The three justices asked : &amp;ldquo ; What should we do with him ? &amp;rdquo ; His lawyer Hovan Patey answered : &amp;ldquo ; Anything but lock him up and throw away the key . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; He wasn&amp;rsquo ; t persuasive enough . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " https : **33;22474;TOOLONG ... target= " blank " rel= " noopener " &gt;The case&lt;/a&gt; highlights the inter-generational cost of colonialism and residential schools as well as the challenges facing judges , the vast majority non-Aboriginal , trying to compensate for it : The crippling historical wounds and scar tissue Indigenous people bear are the very reason some are considered a threat to public safety . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Johnny Wilfred Troy Awasis was designated a dangerous offender in June 2016 and sentenced to an indeterminate term of imprisonment after convictions for two sexual offences in August 2011. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; A troubling corollary to the news that Indigenous overrepresentation in Canada has hit new highs &amp;mdash ; one-in-three men behind bars &amp;mdash ; is that a dangerous offender designation is a virtual guarantee an Aboriginal man will die in jail , receiving only toe-tag parole . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Patey argued that was grossly disproportionate to Awasis moral culpability and manifestly unfit given the 38-year-old&amp;rsquo ; s tragic background . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; He proposed a long-term offender declaration , a sentence of eight to nine years ( less time served ) , and a 10-year supervision order given the evidence of Awasis&amp;rsquo ; treatability. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; That is a very , very significant sanction with a lot of safeguards built into it , but it is not a sentence that will guarantee he&amp;rsquo ; ll die in jail , &amp;rdquo ; Patey added . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Like many Indigenous offenders , Awasis wasn&amp;rsquo ; t brought up , he was dragged up . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; His parents and grandparents went to residential schools . His mother Bertha Johnny , of the Anaham First Nation in the B.C. Interior , was raped at 13 by a priest and gave birth to a child surrendered to social services . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; She moved to Vancouver , became addicted , lived on the street , and bore two more children by different fathers . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Then she met Wilfred Awasis , a member of the Thunderchild Cree Nation from Saskatchewan , described as a &amp;ldquo ; violent alcoholic , &amp;rdquo ; and they had four children , the second Johnny . The family lived for a while on the Anaham Reserve , and then in Saskatchewan . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; When his father was later imprisoned , his mother and Johnny returned to the Downtown Eastside. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; At six , he was left with an aunt and , when his mother didn&amp;rsquo ; t show up for a month , Awasis was placed in a residential school for about seven months . He was emotionally , physically and sexually abused . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; He was seven when his mother retrieved him and returned him to a squalid hard-scrabble life . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; School was a disaster , not only because of his life , but also his learning disabilities . He stopped showing up in Grade 8 . He was using marijuana at 10 , alcohol and cocaine at 12 , and was in the courts at 13. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Johnny was repeatedly apprehended by the Ministry of Children and Family Development , then returned to his mother in an unconscionable cycle of poverty and neglect . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; By 18 , he was living in a drug house and dealing to support his cocaine habit : He has never worked . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; He fathered a child in 2005 with a partner , also addicted , and the child was taken into care . Awasis was convicted of assaulting her . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; His record is book-length &amp;mdash ; mischief , assault , extortion , robbery , theft , escaping custody , break-and-enter , attempted theft over $5,000 , uttering threats , assaulting a peace officer , being unlawfully at large &amp;hellip ; and a sexual assault in 2006. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; In November 2010 , he was released on parole to the Circle of Eagles , a halfway house focusing on Indigenous culture and providing spiritual counselling by elders . He was on his way back to prison within an hour . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; He was released again and once more parole was quickly suspended . After a third release , back to Circle of Eagles in June 2011 , parole was again revoked. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The reason each time : He immediately got drunk . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Awasis was finally released at the end of July 2011 when his sentence expired . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Within 10 days , on Aug. 8 , he threatened a woman at a social services office . The following day , he committed the first sexual assault in this appeal , and on Aug. 31 , the second . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; He was arrested on Sept. 3 , 2011 , and imprisoned . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Since his youth , Awasis was described as &amp;ldquo ; out of control , &amp;rdquo ; unable to experience empathy or feel for his victims , a child &amp;ldquo ; who never had a chance . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; His correctional service &amp;ldquo ; healing&amp;rdquo ; plan included abstaining from substance abuse , counselling , maintaining a connection to his heritage , upgrading his education , accepting responsibility and being honest . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Given that he can focus for about as long as a goldfish and has symptoms of fetal alcohol syndrome , severe personality disorder , psychopathic traits , cognitive and addiction issues , I&amp;rsquo ; m not surprised he&amp;rsquo ; s had trouble &amp;ldquo ; healing . &amp;rdquo ; They might as well have asked him to Awaken the Giant Within . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Still , he is the face of the victims of colonialism , and the Supreme Court of Canada has been telling judges for a quarter-century to have compassion and not to write them off . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Yet , not only did the trial judge condemn Awasis to indefinite incarceration because she considered &amp;ldquo ; public protection&amp;rdquo ; paramount regardless of his &amp;ldquo ; reduced moral blameworthiness , &amp;rdquo ; the appeal division concurred . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; The judge was not satisfied , based on the appellant&amp;rsquo ; s history , that his motivation to change was sincere , or that he had the capacity to change , which were findings open to her on the evidence , &amp;rdquo ; &lt;a href= " https : **33;22509;TOOLONG ... target= " blank " rel= " noopener " &gt;wrote Justice Barbara Fisher&lt;/a&gt; , supported by benchmates Harvey Groberman and Patrice Abrioux. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Here is the conundrum : For minor cases , &lt;span class= " s1 " &gt;judges use Gladue reports , other tools and information provided to craft more appropriate sentences for Aboriginal offenders . &lt;/span&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;span class= " s1 " &gt;In serious cases like this , however&lt;/span&gt; &lt;span class= " s1 " &gt; , the exercise seems to shift&lt;/span&gt; &lt;span class= " s1 " &gt; from the Supreme Court 's instruction to reduce or eliminate the use of incarceration for Indigenous people , and instead , they ask : " How do I get around that here ? " &lt;/span&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;span class= " s1 " &gt; " Mr. Awasis ' case puts this starkly in focus : He committed serious offences , but has suffered some of the most tragic and impactful effects of colonialism imaginable , " lawyer Patey explained . &lt;/span&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;span class= " s1 " &gt; " His life was affected two generations before he was born . &lt;/span&gt; &lt;span class= " s1 " &gt; It 's difficult to say an offender has reduced moral blameworthiness , but then impose the severest sanction in Canadian law . " &lt;/span&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; He was frustrated : &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; We keep saying we&amp;rsquo ; re going to do this , but in the most difficult situations , in those troubling contexts , it&amp;rsquo ; s &amp;lsquo ; Yes , we acknowledge all of these principles , yes , we acknowledge all these factors are present , yes , we must do what we can to reduce the over-representation of the Aboriginal population in prison , BUT&amp;rsquo ; &amp;hellip ; &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; I think that&amp;rsquo ; s what has happened here , and that&amp;rsquo ; s what we see . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Instead of condemning Awasis , the judges had an alternative with a long-term offender sentence that would have respected the Supreme Court&amp;rsquo ; s direction and given him a chance &amp;mdash ; as slim as it might be . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " **29;22544;TOOLONG " target= " blank " rel= " noopener " **36;22575;TOOLONG &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " http : //twitter.com/ianmulgrew " target= " blank " rel= " noopener " **31;22613;TOOLONG ... &lt;/p&gt; Ian MulgrewSat , 08 Feb 2020 00:31:06 **45;22646;TOOLONG ... security rules do n't violate Charter rights , Federal Court ruleshttp : **35;22693;TOOLONG ... &lt;img src= " https : **34;22730;TOOLONG ... title= " Port of Vancouver file photo " alt= " Port of Vancouver file photo " data-portal-copyright= " NICK PROCAYLO " **28;22766;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 934404 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " https : **33;22796;TOOLONG ... Federal Court of Canada has rejected&lt;/a&gt; an application from a group representing port workers that wanted government marine security regulations declared unconstitutional . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The Canadian Maritime Workers Council , which includes unions representing B.C. dockworkers , argued that the Charter rights of waterfront employees are being violated by the screening process used to issue security passes . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The council , a coalition of unions representing 6,820 dock workers , argued that those who can&amp;rsquo ; t get the special passes that allow access to restricted zones at Canada&amp;rsquo ; s ports lose job opportunities , work hours and even merit-based promotions . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; But Justice Peter Pamel dismissed the council&amp;rsquo ; s application , noting that there are limits on the scope of the government&amp;rsquo ; s screening measures and that individual workers have the right to appeal if they are denied the passes . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; And he accepted the federal government&amp;rsquo ; s arguments that the same issues raised by the council were already rejected more than a decade ago by the Federal Court of Appeal in response to a similar challenge in B.C. by the&amp;nbsp ; International Longshore and Warehouse Union . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Pamel also agreed with the attorney general&amp;rsquo ; s position that any &amp;ldquo ; constitutional concerns are best addressed in the context of the judicial review of individual decisions . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; I dismiss the applicant&amp;rsquo ; s application , &amp;rdquo ; Pamel said in his ruling . &amp;ldquo ; That said , this does not shut the door to appropriate challenges of individual ministerial decisions in the future . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The union body had argued that the&amp;nbsp ; screening process includes background checks on workers , as well as on close associates and family members , which &amp;ldquo ; restrict a port worker&amp;rsquo ; s ability to maintain associations with other people for hobbies , church-related activities , social activities and community-related activities . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; In effect , a port worker who is associated with a convicted or suspected criminal , even in the most innocent setting , is at risk of seeing his or her clearance revoked , regardless of the port worker&amp;rsquo ; s actual relationship with the person , his or her vulnerability to influences or the probable risk he or she might pose to maritime transportation security . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Pamel noted that the security regulations , which were introduced in response to the Sept. 11 , 2001 , terrorism attacks in the U.S. , grant the federal transportation minister &amp;ldquo ; the power to issue , deny , suspend , reinstate or cancel security clearances required to perform certain functions or to have access to certain port areas . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; The minister exercises this power by considering regulatory criteria that include &amp;hellip ; consideration of a port worker&amp;rsquo ; s associations with suspected or convicted criminals . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; In the current case , &amp;ldquo ; there is no evidence before me that a union member&amp;rsquo ; s right to liberty and security has been infringed , &amp;rdquo ; Pamel said . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The case was heard in Montreal in October , with Pamel releasing his decision Jan. 30. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Postmedia has previously documented more than two dozens cases of members and associates of gangs , including the Hells Angels , working at Port of Vancouver facilities . As well , the Postmedia investigation found cases where longshore workers convicted of drug trafficking continued to work at the port . But at the time , the federal government said those identified did not have the high-security clearances needed to access restricted areas of the port . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; **41;22831;TOOLONG &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;em&gt;blog : **42;22874;TOOLONG &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; **39;22918;TOOLONG &lt;/p&gt; Kim BolanSat , 08 Feb 2020 00:21:58 **45;22959;TOOLONG ... NewsNationalVancouver couple remains stranded in quarantined Wuhanhttp : **35;23006;TOOLONG ... &lt;img src= " https : **34;23043;TOOLONG ... title= " Edward Yuan of Vancouver stands on deserted street in Wuhan . " alt= " Edward Yuan of Vancouver stands on deserted street in Wuhan . " data-portal-copyright= " Eve Xiao " **28;23079;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1201329 " /&gt; &lt;iframe width= " 100% " height= " 100% " frameborder= " 0 " allowfullscreen= " true " src= " https : **34;23109;TOOLONG ... &gt; &lt;/iframe&gt; &lt;p&gt; Edward Yuan had a difficult choice to make . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Yuan , 28 , was shortlisted for a government-chartered flight from Wuhan , China , to Canada . But because his wife , 27-year-old Eve Xiao , is a permanent resident of Canada and not a citizen , she would not be able to go with him . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Wuhan , a city of 11 million people , is the epicentre of an outbreak of a novel coronavirus that has killed almost 500 people and sickened 25,000 , and has been quarantined for almost two weeks , with no travel in or out . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; I&amp;rsquo ; d like to come home to Canada , but I can&amp;rsquo ; t leave my wife alone in China , and we are still deciding what to do at this moment , &amp;rdquo ; Yuan said in an email on Tuesday evening , less than 12 hours before he was due to be at the airport for the flight . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; In the end , he notified the Canadian government that he would not be leaving , instead deciding to wait and see if there will be a second chance to get out of Wuhan . This is despite the concerns he has about his sick mother in B.C. and the possibility of losing his job if he doesn&amp;rsquo ; t return soon . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The &lt;a href= " http : **34;23145;TOOLONG ... target= " blank " rel= " noopener " &gt;Vancouver couple left on their honeymoon&lt;/a&gt; and an extended vacation in November , spending the first month or so in Japan before heading to Wuhan to visit family . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Since the coronavirus outbreak , Yuan and Xiao have barely ventured outside . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; We have been staying in-house for 12 days now , no signs of symptoms of coronavirus so far . We still have some food left but need to get groceries soon , &amp;rdquo ; Yuan said . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Yuan said he and Xiao are hoping to leave together if there is a second flight or any other way out of Wuhan , but there is no word yet whether the Chinese government will lift the travel ban for permanent residents without children who want to leave the quarantine zone . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Xiao has lived , studied and worked in B.C. for more than eight years . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;iframe width= " 640 " height= " 360 " src= " https : **34;23181;TOOLONG ... frameborder= " 0 " allow= " accelerometer ; autoplay ; encrypted-media ; gyroscope ; picture-in-picture " **34;23217;TOOLONG &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; I believe the Canadian government would also like to help us return home as a family , and is trying to communicate with Chinese authorities regarding the permanent resident issue , &amp;rdquo ; Yuan said . &amp;ldquo ; As I have a few friends in similar situations currently stranded in Wuhan , we are all hoping to hear good news from both governments , whether about the evacuation or the removal of the travel ban . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The government-chartered flight that Yuan was supposed to take was delayed due to crosswinds that kept the plane from leaving Hanoi , Vietnam , on Thursday , and stranded people in Wuhan for another day . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; There is a narrow window given by the Chinese for flights into Wuhan and the weather conditions were such that the plane could not take off , &amp;rdquo ; Prime Minister Justin Trudeau explained as he entered a Liberal caucus meeting Wednesday morning in Ottawa . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; We understand for loved ones here in Canada with families over there that it&amp;rsquo ; s extremely difficult , but we&amp;rsquo ; re doing everything we can to get them home . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Foreign Affairs Minister Francois-Philippe Champagne said 373 Canadians have asked for help to leave Wuhan , but the plane was to carry just 211 passengers . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Champagne also said the government is still weighing its options for taking more Canadians out of Wuhan once the first flight has left , including whether it should send a second plane or can secure enough space on flights organized by the U.S. and other allies . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Upon arrival in Canada , the evacuees on the flight from Wuhan will be quarantined at Canadian Forces Base Trenton in southern Ontario for 14 days , during which time they will receive regular health assessments . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Those planning to take the flight have also been told that visits from friends and family will not be permitted , to prevent possible transmission of the virus . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Yuan believes the quarantine at Trenton for returning Canadians is a good idea . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; I&amp;rsquo ; m proud that the government decided to ( enforce ) a 14-day quarantine on the travellers as we have requested . It is very important for public health in Canada , &amp;rdquo ; he said . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The plane will stop in Vancouver to refuel on its way to Trenton . If a passenger falls sick mid-flight , they will be moved to an isolated area of the plane and , upon landing in B.C. , will be transported to a facility and isolated at Richmond Hospital . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; As the closest hospital to Vancouver International Airport , Richmond Hospital serves travellers in need of medical care , &amp;rdquo ; the B.C. Centre for Disease Control said in a statement . &amp;ldquo ; All hospitals in B.C. take steps to prepare for the possibility of a pandemic in accordance with the Ministry of Health&amp;rsquo ; s pandemic response plans . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; There have been five cases of the novel coronavirus in Canada : three in Ontario and two in B.C. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The first B.C. case , discovered late last month , was a man in his 40s who returned from a business trip in China that had him spending most of his time in Wuhan . He voluntarily isolated himself at home and is recovering . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; On Tuesday , &lt;a href= " https : **33;23253;TOOLONG ... target= " blank " rel= " noopener " &gt;a presumptive case&lt;/a&gt; was identified in the Vancouver Coastal Health region . A woman in her 50s who had family visiting from Hubei province , of which Wuhan is the capital , is in quarantine in her home . Others in her household are being tested . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Local public health officials said the case tested positive , but they are awaiting official confirmation from the National Microbiology Lab in Winnipeg . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Although the risk to Canadians and British Columbians remains low , there is still &lt;a href= " https : **33;23288;TOOLONG ... target= " blank " rel= " noopener " &gt;concern in the general public&lt;/a&gt; . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Hand sanitizer supplies were running low in many Metro Vancouver drug and grocery stores . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Whole Foods markets ran out over the weekend and are not expecting to receive new shipments until late February . At least half a dozen Real Canadian Superstore locations had also run out of hand sanitizer in the past week , but were expecting stock in the coming days . London Drugs just received another shipment , but was expecting it to sell fast . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Stores also had trouble keeping masks and disposable gloves in stock . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;em&gt; -- with files from Canadian Press and Nick Eagland&lt;/em&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " **31;23323;TOOLONG " **38;23356;TOOLONG &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " http : //twitter.com/jensaltman " target= " blank " rel= " noopener " **31;23396;TOOLONG ... &lt;/p&gt; &lt;h2 class= " cat-head short " **40;23429;TOOLONG ... &lt;ul class= " relatedlinks " &gt; &lt;li&gt;&lt;a href= " https : **33;23471;TOOLONG ... case of coronavirus reported in Metro Vancouver **25;23506;TOOLONG &lt;li&gt;&lt;a href= " https : **33;23533;TOOLONG ... of Canadians taking precautions against coronavirus : **25;23568;TOOLONG &lt;li&gt;&lt;a href= " http : **34;23595;TOOLONG ... couple describes life trapped in Wuhan as B.C. reports first case of coronavirus **25;23631;TOOLONG &lt;/ul&gt; Jennifer SaltmanThu , 06 Feb 2020 20:10:28 **45;23658;TOOLONG ... NewsNationalWorldDouglas Todd : Think B.C. has a lot of housing regulations ? You ai n't seen nothinghttp : **33;23705;TOOLONG ... &lt;img src= " https : **34;23740;TOOLONG ... title= " Berlin has frozen rents for five years . " alt= " Berlin has frozen rents for five years . " data-portal-copyright= " Fabrizio Bensch " **28;23776;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1208328 " /&gt; &lt;iframe width= " 100% " height= " 100% " frameborder= " 0 " allowfullscreen= " true " src= " https : **34;23806;TOOLONG ... &gt; &lt;/iframe&gt; &lt;p&gt; The world&amp;rsquo ; s most populous countries , India and China , don&amp;rsquo ; t allow any non-resident foreign nationals to buy their real estate . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The city of Berlin just moved to protect 1.5 million tenants by freezing most rents for five years . The Netherlands is cutting back on sudden reno-victions by making sure 70 per cent of an apartment building&amp;rsquo ; s tenants agree before landlords makes improvements . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; In Canada , it&amp;rsquo ; s British Columbia that arguably has the most rigorous housing restrictions , with a 20 per cent foreign-buyers tax and a speculation-and-vacancy tax . But such measures are minor compared to the astonishing rules concocted by other public officials to deflate housing bubbles and soften rent shocks . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Asian countries are much &lt;a href= " https : **33;23842;TOOLONG ... target= " blank " rel= " noopener " &gt;less open than Western nations to allowing foreign nationals to affect their property values&lt;/a&gt; by investing in their land . And many European countries have hard caps on rents , as well as direct financial top-ups to tenants paying more than 30 per cent of their income for housing . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; There seems no limit to legislators&amp;rsquo ; housing creativity . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Many of the planet&amp;rsquo ; s desirable cities are being hit by housing pressures similar to those squeezing Vancouver , Victoria and Toronto . Faced with skyrocketing prices and rents , often combined with stagnant local wages , governments across the political spectrum realize they&amp;rsquo ; re called to devise a range of policies to calm prices and guard vulnerable tenants . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Such regulations and taxes can counter the factors driving up prices , according to Knight Frank , a consultancy . Soaring valuations are caused , Knight Frank says , by a sharp global rise in the number of millionaires , increasingly convenient flights from Asia to the West , record low interest rates and urban infrastructure megaprojects , such as Vancouver&amp;rsquo ; s proposed subway to UBC. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Developers and their often-close political allies welcome the economic and population growth &amp;mdash ; and customarily claim the only way to create more reasonably priced housing ( and hike their profits ) is to increase density and reduce so-called red tape . But many economists say it&amp;rsquo ; s impossible to build one&amp;rsquo ; s way to affordability. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; Rather counter-intuitively , building more houses is not the right prescription . House prices won&amp;rsquo ; t fall until the tide of cash flowing into the market abates . &amp;hellip ; The best way to do this is through the tax system , &amp;rdquo ; says &lt;a href= " https : **33;23877;TOOLONG ... target= " blank " rel= " noopener " &gt;Ann Pettifor&lt;/a&gt; , director of Policy Research in Macroeconomics . Without stopping money funnelling into expensive areas , Pettifor says the market will simply absorb it and prices will keep rising . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;h2 class= " cat-head short " **40;23912;TOOLONG ... &lt;ul class= " relatedlinks " &gt; &lt;li&gt;&lt;a href= " https : **33;23954;TOOLONG ... Todd : Once abhorred , foreign buyers tax now mainstream in **27;23989;TOOLONG &lt;li&gt;&lt;a href= " https : **33;24018;TOOLONG ... Todd : A tale of two ' desirable ' cities : Barcelona and **30;24053;TOOLONG &lt;li&gt;&lt;a href= " https : **33;24085;TOOLONG ... Canada 's slipping **32;24120;TOOLONG &lt;/ul&gt; &lt;p&gt; That&amp;rsquo ; s why restrictions abound elsewhere on foreign investment in real-estate . The biggest sources of immigrants to Canada &amp;mdash ; China , India and the Philippines &amp;mdash ; all impose various bans on non-resident buyers . That means millions of new Canadians are already familiar with &lt;a href= " https : **33;24154;TOOLONG ... target= " blank " rel= " noopener " &gt; housing rules that protect local sovereignty&lt;/a&gt; . Similar curbs on non-resident ownership are in place in Indonesia , Thailand , Singapore , Malaysia and Hong Kong . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Like in Canada , many of Australia&amp;rsquo ; s big cities are also super-attractive to foreign buyers , typically from Asia . But , unlike Canada , Australia keeps track by requiring every foreign national who wants to buy a dwelling to register . And , to limit the depletion of its older , more affordable housing stock , Australia innovatively restricts offshore residents to buying only new dwellings . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The policy variations go on and on . In Denmark , foreign nationals from outside Europe can not buy real estate unless they prove they are permanent residents and will live full-time in the dwelling . Even European Union citizens can not buy summer homes on Denmark&amp;rsquo ; s sought-after coast . Though large European countries like France and Germany are fairly open , small Switzerland has erected more barriers than Denmark . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Even in Canada , which champions the unfettered global movement of capital and humans more than almost any other nation , Liberal Prime Minister Justin Trudeau finally promised in the fall election campaign to follow the lead of B.C. and impose a one-per-cent tax on the assessed values of homes owned by non-resident non-Canadians . The NDP&amp;rsquo ; s Jagmeet Singh called for a national 15-per-cent foreign-buyers tax . &lt;a href= " https : **33;24189;TOOLONG ... target= " blank " rel= " noopener " &gt;We 'll see if they follow through . &lt;/a&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; What&amp;rsquo ; s being done for tenants ? &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Given the increasingly often-outrageous cost of home ownership , more people now rent in North America than before . So some governments are moving harder to counter gouging. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Like Vancouver , popular Berlin is growing by almost 40,000 newcomers a year . So its average rents have more than doubled in a decade . Politicians are going far to protect the city&amp;rsquo ; s tenants . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Berlin&amp;rsquo ; s government has just &lt;a href= " https : **33;24224;TOOLONG ... target= " blank " rel= " noopener " &gt;frozen most rents for five years , while capping them at about US$1 a square foot&lt;/a&gt; . The German capital has also become the first in the world to say landlords can not charge rents higher than what the previous tenant paid . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; In response to justified fears that Berlin&amp;rsquo ; s laws will kill any incentive to construct new purpose-built rental stock , the government decided any building that went up after 2014 will be exempt and rents will eventually be allowed to rise by 1.3 per cent a year . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Spain and the Netherlands also have nationwide rent-control measures , and have capped rents to the inflation rate . And they&amp;rsquo ; re not alone . Even the staunchly free-enterprise U.S. is home to five states with broad rent-regulation measures ; California , New York , New Jersey , Maryland and Oregon . Some Canadian cities are similar . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; But they&amp;rsquo ; re not as groundbreaking on behalf of renters as a host of other jurisdictions . Spain has a five-year-minimum on rent contracts , to guard against hikes . And when sought-after Barcelona began being flooded by 20 million tourists a year , losing more than 20,000 rental units to Airbnb , &lt;a href= " https : **33;24259;TOOLONG ... target= " blank " rel= " noopener " &gt;city politicians cracked down&lt;/a&gt; . So have many other cities . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The Netherlands not only got tough on landlords who crank up rents in the name of renovating , Amsterdam has found a way to give high priority to families for mid-priced rentals and the national government promises to provide free lawyers to tenants who need to sue landlords . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; This is not to mention the dramatic efforts going into social housing . Germany , for example , puts $2 billion a year into housing projects . In lovely Vienna , Austria , 62 per cent of citizens are in social housing ( compared to one per cent in the U.S. ) . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; There is truly no shortage of imaginative ideas for aiding people squeezed too hard by the housing market . Each idea needs to fit its context . But in most cases the only thing stopping them becoming reality is the social and political will . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;24294;TOOLONG ... title= " Berlin has frozen rents for five years . " alt= " Berlin has frozen rents for five years . " data-portal-copyright= " Fabrizio Bensch " **28;24330;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1208328 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " **26;24360;TOOLONG " **33;24388;TOOLONG &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " http : **25;24423;TOOLONG " &gt;twit ... &lt;/p&gt; Douglas ToddThu , 06 Feb 2020 17:39:42 **45;24450;TOOLONG ... &amp;amp ; SellingColumnistsLocal **25;24497;TOOLONG BlogsWorldDaphne Bramham : Urgent overhaul of long-term care funding and oversight neededhttp : **34;24524;TOOLONG ... &lt;img src= " https : **34;24560;TOOLONG ... title= " In 2017-18 , for-profit operators failed to deliver 207,000 hours of care for which the B.C. government paid them . " alt= " In 2017-18 , for-profit operators failed to deliver 207,000 hours of care for which the B.C. government paid them . " data-portal-copyright= " AlexRaths " **28;24596;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1206871 " /&gt; &lt;iframe width= " 100% " height= " 100% " frameborder= " 0 " allowfullscreen= " true " src= " https : **32;24626;TOOLONG ... &gt; &lt;/iframe&gt; &lt;p&gt; Some corporate-owned , long-term care homes in B.C. are getting money for nothing , while not-for-profits may be getting less than they need for their services to be sustainable . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; But the bottom line is that the losers are both vulnerable seniors and taxpayers who are footing the $1.3-billion annual bill . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; To be clear , it may be unconscionable , but for-profit operators who run a third of all the long-term homes and beds in B.C. are not breaking any rules . Just as it&amp;rsquo ; s fair to assume that not-for-profits , who account for another third of the total , aren&amp;rsquo ; t deliberately leaving money on the table . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The problem is that the system is broken . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;24660;TOOLONG ... title= " B.C. Seniors Advocate Isobel Mackenzie ( Richard Lam/PNG ) " alt= " B.C. Seniors Advocate Isobel Mackenzie ( Richard Lam/PNG ) " data-portal-copyright= " " **28;24696;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1207232 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; Patients and their families have been complaining for years . On Tuesday , B.C. Seniors Advocate Isobel Mackenzie clearly set out the evidence in her report , &lt;a href= " https : **33;24726;TOOLONG ... target= " blank " rel= " noopener " &gt;A Billion Reasons to Care&lt;/a&gt; , which comes within a few months of Island Health taking over three privately operated homes . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Mackenzie&amp;rsquo ; s funding review is a scorching indictment of the government&amp;rsquo ; s failure to properly manage one of the largest contracting relationships it has with service providers . To some extent , Mackenzie said , the government is handing long-term care operators blank cheques . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; She said the rules haven&amp;rsquo ; t kept pace with the fundamental shift in government procurement policy that began 20 years ago when long-term care was contracted out to private operators who were then allowed to opt out of the Health Employers Association of B.C. , which once bargained on behalf of all publicly funded , health-care employers . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Rules are non-existent , vague or not uniformly applied . Direct care hours , for example , aren&amp;rsquo ; t necessarily separated out from the hours that care aides spend on food service or housekeeping . Financial monitoring is scant with no requirement for detailed or audited reports . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; In 2017-18 , for-profit operators failed to deliver 207,000 hours of care for which the B.C. government paid them . Were they fined ? No . They got to keep the money . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Meantime , not-for-profit operators delivered 80,000 hours of care more than they were contracted to provide . Those extra hours were paid for either by lower costs in another area or by other funding sources . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; It is true that all operators face a staffing crisis that Mackenzie describes as being of epidemic proportions , with nearly 90 per cent of care homes not able to meet minimum staffing guidelines . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; But it&amp;rsquo ; s partially self-inflicted . For-profit operators&amp;rsquo ; wage costs for each hour of direct care is lower across all classifications than the costs at not-for-profits and the homes run directly by health authorities . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Some for-profits are paying care aides , who provide two-thirds of the care , nearly a third less than the industry standard , which works out to $6.63 an hour . Part of the difference is that for-profit operators are more likely to hire part-time rather than full-time workers , which eliminates the need to pay benefits . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Raise the salaries , says Mackenzie , and workers will follow the money . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The government and health authorities should also follow the money . Rather than setting a minimum wage or requiring that all operators pay the industry standard , Mackenzie says to simply end the incentive to not deliver the care . If operators didn&amp;rsquo ; t deliver 207,000 hours of care , they should have been required to give that money back . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; But there are no penalties in any of the contracts . Don&amp;rsquo ; t deliver and , ka-ching ! , the bottom line suddenly looks a lot better as taxpayers&amp;rsquo ; money transforms into shareholder profits . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Disturbingly , Mackenzie found that contracts varied both between and within health authorities . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; All spoke to delivery of care , &amp;rdquo ; she said Tuesday . &amp;ldquo ; But none specified the type of care . None outlined any legal requirements to provide the care . And none had explicit penalties for non-compliance. &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Every health authority also had different reporting systems . Different ones allowed expenses to be claimed differently . There were also anomalies between the profit and non-profit operators , including for-profits having surpluses 12 times higher and profits three times higher . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The health authorities allowed for-profit operators to claim building expenses at 20 per cent of their revenues compared to the **25;24761;TOOLONG ; nine per cent . They also allowed mortgage interest rates considerably higher than market rates , double the depreciation rates , unexplained lump-sum payments to contractors working for affiliated companies , unspecified management fees in addition to administrative expenses that are higher than non-profits. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; One concern Mackenzie has about non-profits claiming lower building expenses is that they will not be able to upgrade their facilities as they age , meet any new accessibility requirements that may be required , or expand to meet the tsunami of demand from an aging population . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; It all needs to change and change quickly before even more public money is funnelled into corporate profits at the expense of vulnerable seniors who aren&amp;rsquo ; t getting the care they need and deserve . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " **29;24788;TOOLONG " **36;24819;TOOLONG &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Twitter : @bramhamdaphne &lt;/p&gt; Daphne BramhamWed , 05 Feb 2020 17:40:58 **45;24857;TOOLONG ...
