NewsNationalDaphne Bramham : Allowing paroled murderers to visit brothels is n't just stupid , it 's illegal and deadlyhttp : **34;25409;TOOLONG ... &lt;img src= " https : **34;25445;TOOLONG ... title= " Maryl ? ne L ? vesque was killed in a Sainte-Foy hotel room Jan. 22 . Parolee Eustachio Gallese was arrested in connection with the death . Photo : Facebook " alt= " Maryl ? ne L ? vesque was killed in a Sainte-Foy hotel room Jan. 22 . Parolee Eustachio Gallese was arrested in connection with the death . Photo : Facebook " data-portal-copyright= " " **28;25481;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1204666 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; It is unbelievable that men who have killed their intimate partners would not only be allowed , but encouraged , to go to brothels while they are on parole and out on day passes . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; But that&amp;rsquo ; s exactly what Canada&amp;rsquo ; s parole board agreed to last September for Eustachio Gallese , 51 , who had brutally murdered his wife in 2004. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; He had sexual needs , his caseworker told the board . But because Gallese wasn&amp;rsquo ; t deemed ready to have relationships with a woman , buying sexual services was the solution to satisfying his &amp;ldquo ; sexual needs . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; It&amp;rsquo ; s a solution that blatantly disregards the fact that buying sexual services is a criminal offence , as is operating a brothel . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; But , more tragically , the order was approved with cruel disregard for the unsuspecting women on whom Gallese was set loose . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; On Jan. 23 this year , Gallese turned himself in to Quebec City police and pointed them to the hotel room where they found the body of 22-year-old Maryl&amp;egrave ; ne L&amp;eacute ; vesque . He&amp;rsquo ; s since been charged with murder . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; L&amp;eacute ; vesque had defensive wounds and had been stabbed , according to Le Journal de Quebec . The newspaper also reported that L&amp;eacute ; vesque had worked in a massage parlour and that Gallese was a regular customer , and had bought her gifts including a television for Christmas . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;25511;TOOLONG ... title= " Maryl ? ne L ? vesque was killed in a Sainte-Foy hotel room Jan. 22 . Parolee Eustachio Gallese was arrested in connection with the death . Photo : Facebook " alt= " Maryl ? ne L ? vesque was killed in a Sainte-Foy hotel room Jan. 22 . Parolee Eustachio Gallese was arrested in connection with the death . Photo : Facebook " data-portal-copyright= " " **28;25547;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1204673 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; But both the Montreal Gazette and Le Soleil reported that Gallese had been banned from the brothel because he had been violent with several others who worked there . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; L&amp;eacute ; vesque made the mistake of meeting him at a hotel . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; In 1997 , Gallese was convicted for conjugal violence . Seven years later , Gallese murdered his 32-year-old partner , Chantal Desch&amp;ecirc ; nes , beating her first with a hammer and then repeatedly stabbing her . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; He was sentenced in 2006 to life in prison with no chance of parole for 15 years , and deemed at high risk of committing violence against a partner a year later . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; But , at some point , that risk evaluation was revised to moderate and in 2016 &amp;mdash ; five years before he was due for parole &amp;mdash ; Gallese was allowed supervised outings . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Last March , he was released to a halfway house . The parole board denied him full parole last September at a hearing . A written summary of the decision indicates that parole board members were caught off guard when Gallese discussed his interactions with women over the previous six months . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; During the hearing , your parole officer underlined a strategy that was developed with the goal that would allow you to meet women in order to address your sexual needs , &amp;rdquo ; it says . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; It went on to say : &amp;ldquo ; The hearing allowed us to realize you managed , and this with the approval of your case-management team , relations with women that the board considers inappropriate . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; It was a strategy , the board noted , that &amp;ldquo ; paradoxically constitutes a worrying and significant risk factor . &amp;rdquo ; It ordered a re-examination of the terms in six months . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Two months shy of that re-evaluation , L&amp;eacute ; vesque is dead . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; How many other parolees , if any , have similar orders in place is not clear . The Parole Board of Canada did not respond to questions before this column&amp;rsquo ; s deadline . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; But even if it is a singular case , the decision in Gallese&amp;rsquo ; s case reflects some deeply disturbing notions . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The first is that men &amp;mdash ; even violent criminals &amp;mdash ; have a right to satiate their sexual appetites with another person . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The second is the perverse idea that if a violent man is incapable or not ready to form a healthy relationship with another person , it&amp;rsquo ; s OK for him to engage in unhealthy relationships where , as the buyer , he has power over the seller . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Finally , putting the sexual needs of a violent criminal ahead of the safety of other Canadians , including those who do sex work , suggests a grotesque hierarchy that is an affront to the constitutional and moral ideals of equality . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Two former parole board members have posited that the reason Gallese was allowed to visit massage parlours is that 14 of Quebec&amp;rsquo ; s parole board members have less than three years&amp;rsquo ; experience and several have never dealt with cases involving dangerous offenders . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; They suggest that more experienced board members might have challenged the caseworker&amp;rsquo ; s plan . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; That may be so . But it does raise questions about what qualifications these government-appointed board members have . One might expect that at the very least they are reasonable people who are reasonably well-informed. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; But what reasonable person would knowingly place a violent offender in an illegal brothel to commit the criminal offence of paying for sex ? &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; And it doesn&amp;rsquo ; t seem unreasonable that parole board caseworkers , as well as inexperienced parole board members , ought to know at least something about the murdered and missing Indigenous women , or the epidemic of sexual violence that translates into one death every 36 hours , or the fact that only five per cent of sexual assaults are ever reported , partly because all but one in five of those will be dismissed as unfounded by police . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Public Safety Minister Bill Blair ordered an investigation by Corrections Canada and the parole board to determine the circumstances of Gallese&amp;rsquo ; s release and provide recommendations for change . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; We want to be clear that the use of sexual services is not a practice we support in the case management of offenders , &amp;rdquo ; Esther Mailhot , Corrections Canada&amp;rsquo ; s communications adviser , said in an emailed response . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Corrections is taking Gallese&amp;rsquo ; s case &amp;ldquo ; very seriously&amp;rdquo ; and &amp;ldquo ; any recommendations &amp;hellip ; will be reviewed and implemented to prevent similar incidents from occurring in the future . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Why this tragedy happened is simple : The people responsible for keeping the public safe failed . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The more complicated question is , how did the institutions and the culture at both Corrections Canada and the parole board allow those individuals to fail so spectacularly ? Email &lt;a href= " **28;25735;TOOLONG " **35;25765;TOOLONG &lt;/em&gt; &lt;/p&gt; Daphne BramhamSat , 01 Feb 2020 00:46:30 **45;25802;TOOLONG ... **37;25849;TOOLONG Bramham : Before planes become Noah 's ark , some regulation is neededhttp : **34;25888;TOOLONG ... &lt;img src= " https : **34;25924;TOOLONG ... title= " A long list of bizarre and strange animals are queuing for flights alongside their owners . " alt= " A long list of bizarre and strange animals are queuing for flights alongside their owners . " data-portal-copyright= " Photo Illustration/Getty Images/iStockphoto " **28;25960;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1198455 " /&gt; &lt;iframe width= " 100% " height= " 100% " frameborder= " 0 " allowfullscreen= " true " src= " https : **34;25990;TOOLONG ... &gt; &lt;/iframe&gt; &lt;p&gt; Images of a &amp;ldquo ; comfort turkey , &amp;rdquo ; a duck with shoes and a kangaroo on airline flights have all gone viral on the internet . So , too , did the photo of a pheasant at Newark airport and a story about Wally , an emotional support alligator . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Beyond that , who hasn&amp;rsquo ; t heard a story or two about people circumventing no-pet rules with therapists&amp;rsquo ; letters bought online along with a cute little coats for their dogs emblazoned with &amp;ldquo ; emotional support animal&amp;rdquo ; or ESA . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; ESAs are not like service dogs . Service dogs are specially trained to do a wide range of tasks , such as providing assistance to people who are blind and acting as an early alert system to people with epilepsy. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The fact is there&amp;rsquo ; s no agreed-upon definition of emotional support animals , which is why there&amp;rsquo ; s a Noah&amp;rsquo ; s ark of animals queuing for flights alongside their people . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Westjet has felt it necessary to spell out on its website what animals are banned from flying in the passenger cabin . &amp;nbsp ; The list is both bizarre and strangely delightful ( unless they were to show up in the seat next to me ) . &amp;nbsp ; It includes : animals with tusks , horns , or hoofs , amphibians , ferrets , goats , hedgehogs , reptiles , rodents , snakes , spiders and sugar gliders ( a nocturnal gliding possum ) . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Air Canada has kept it simple : Service dogs only . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; But who qualifies for an ESA , what kind of animal is acceptable and what training it needs is so opaque that the Canadian Transportation Authority is doing a public consultation to see whether more or better regulations are required . Anyone who wants to voice an opinion has until Feb. 7 to send email to &lt;a href= " **34;26026;TOOLONG " **41;26062;TOOLONG . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Meantime , the &lt;a href= " https : **33;26105;TOOLONG ... target= " blank " rel= " noopener " &gt;U.S . Department of Transport is proposing&lt;/a&gt; to restrict flying to service dogs with specialized training who help people with disabilities , after receiving 3,065 complaints from passengers in 2018 ( up from 719 in 2013 ) about animals defecating , urinating and biting on flights . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; An ESA is an example of a well-intended idea that has metastasized and developed into a world of nonsense , &amp;rdquo ; according to Jeffrey Younggren , a forensic psychologist and clinical professor at the University of New Mexico&amp;rsquo ; s department of psychiatry and behavioural sciences . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; He was lead author on a 2019 &lt;a href= " https : **28;26140;TOOLONG ? doi= ... target= " blank " rel= " noopener " &gt;paper&lt;/a&gt; published by the American Psychological Association that looked at the ethical challenges of certifying emotional support animals . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Among their recommendations was more research on effects of ESAs on patients , which echoed a 2018 British &lt;a href= " https : **33;26170;TOOLONG ... target= " blank " rel= " noopener " &gt;review of studies&lt;/a&gt; on emotional support animals published by BMC Psychiatry that found quantitative evidence relating to the benefits of pets to be mixed and in need of further study . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Younggren and his colleagues said before prescribing ESAs , therapists must not only diagnose the person as psychologically disabled with significant impairment in functioning , but certify that the animals are trained and their presence helps the condition . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; That means , Younggren wrote , &amp;ldquo ; You have to see the person with the animal . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; But online there&amp;rsquo ; s none of that . One company&amp;rsquo ; s website says : &amp;ldquo ; If you live in Alberta , Ontario , British Columbia or Newfoundland and Labrador , you can get your ESA Letter from the comfort of your Home for Less than USD 1/Day ! &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; After a quick , click-through questionnaire about how often you&amp;rsquo ; ve felt sad , angry , depressed , frightened , paranoid or suicidal in the last couple of weeks , a phone conversation with a therapist &amp;hellip ; and , of course , paying the money &amp;hellip ; then , you and your pet are good to go . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; To make it more official looking , the company also offers a choice of four different styles of dog coats in a range of fabrics for your &amp;lsquo ; certified&amp;rsquo ; ESA dog . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; For more choices , there&amp;rsquo ; s Amazon where you get everything from ESA harnesses and leashes to a medallion for $10.25 that claims ( erroneously ) that the emotional support animal is protected by federal law . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Neither Canada nor the United States has federal laws regarding ESAs . What exists is a patchwork with rules that may cover airlines , but could be different from laws regulating airports or even restaurants in airports . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; There are also other provincial or state laws that apply . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; In B.C. , for example , the only exemptions to no-pet rules in condominiums and rental properties are those covered by the Guide Dog Service Animal Act . But a 2016 B.C. Human Rights Tribunal ruling suggested that under the Human Rights Code , an exemption for uncertified pets may be required if not having that pet could put the individual at significant risk . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; There&amp;rsquo ; s no doubt Canadians and Americans love their pets almost to distraction . In 2018 alone , Canadians spent $8 billion on pets , which was nearly double the amount only three years earlier . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Where once dogs and cats might have been sent to kennels , they&amp;rsquo ; ve morphed into pet spas where part of the service is sending daily emails and videos to their vacationing &amp;lsquo ; parents . &amp;rsquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; But while service dogs have long proven their worth as constant companions , there&amp;rsquo ; s still some way to go before emotional support animals and their owners get the same kind of consideration . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Because , while it may all be part of a developing country adventure to share space on buses with chickens and goats taking their final trip to market , having a turkey as a flight companion or as a next door neighbour is a different thing altogether . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;26205;TOOLONG ... title= " A long list of bizarre and strange animals are queuing for flights alongside their owners . Mulgrew : Canada 's growing Indigenous prison population ' a national travesty'http : **31;26434;TOOLONG ... &lt;img src= " https : **34;26467;TOOLONG ... title= " Since April 2010 the Indigenous inmates have increased by 43.4 per cent ( or 1,265 ) , whereas the non-Indigenous incarcerated population has declined by 13.7 per cent ( or 1,549 ) , according to the office of the Correctional Investigator of Canada . " data-portal-copyright= " Rattankun Thongbun " **28;26503;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1202572 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; After 48 years of criminal defence work and advocacy for prisoners&amp;rsquo ; rights , Abbotsford lawyer John Conroy was staggered at the new numbers revealing the swelling tide of Indigenous people being kept locked up . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; I started practising in 1972 and I&amp;rsquo ; m 72 years old , &amp;rdquo ; he said . &amp;ldquo ; How can things be worse ? But they are . Here we are in 2020 and it&amp;rsquo ; s worse than ever . I do not understand with the amount of attention and amount of information we have that we&amp;rsquo ; re still in this situation . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The appalling statistics suggest a modern , racially divided Bedlam. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Conroy pointed out &amp;ldquo ; 73 per cent of the men and 80 per cent of the women have the criteria for qualifying for a current mental disorder , 29 require follow-up with mental health services , 25 per cent have some kind of cognitive deficit , 10 to 23 per cent fetal alcohol spectrum disorder ( FASD ) , acquired brain injuries &amp;hellip ; it goes on and on and on . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Indigenous people constitute less than five per cent of the population yet now they account for one-in-three of the men in jail , more than four of 10 incarcerated women . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; On the Prairies , the numbers are worse &amp;mdash ; more than seven out of 10 are Aboriginal . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; In 2017-18 , Aboriginal adults represented three-quarters of admissions to custody in Manitoba ( 75 per cent ) and Saskatchewan ( 74 per cent ) . Those provinces also have the highest proportion of Aboriginals , 15 per cent and 14 per cent , respectively . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Compared to 2007-08 , admissions of Aboriginal males increased the most in B.C. ( 83 per cent ) , from 3,932 to 7,181. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Decades of well-meaning Indigenous legal policies have produced the opposite of what was intended &amp;mdash ; increasing incarceration riven by racism &amp;mdash ; a visible disconnect between years of compassionate rhetoric and hard-hearted practice . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;26533;TOOLONG ... title= " Abbotsford lawyer John Conroy : ' There are judges who are sending these people to prison and they seem to be disproportionately sending them to prison compared with non-Aboriginal people . ' " alt= " Abbotsford lawyer John Conroy : ' There are judges who are sending these people to prison and they seem to be disproportionately sending them to prison compared with non-Aboriginal people . ' " data-portal-copyright= " Handout " **28;26569;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 417321 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; Having an Indigenous federal attorney general didn&amp;rsquo ; t help &amp;mdash ; the numbers went up on Jody **25;26599;TOOLONG ; s watch as well . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The shocking report by the country&amp;rsquo ; s correctional investigator , Dr. Ivan Zinger , asserts that the number and proportion of imprisoned Indigenous individuals has reached historic highs . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Providing independent oversight of the Correctional Service of Canada , with some 19,000 full-time employees and 53 institutions , Zinger says Indigenous custody rates have accelerated ! &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; The Indigenization of Canada&amp;rsquo ; s prison population is nothing short of a national travesty , &amp;rdquo ; Zinger added . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Since April 2010 the Indigenous inmates have increased by 43.4 per cent ( or 1,265 ) , whereas the non-Indigenous incarcerated population has declined by 13.7 per cent ( or 1,549 ) . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; There are judges who are sending these people to prison and they seem to be disproportionately sending them to prison compared with non-Aboriginal people , &amp;rdquo ; Conroy noted . &amp;ldquo ; Why are so many being sentenced to prison ? &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Year after year , Zinger has documented that Indigenous inmates are disproportionately classified and placed in maximum security institutions , over-represented in use of force and self-injurious incidents , and historically were more likely to be placed and held longer in solitary . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Indigenous offenders serve a higher proportion of their sentence behind bars before being granted parole . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Worse , a national recidivism study shows it doesn&amp;rsquo ; t help &amp;mdash ; Indigenous offenders reoffend at much higher levels , as high as 70 per cent on the Prairies. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; When you look at the cases coming down the pipe , it isn&amp;rsquo ; t hopeful . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;h2 class= " cat-head short " **40;26626;TOOLONG ... &lt;ul class= " relatedlinks " &gt; &lt;li&gt;&lt;a href= " https : **33;26668;TOOLONG ... Mulgrew : B.C. Law Society injunction ignores access-to-justice **27;26703;TOOLONG &lt;li&gt;&lt;a href= " https : **33;26732;TOOLONG ... Mulgrew : High court ends decades-old water **28;26767;TOOLONG &lt;li&gt;&lt;a href= " https : **33;26797;TOOLONG ... Mulgrew : Coverage not denial needed in medicare **27;26832;TOOLONG &lt;/ul&gt; &lt;p&gt; Numbers from the Legal Services Society of B.C. indicate Indigenous people account for about one-in-three legal aid criminal cases and about one in four in family cases before the courts . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; For some reason , in spite of all the hopeful and aspirational rhetoric , legal stakeholders refuse to recognize the reality of Indigenous Canada or the circumstances in which many Aboriginal people live . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; When you read the facts of the case , you can&amp;rsquo ; t help but shake your head and say this guy never had a chance , &amp;rdquo ; Conroy said . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;span class= " s1 " &gt;A fundamental problem is that few native communities have the resources , the infrastructure or the capacity to handle violent offenders , so it 's very difficult for them to make a claim in court that they should n't be jailed or argue for early release . &lt;/span&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; As a result , more and more Aboriginal men are &amp;ldquo ; banished&amp;rdquo ; across Canada &amp;mdash ; a medieval punishment legally meted out by Indigenous communities to keep away members it can not control . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;span class= " s1 " &gt;Similarly , detailed accounts ordered by the Supreme Court of Canada on the effects of colonialism and the social-economic circumstances of an Aboriginal offender -- so-called Gladue Reports -- are seen by some Indigenous advocates as valuable tools to assist judges in crafting appropriate sentences but in the prison bureaucracy they can be detrimental . &lt;/span&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;span class= " s1 " &gt;A report showing a history or prevalence of violence can cause a minor offender to be sent to a maximum instead of medium institution . &lt;/span&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; Over my 25 years dealing with prison law , I have seen a big change from focus on helping a prisoner rehabilitate to managing potential PR problems , &amp;rdquo ; veteran Vancouver defence lawyer Donna Turko said . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;span class= " s1 " &gt;Various concerns are in play , she explained -- judges ca n't be too easy on violent offenders as native communities need protection , too , &lt;/span&gt; &lt;span class= " s1 " &gt;sentencing circles and diversion-style programs are scarce and &lt;/span&gt; &lt;span class= " s1 " &gt;parole boards seem increasingly afraid of public opinion and reluctant to release offenders . &lt;/span&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;26861;TOOLONG ... title= " Vancouver defence lawyer Donna Turko : ' Over my 25 years dealing with prison law , I have seen a big change from focus on helping a prisoner rehabilitate to managing potential PR problems . ' " alt= " Vancouver defence lawyer Donna Turko : ' Over my 25 years dealing with prison law , I have seen a big change from focus on helping a prisoner rehabilitate to managing potential PR problems . ' " data-portal-copyright= " Arlen Redekop " **28;26897;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1004722 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; Mostly , though , a shadow has always fallen between the rhetoric about and the reality of Aboriginal crime . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Consider a case in central B.C. involving an older native man who endured residential school and became a dysfunctional alcoholic living off the land . One day , he got very , very drunk and shot a woman . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; He spent months in pre-trial custody , went in-custody alcohol treatment , took counselling training and , while out on bail for more than two years , acted as a counsellor in his community . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; But he is facing a mandatory minimum sentence of four years and the Crown wants six years in a federal pen . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Instead , the defence wants a provincial sentence of less than two years with an additional conditional term in the community &amp;mdash ; that way he would be incarcerated close to his family , not shipped somewhere across the country . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Which will have a better outcome for the individual , which for the community ? &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; If your aim is to punish and exact a measure of vengeance , a six-year term makes some sense &amp;mdash ; but if it&amp;rsquo ; s long-term healthier citizens and communities , well &amp;hellip ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; Bold and urgent&amp;rdquo ; action is needed , Zinger said . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; People such as Conroy have been ardently arguing that since the 1970s . It obviously hasn&amp;rsquo ; t mattered . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " **29;26927;TOOLONG " target= " blank " rel= " noopener " **36;26958;TOOLONG &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " http : //twitter.com/ianmulgrew " target= " blank " rel= " noopener " **31;26996;TOOLONG ... &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;em&gt;&lt;a href= " **27;27029;TOOLONG " &gt;CLICK HERE&lt;/a&gt; to report a typo. &lt;/em&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;em&gt;Is there more to this story ? Bramham : Expert 's critical assessment of Canada 's response to gender violence has n't changed over the yearshttp : **35;27172;TOOLONG ... &lt;img src= " https : **34;27209;TOOLONG ... title= " There have been improvements , but also steps backward , in Canada 's response to domestic violence in the past 17 years " alt= " There have been improvements , but also steps backward , in Canada 's response to domestic violence in the past 17 years " data-portal-copyright= " Getty Images/Cultura RF " **28;27245;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1221055 " /&gt; &lt;iframe width= " 100% " height= " 100% " frameborder= " 0 " allowfullscreen= " true " src= " https : **32;27275;TOOLONG ... &gt; &lt;/iframe&gt; &lt;p&gt; Inconsistencies in how police respond to complaints , complainants mistreated by police , social stigma , poverty and lack of services &amp;mdash ; combined they make battered women fear even reporting abuse . And they are all reasons why an American judge granted a Canadian woman asylum under the UN Torture Convention 12 years ago . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Lisa Rupert&amp;rsquo ; s critical assessment of how Canadian women who experience violence , coercion and sexual slavery are treated was pivotal to that judge&amp;rsquo ; s decision . &amp;nbsp ; In the intervening years , little has caused the YWCA&amp;rsquo ; s vice-president of housing services and violence prevention to change her assessment . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; People understand trauma more , now . So , you might get better service from police and might get better service more generally , &amp;rdquo ; she said . &amp;ldquo ; People will understand things like maybe you won&amp;rsquo ; t come to your meetings on time or maybe you&amp;rsquo ; ll be triggered by certain things . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Police are better trained and some , like the Vancouver Police Department , have specialized units . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; I can&amp;rsquo ; t say enough about the family services staff who work there . They provide a very high level of response , &amp;rdquo ; Rupert said . &amp;nbsp ; &amp;ldquo ; But I don&amp;rsquo ; t know if you just call and get a random police response that things happen that way , I suspect not . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;27309;TOOLONG ... title= " Lisa Rupert 's assessment of Canada 's failure to protect women fleeing domestic and gang violence was pivotal in a US immigration decision to allow a Canadian woman asylum under the UN Torture Convention . " alt= " Lisa Rupert 's assessment of Canada 's failure to protect women fleeing domestic and gang violence was pivotal in a US immigration decision to allow a Canadian woman asylum under the UN Torture Convention . " data-portal-copyright= " YWCA " **28;27345;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1221060 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; Statistics Canada&amp;rsquo ; s latest figures suggest only five per cent of sexual assaults were reported in 2014 and , of those , one in five was dismissed by police as unfounded . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; One reason may be that Canada&amp;rsquo ; s national force itself has been racked by sexual harassment , discrimination and sexual abuse . In 2016 , the RCMP apologized to female officers and staff and agreed to a $100-million settlement with complainants. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The Criminal Code was amended in 2016 to clarify the consent provisions , provide more onerous interim release requirements for offences involving violence against an intimate partner and increase the maximum prison term for repeat offences with abuse of an intimate partner being an aggravating factor in sentencing . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; But assessing how Canada is doing when it comes to gender-based violence is difficult because the information collected by provinces is fragmented and isn&amp;rsquo ; t designed to allow comparisons among provinces , as both the United Nations Human Rights Commission&amp;rsquo ; s rapporteur and others have pointed out . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Still , the statistics are grim , especially for Indigenous women and girls whose risk of violence is five times higher than that of other Canadians . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Every two and a half days , a Canadian woman is killed by an intimate partner . Eight in 10 women report having experienced intimate partner violence . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; Because of the pervasiveness of sexual violence in women&amp;rsquo ; s lives , women are now more likely than men to be victims of violent crime , &amp;rdquo ; according to the &lt;a href= " https : **33;27375;TOOLONG ... target= " blank " rel= " noopener " &gt;UN rapporteur 's report&lt;/a&gt; released in July . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;h2 class= " cat-head short " **40;27410;TOOLONG ... &lt;ul class= " relatedlinks " &gt; &lt;li&gt;&lt;a href= " https : **33;27452;TOOLONG ... the odds , a Canadian woman is granted asylum in the U.S. under the UN Convention Against **28;27487;TOOLONG &lt;/ul&gt; &lt;p&gt; Technology is one reason it is now more difficult for women fleeing a stalker . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; With technology , people are now able to track their partners much more easily , &amp;rdquo ; Rupert said . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; If you want to stay safe , the advice is stay off of all social media . Don&amp;rsquo ; t use apps on your phone and don&amp;rsquo ; t use technology in ways that are so much part of our lives , said Rupert , adding , &amp;ldquo ; It&amp;rsquo ; s not fair and it&amp;rsquo ; s imposing another punishment on the victim . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;span style= " font-family : -apple-system , BlinkMacSystemFont , ' Segoe UI ' , Roboto , Oxygen-Sans , Ubuntu , Cantarell , ' Helvetica Neue ' , sans-serif ; " &gt;There was a time when provincial legal aid programs provided lawyers in cases of domestic violence . " That was amazing , " Rupert said . " I wish we could go back to that . " &lt;/span&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Last year , Ontario eliminated funding for it . British Columbia eliminated it several years ago . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Even for women willing to take the extreme measure of leaving Canada , it&amp;rsquo ; s harder . Twice in the last two years , two different U.S. attorneys general have ordered that domestic violence and gang affiliation are acceptable reasons for granting asylum . The first order was overturned in the courts , the second is under appeal . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; There&amp;rsquo ; s another twist . An unintended consequence of the well-meaning Hague Convention on International Child Abduction is it binds women to their abusers , often forcing them to remain in countries where they are not citizens . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; If a father has a custody order , women who flee abroad with their children can be charged with abduction . Child abduction carries a penalty of up to 10 years in prison . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; In 2019 , StatsCan &lt;a href= " https : **33;27517;TOOLONG ... target= " blank " rel= " noopener " &gt;reported&lt;/a&gt; that the percentage of non-permanent resident women living in Canadian transition houses was six times higher than in the general population . The number of non-resident children was nearly 10 times higher . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Because of that , YWCA-Vancouver has three rooms permanently designated for them along with funding to help them apply for citizenship . Japanese women have been the most frequent occupants , followed by Americans . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; As for how many women fleeing from domestic violence and gangs in other countries come here and are granted asylum , nobody knows . The Canadian government doesn&amp;rsquo ; t track that . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The best information comes from a 2018 CBC &lt;a href= " https : **33;27552;TOOLONG ... target= " blank " rel= " noopener " **27;27587;TOOLONG that found 15 per cent of female refugee claimants who arrived between 2013 and 2017 listed gender persecution ( a.k.a. domestic violence ) as their primary reason before religious , ethnic or political persecution . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Of the nearly 3,000 domestic violence claims heard by the Immigration and Refugee Board , 58 per cent were accepted . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; For generations and across most societies , domestic violence has been hidden away . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Historically , women and children were not deemed to have rights , so domestic violence was seen as a private matter , not a public concern . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; That notion persists , as does the faulty belief that someone who assaults a loved one is less of a monster than one who abuses a stranger and less of a risk to others . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; We have to recognize that the people they are assaulting are members of society , that there is a cost to those people and to their family members and a financial cost to the larger society , &amp;rdquo ; says Rupert . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; We need to recognize that there is a risk to other people and the other partners of those people as well as to the children that even trickles down to grandchildren . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " **29;27616;TOOLONG " **36;27647;TOOLONG &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Twitter : @bramhamdaphne &lt;/p&gt; Daphne BramhamMon , 27 Jan 2020 21:44:00 **45;27685;TOOLONG ... &amp;amp ; ChildHealthLifeLocal **45;27732;TOOLONG ... The illness will officially be confirmed once it completes that testing . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The news of Canada&amp;rsquo ; s first coronavirus patient comes as authorities around the world grapple with the new type of virus , which originated in China but has since spread to Europe and North America . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;h2 class= " cat-head short " **40;27916;TOOLONG ... &lt;ul class= " relatedlinks " &gt; &lt;li&gt;&lt;a href= " https : **33;27958;TOOLONG ... health officials prepare for possible virus spread after containing **25;27993;TOOLONG &lt;li&gt;&lt;a href= " https : **33;28020;TOOLONG ... diagnosis of **32;28055;TOOLONG &lt;/ul&gt; &lt;p&gt; Authorities around the world have confirmed more than 1,975 cases so far , including three in France and two in the United States . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Dr. David Williams , chief medical officer of health for Ontario , told the news conference that the province&amp;rsquo ; s health system acted as it should . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;28089;TOOLONG ... title= " Christine Elliott , Ontario 's Health Minister , centre , sits between Dr. Barbara Yaffe , Ontario 's Associate Chief Medical Officer of Health and Dr. Peter Donnelly , President and CEO of Public Health Ontario , as they attend a news conference in Toronto on Saturday , January 25 , 2020 , as officials announce that Canada 's first presumptive positive case of the Coronavirus is being treated at Toronto 's Sunnybrook Health Sciences Centre , after arriving on a flight from China . " data-portal-copyright= " Chris Young " **28;28125;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1199087 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; He added that the widely available information about the illness appears to have contributed to the early detection of this case . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; The individual , knowing his responsibility , when feeling unwell , even without having really severe symptoms , was concerned enough and informed prompt enough , &amp;rdquo ; Williams said . &amp;ldquo ; That just tells you that people have knowledge of it , they want to take proper precautions to protect their health and protect their family members and others . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; As a result , the risk to Ontarians is still low , and things are managed and well-controlled , &amp;rdquo ; Williams said . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; While 56 people have died of the virus in China &amp;mdash ; most of the deaths have been older patients &amp;mdash ; the World Health Organization has not declared the outbreak to be an international public health emergency . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Federal Health Minister Patty Hajdu echoed the sentiment in a written statement , saying officials are taking all necessary precautions . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; The Government of Canada has been working closely with provincial and territorial counterparts , and international partners , since China first reported 2019-nCoV cases to ensure that our country is prepared to limit the spread of 2019-nCoV in Canada , &amp;rdquo ; she said . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The new virus comes from a large family of coronaviruses , some causing nothing worse than a common cold . But in late 2002 , a coronavirus named severe acute respiratory syndrome erupted in southern China , causing a severe pneumonia that rapidly spread to other countries . SARS infected more than 8,000 people and killed 774 , including 44 Canadians . Toronto was hard hit in that outbreak . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The first people infected with coronavirus visited or worked at a seafood market in the Chinese city of Wuhan , which has gone into isolation since the outbreak . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Bustling streets , malls and other public spaces have turned eerily quiet , masks are mandatory in public , and some hospitals have run low on medical supplies . Transportation has also been shut down in roughly a dozen Chinese cities , home to roughly 36 million people . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Canadian officials have said such mass quarantines are unlikely , even if the virus spread here . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; It is not clear how lethal the new coronavirus is or even whether it is as dangerous as the ordinary flu , which results in 12,200 hospitalizations and about 3,500 deaths yearly . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The federal government has beefed up measures at major airports in Vancouver , Toronto and Montreal . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Visitors are now being asked about any travel to Wuhan in the past 14 days , and a positive response would trigger further investigation . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;em&gt; -- with files from Harrison Mooney , Postmedia News&lt;/em&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;span class= " s2 " &gt;&lt;a href= " **27;28155;TOOLONG " &gt;&lt;em&gt;CLICK **38;28184;TOOLONG &lt;em&gt;&lt;span class= " s1 " &gt; to report a typo. &lt;/span&gt;&lt;/em&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;em&gt;&lt;span class= " s1 " &gt;Is there more to this story ? NewsNationalCBSA treated Mexican businessman unfairly on Vancouver visit , court ruleshttp : **35;28360;TOOLONG ... &lt;img src= " https : **34;28397;TOOLONG ... title= " A Mexican businessman who unsuccessfully tried to visit Vancouver last year has won a court challenge of a CBSA ruling that he was inadmissible . " alt= " A Mexican businessman who unsuccessfully tried to visit Vancouver last year has won a court challenge of a CBSA ruling that he was inadmissible . " data-portal-copyright= " Nick Procaylo " **28;28433;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 943791 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; A Mexican businessman accused in his country of money laundering was treated unfairly by the Canada Border Services Agency who considered him inadmissible to visit Canada , a federal judge says . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " https : **33;28463;TOOLONG ... &lt;a href= " https : **33;28498;TOOLONG ... &lt;a href= " https : **33;28533;TOOLONG ... Shirzad Ahmed&lt;/a&gt; &lt;a href= " https : **33;28568;TOOLONG ... of the Federal Court of Canada &lt;/a&gt; &lt;a href= " https : **33;28603;TOOLONG ... &amp;nbsp ; &amp;nbsp ; &lt;span style= " font-family : -apple-system , BlinkMacSystemFont , ' Segoe UI ' , Roboto , Oxygen-Sans , Ubuntu , Cantarell , ' Helvetica Neue ' , sans-serif ; " &gt;that the CBSA officer who sent Sergio Antonio Reyes Garcia back to Mexico when he flew in to Vancouver on March 3 , 2019 , did not have the legal authority to declare the businessman inadmissible . &lt;/span&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Ahmed said the officer was within his rights to decide that Reyes Garcia had misrepresented information about the money laundering investigation when he first applied for an electronic travel authorization that would allow him repeated entry to Canada as a visitor . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; But Ahmed sided with Reyes Garcia&amp;rsquo ; s claim that he was denied due process by the CBSA. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; In my view , it was open to the officer to form his own opinion as to an alleged misrepresentation based on the interview and the applicant&amp;rsquo ; s ( electronic ) application , &amp;rdquo ; Ahmed said in a written ruling released Jan. 22. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; However , although it was open to the officer to form an opinion as to an alleged misrepresentation &amp;hellip ; the officer did not have the authority to make a final admissibility determination . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The judge said Canadian immigration law says the CBSA officer could have written a report of his concerns and sent the matter to the Immigration Division to hold an inadmissibility hearing . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Only the minister&amp;rsquo ; s delegate or the Immigration Division may make such findings , the judge ruled . &amp;ldquo ; Therefore , the officer erred in finding that the applicant had misrepresented and that he was therefore inadmissible , &amp;rdquo ; Ahmed said . &amp;ldquo ; I find the officer breached procedural fairness and that the officer&amp;rsquo ; s decision is unreasonable . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Ahmed set aside the original CBSA decision and sent the case back for another officer to consider . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Last fall , Postmedia obtained a transcript of Reyes Garcia&amp;rsquo ; s interview with the CBSA agent . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; In it , the agent alleged Reyes Garcia and his business partner Manuel Barreiro had been misusing foreign companies , including one set up in B.C. called Kross Investments Ltd. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; What connects you to Manuel is a money-laundering scandal in Mexico , &amp;rdquo ; CBSA agent Rahim Bajwa said to Reyes Garcia , citing conversations found on the Mexican man&amp;rsquo ; s cellphone. &amp;ldquo ; There is discussion about money leaving Mexico and entering Canada &amp;mdash ; discussion about you and Manuel attempting secret meetings in Canada and opening up false companies in Canada . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Reyes Garcia said the allegations against him and his business partner stem from a &amp;ldquo ; political problem&amp;rdquo ; in Mexico . He said the former Mexican president&amp;rsquo ; s party targeted Barreiro for investigation because he had business with a rival presidential candidate . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Reyes Garcia said that while allegations were made against him in the Mexican media , he was never charged and has been cleared of any wrongdoing . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; He voluntarily returned to Mexico after his airport interview , but filed the Federal Court challenge to regain his travel document . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Bajwa , the CBSA agent , noted that on Reyes Garcia&amp;rsquo ; s cellphone were details of financial transactions involving cash sent from Mexico through the B.C. company to a Swiss bank . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; In the most basic form , what is happening right now is called money laundering . These activities and these sorts of transactions are confined to money laundering &amp;mdash ; money leaving Mexico , going through Canada and various other parts of the world , &amp;rdquo ; Bajwa said . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; Once the money has left Mexico , it is difficult to trace . Money launderers are aware of this and try their best to hide the trade because they don&amp;rsquo ; t want anything to come back to them . This is done because the money is earned through illegitimate means , including drug trafficking , embezzlement , misappropriation of funds etc. &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;em&gt;&lt;span style= " font-weight:400 ; " &gt; **52;28638;TOOLONG ... &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;em&gt;blog : **42;28692;TOOLONG &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; **39;28736;TOOLONG &lt;/p&gt; Kim BolanSat , 25 Jan 2020 22:30:03 **45;28777;TOOLONG ... NewsNationalIan Mulgrew : Coverage not denial needed in medicare crisishttp : **34;28824;TOOLONG ... &lt;img src= " https : **34;28860;TOOLONG ... title= " " alt= " " data-portal-copyright= " Getty Images/iStockphoto " **28;28896;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1179250 " /&gt; &lt;iframe width= " 100% " height= " 100% " frameborder= " 0 " allowfullscreen= " true " src= " https : **34;28926;TOOLONG ... &gt; &lt;/iframe&gt; &lt;p&gt; Medicare is in crisis across Canada while governments fiddle and insist there is no place for private insurance to cover medically necessary care . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; A new report by the &lt;a href= " https : **26;28962;TOOLONG " target= " blank " rel= " noopener " &gt;Fraser Institute&lt;/a&gt; says the de facto ban looks like folly since we are alone among 17 countries &amp;mdash ; including Australia , Germany , Switzerland and the Netherlands &amp;mdash ; who provide universal health-care . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Canada has the longest wait times , ranking in 2016 in last place for the percentage of patients who waited four weeks or longer to see a specialist , last for the percentage of patients who waited four weeks or longer to get treatment , and last for the percentage of patients who waited four or more hours in emergency departments . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Written by Steven Globerman , the 46-page study by the conservative think tank says eliminating the prohibition could shorten health care queues and encourage cost-efficient innovations . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; The idea of using private insurance to cover some or all health-care costs &amp;mdash ; instead of taxpayers paying for it &amp;mdash ; has been pilloried in Canada for many years , but it&amp;rsquo ; s a common feature of other universal health-care systems around the world , &amp;rdquo ; said the resident scholar and Addington Chair in Measurement , professor emeritus , Western Washington University . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Trouble is , medicare in this country is a patchwork quilt &amp;mdash ; there are a baker&amp;rsquo ; s dozen jurisdictions and medical systems . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; As a result , you should get private insurance for travel within Canada so you don&amp;rsquo ; t wake up in a hospital in another province or territory owing a small fortune for an ambulance trip or other service for which you aren&amp;rsquo ; t covered . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; At the marathon B.C. medicare constitutional trial , which includes a challenge to the province&amp;rsquo ; s ban on private insurance , it was evident both levels of government would rather Canadians remained in the dark , mistakenly believing they have the best health care system in the world . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The most cogent expert opinion and the data presented during more than three years of evidence , however , indicated the situation instead is dire , and this latest study echoes that view . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Titled &lt;a href= " https : **33;28990;TOOLONG ... target= " blank " rel= " noopener " &gt;Understanding Universal Health Care Reform Options : Private Insurance&lt;/a&gt; , the report released Tuesday underscores Canada&amp;rsquo ; s laggard performance compared with other developed countries providing universal health care : &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; In 2016 , no one in Germany waited four months for elective surgery , but nearly one in five Canadians did ; only one in a hundred waited more than an hour in a French emergency room , while 29 in 100 Canadians sat around waiting to be seen ; in the Netherlands , 77 per cent of patients got a same-day appointment , only 44 per cent of Canadians did . Even Americans do better than us. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; In the 2000s , the Supreme Court of Canada wrestled with the issue of private insurance in a Quebec case known as &lt;a href= " https : **33;29025;TOOLONG ... &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The majority held that a ban on private insurance was unconstitutional given pernicious waiting times in the public system . For all the government hand-wringing , however , the decision did not result in significant change . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Regulation minimized the procedures private insurance could cover &amp;mdash ; hip and knee replacement and cataract surgery ( those with the longest queues ) , who may provide the surgeries and where they may be provided . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Quebec medicare endures. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Other nations allow two options : full coverage of all medically necessary services or supplementary coverage that facilitates faster access to medical procedures and treatments , a wider selection of providers and amenities such as private rooms . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; There is little legal scope or opportunity in Canada for either . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Without any evidence , both senior levels of government insist allowing such private insurance will reduce public access to health care or let the well-heeled obtain &amp;ldquo ; better&amp;rdquo ; care . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The official fearmongering includes predicting the very death of Medicare . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; No developed country has experienced such a catastrophe by providing private insurance . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; And the rich in Canada can already get quicker , perhaps even &amp;ldquo ; better&amp;rdquo ; care , anytime with a cheap flight to the U.S. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; As the evidence from Europe indicated , Globerman maintained a private health insurance market might reduce wait times for most if not all services . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; In this regard , he noted the poor would enjoy improved access notwithstanding that their access would not be identical to that enjoyed by wealthier Canadians . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Interestingly , Globerman believed that the most significant benefit flowing from private insurance could be innovation . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; His research indicated models like Canada&amp;rsquo ; s often limit or restrict access to innovative medical treatment in the interest of short-term cost savings , while private insurers have stronger incentives to embrace innovations that may increase short-term costs but ultimately save money by improving long-term health outcomes . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Globerman added &amp;ldquo ; a systematic reevaluation of allowing access to private health insurance for basic services seems appropriate , especially in light of theory and evidence that argues , on balance , that doing so would have net social benefits . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; But I&amp;rsquo ; m sure his report will be dismissed by government and vested interests as right-wing propaganda . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Better to stick with the current three monkeys&amp;rsquo ; approach &amp;mdash ; hear , see and brook no discussion about medicare&amp;rsquo ; s ailments and potential cures . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " **29;29060;TOOLONG " target= " blank " rel= " noopener " **36;29091;TOOLONG &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " http : //twitter.com/ianmulgrew " target= " blank " rel= " noopener " **31;29129;TOOLONG ... &lt;/p&gt; Ian MulgrewFri , 24 Jan 2020 18:42:38 **45;29162;TOOLONG ... Todd : Vancouver 's luxury builders should be nervoushttp : **33;29209;TOOLONG ... &lt;img src= " https : **34;29244;TOOLONG ... title= " Vancouver 's high-end builders are watching the way thousands of New York City condos in glistening skinny towers are going unsold . " alt= " Vancouver 's high-end builders are watching the way thousands of New York City condos in glistening skinny towers are going unsold . " data-portal-copyright= " Gerry Kahrmann " **28;29280;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1196372 " /&gt; &lt;iframe width= " 100% " height= " 100% " frameborder= " 0 " allowfullscreen= " true " src= " https : **34;29310;TOOLONG ... &gt; &lt;/iframe&gt; &lt;p&gt; In Manhattan , half the luxury condos built in the past five years remain unsold . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Vancouver builders are watching as thousands of New York City condos in glistening &amp;ldquo ; super-skinny&amp;rdquo ; towers , rapidly erected to serve a flood of demand from global millionaires , are &lt;a href= " https : **33;29346;TOOLONG ... target= " blank " rel= " noopener " &gt;largely empty&lt;/a&gt; as that city core&amp;rsquo ; s real-estate bubble is bursting . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The future is looking wobbly for high-end developers in potentially more risky Vancouver , where prices in the past decade have risen three times faster than in the Big Apple . In both places , middle-class young people are being squeezed out of home ownership . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Even though there are differences between the affluent housing markets in New York and Vancouver , which are routinely ranked among the &lt;a href= " http : **34;29381;TOOLONG ... target= " blank " rel= " noopener " &gt;world 's sought-after destinations&lt;/a&gt; , the downward trend is similar in the East and West Coast cities . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; As with New York&amp;rsquo ; s flashy highrises , many luxury condos with spectacular views in Vancouver are going un-bought . Dramatic price drops are also occurring among the posh single-family residences of Metro Vancouver , where prices have plunged by more than one-quarter since the peak in 2017. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Global media outlets have noted that popular Canadian singer Michael Bubl&amp;eacute ; last year had to sell his sprawling West Vancouver mansion for 28 per cent less than its assessed value . It was bought by the only one who showed interest : An increasingly hard-to-find buyer from China . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Prices have plummeted by more than 25 per cent in the past two years across hilly West Vancouver , which is often ranked as the municipality with the highest per capita income in Canada . A similar dip has occurred in the city of Vancouver and many of its suburbs , where assessed &lt;a href= " http : **34;29417;TOOLONG ... went down by more than 11 per cent in 2019 alone . &lt;/a&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;h2 class= " cat-head short " **40;29453;TOOLONG ... &lt;ul class= " relatedlinks " &gt; &lt;li&gt;&lt;a href= " http : **34;29495;TOOLONG ... Todd : A tale of two desirable cities : Barcelona and **30;29531;TOOLONG &lt;li&gt;&lt;a href= " http : **34;29563;TOOLONG ... Todd : Contrasting two costly housing markets : Toronto and **30;29599;TOOLONG &lt;li&gt;&lt;a href= " http : **34;29631;TOOLONG ... Todd : Royal couple 's move to B.C. mimics migration trends of **28;29667;TOOLONG &lt;/ul&gt; &lt;p&gt; The city of Vancouver&amp;rsquo ; s luxury condo market might not be quite as hard hit as Manhattan&amp;rsquo ; s . But it&amp;rsquo ; s struggling considerably , with roughly one quarter of the city&amp;rsquo ; s most opulent inventory going unsold . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Hundreds of condo units in towers rising in Vancouver&amp;rsquo ; s Oakridge neighbourhood , with price tags in the multi-millions of dollars , don&amp;rsquo ; t have buyers , for instance . Most of these swank tower homes are priced at more than $2,400 a square foot , completely out of reach of local wage earners . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Yet that&amp;rsquo ; s a common square-foot cost for thousands of other exorbitant condo units being built in this West Coast city , which is often ranked among the world&amp;rsquo ; s most unaffordable . The gap between median wages and median housing costs in Vancouver is far greater than in New York City , which has the advantage of being a global financial centre . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Knight Frank Global Real Estate Consultants , which tracks prices in 15 of the world&amp;rsquo ; s most sought-after cities , projects that New York City&amp;rsquo ; s overall housing prices will fall again in 2020 , by 4.4 per cent . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Vancouver&amp;rsquo ; s housing values are &lt;a href= " https : **33;29697;TOOLONG ... target= " blank " rel= " noopener " &gt;forecast to tumble even more this year , by 10.2 per cent&lt;/a&gt; overall , which Knight Frank says will be the steepest drop in all 15 of the world&amp;rsquo ; s &amp;ldquo ; prime&amp;rdquo ; cities ( which include London , Hong Kong , Los Angeles and Dubai ) . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; Developers globally thought the way to make money was to build luxury , &amp;rdquo ; says a Vancouver-based real estate analyst , &lt;a href= " http : **34;29732;TOOLONG ... target= " blank " rel= " noopener " &gt;Steve Saretsky. &lt;/a&gt; &amp;nbsp ; &amp;ldquo ; But the general theme is the luxury market in global cities has really died off . It&amp;rsquo ; s going to be a sluggish market in Vancouver . The luxury bid , particularly out of China , has really slowed down . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Tighter local government regulations are a factor . The B.C. government&amp;rsquo ; s 20 per cent foreign-buyers tax and speculation and vacancy tax , which are designed to discourage offshore and domestic investment , have been working . But they&amp;rsquo ; re similar to rising restrictions in most of the world&amp;rsquo ; s top housing markets . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The most important thing curbing elite housing is the global economic slowdown , plus restrictions on the amount of money allowed to flow out of China , whose buyers account for one out of four purchases in Sydney and London . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The power of buyers from China has been even greater in Vancouver , which has a much higher proportion of ethnic Chinese residents . China is home to one-sixth of the world&amp;rsquo ; s new millionaires , a cohort that&amp;rsquo ; s been expanding at the rate of 250 an hour for the past eight years , according to The Economist . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; And the &lt;a href= " http : **34;29768;TOOLONG ... target= " blank " rel= " noopener " &gt;Hurun Global Housing Index&lt;/a&gt; says relatively small Vancouver remains the seventh most sought-after city in the world for investors from China , many of whom are looking for a haven for their capital . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Even though the valuations on Metro&amp;rsquo ; s most pricey single-family homes are clearly declining sharply , one could argue that Vancouver&amp;rsquo ; s trendy needle towers are not hit quite as hard as those in New York City . Still , figures indicate a slowdown . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Vancouver&amp;rsquo ; s top-of-the-line developers used to boast they could entirely &amp;ldquo ; pre-sell&amp;rdquo ; towers of condo units ranging from $2 million to $20 million each , before they&amp;rsquo ; re built . Now figures from the Altus Group show a significant portion of the thousands of upmarket condo units being constructed have not found buyers . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; The market in Metro Vancouver today has kind of switched back to being a local market , &amp;rdquo ; says Saretsky. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; The high-end market is coming down . What is moving is the affordable stuff , including the one-bedroom condos in the City of Vancouver . They&amp;rsquo ; re getting multiple offers , and in some cases their prices are actually pushing up , because it&amp;rsquo ; s all anyone can afford , &amp;rdquo ; Saretsky said . Developers , especially in Surrey , Richmond and Langley , are responding again to the domestic market . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; High-end developers might end up stuck with more of their units than they would have liked , he said . &amp;ldquo ; I don&amp;rsquo ; t know if they&amp;rsquo ; re worried , because they&amp;rsquo ; re well-capitalized . But they could end up in a situation where they finish their building and wonder : Do we cut the prices , or do we rent them out and wait for the market to turn around ? If they start discounting prices quite a bit , that kind of screws everybody who bought into the building initially . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Saretsky can&amp;rsquo ; t say how it&amp;rsquo ; s all going to play out for domestic buyers . &amp;ldquo ; Do the reductions at the high end trickle down ? Or does it all trickle up ? &amp;rdquo ; The key thing he knows is the Metro market is now extremely segmented . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; That&amp;rsquo ; s the case in most of the world&amp;rsquo ; s desirable cities , where there is a crisis of homelessness , as well as unaffordability for the middle class , at the same time the world&amp;rsquo ; s multimillionaires have been , at least until two years ago , driving up values . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Whether the downturns in New York , Vancouver and &lt;a href= " http : **34;29804;TOOLONG ... target= " blank " rel= " noopener " &gt;other prime cities&lt;/a&gt; will stretch on for years is difficult to predict . But the immediate drop in the luxury market offers a glimmer of light to the expanding number of ordinary people whose housing needs are , to say the least , going unmet . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;MORE RELATED : &lt;/strong&gt; &lt;a href= " http : **34;29840;TOOLONG ... target= " blank " rel= " noopener " &gt;Forces slowly dropping home values in Metro Vancouver &lt;/a&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " http : **34;29876;TOOLONG ... target= " blank " rel= " noopener " &gt;Douglas Todd : Five things we learned about migration in 2019&lt;/a&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;29912;TOOLONG ... title= " Vancouver 's high-end builders are watching the way thousands of New York City condos in glistening skinny towers are going unsold . " alt= " Vancouver 's high-end builders are watching the way thousands of New York City condos in glistening skinny towers are going unsold . " data-portal-copyright= " Gerry Kahrmann " **28;29948;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1196372 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; dtodd@postmedia.com &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; **25;29978;TOOLONG &lt;a href= " https : **25;30005;TOOLONG " class= " twitter-follow-button " data-show-count= " false " &gt;Follow @DouglasTodd&lt;/a&gt; **25;30032;TOOLONG &lt;/p&gt; Douglas ToddThu , 23 Jan 2020 17:52:07 **45;30059;TOOLONG ... NewsNationalReal EstateStaff BlogsWorldIan Mulgrew : Justice minister gets personal about assisted dyinghttp : **35;30106;TOOLONG ... &lt;img src= " https : **34;30143;TOOLONG ... title= " Federal Justice Minister David Lametti. " alt= " Federal Justice Minister David Lametti. " data-portal-copyright= " Don Healy " **28;30179;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1192188 " /&gt; &lt;iframe width= " 100% " height= " 100% " frameborder= " 0 " allowfullscreen= " true " src= " https : **34;30209;TOOLONG ... &gt; &lt;/iframe&gt; &lt;p&gt; In the midst of a whirlwind coast-to-coast tour to consult Canadians about medical assistance in dying , federal Justice Minister David Lametti interrupted his Vancouver breakfast Friday . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Under pressure of a March 11 deadline set by the Quebec Superior Court , Lametti hopes to quickly craft amendments he can put to parliament that will make the still-controversial law , often called MAID , constitutionally compliant . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; We&amp;rsquo ; ve all been touched by it , &amp;rdquo ; he confided , setting down his coffee . &amp;ldquo ; I&amp;rsquo ; ll tell you my story if you want to hear it . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; As with so many individuals and families , Lametti has personally confronted an excruciating life-and-death decision involving a loved one . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; In 2016 , when we were passing the original legislation , I watched my mother die of a rather negative form of dementia , and it wasn&amp;rsquo ; t easy , &amp;rdquo ; he explained . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; I knew , I had spoken to her ( about medical assistance in dying ) 10 years before . She was a devout Catholic , and I knew what her beliefs were . So I never would have considered assisted dying for her . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The 57-year-old former McGill law professor , more comfortable quoting legal precedent than an example from his own life , continued : &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; My brothers and I were well aware of what her feelings were . That being said , we did at a certain point have to make a decision that she not undergo further treatment &amp;mdash ; which is in a sense a delegated decision , although fully within her belief structure , within the church&amp;rsquo ; s belief structure , and within the medical structure . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Lametti said Canadians have long accepted such delegated decisions when a life was at stake . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;30245;TOOLONG ... title= " David Lametti speaks to Prime Minister Justin Trudeau after being presented as Minister of Justice and Attorney General of Canada at Rideau Hall in Ottawa , November 20 , 2019. " alt= " David Lametti speaks to Prime Minister Justin Trudeau after being presented as Minister of Justice and Attorney General of Canada at Rideau Hall in Ottawa , November 20 , 2019. " data-portal-copyright= " REUTERS/Blair Gable " **28;30281;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1187616 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; This MAID provides another way for people to chose in an autonomous fashion how they wish to end their life given certain conditions , and usually under very tragic circumstances or sad circumstances or personal circumstances . And it allows for people to do it in a positive , oftentimes spiritual , oftentimes uplifting way . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Still , in September , the Quebec court said the federal law was too restrictive &amp;mdash ; allowing MAID only to those facing &amp;ldquo ; reasonably foreseeable&amp;rdquo ; or , depending on your vocabulary , &amp;ldquo ; imminent&amp;rdquo ; death . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Ottawa decided not to appeal the ruling , but was hampered in responding because the decision came down as the federal election campaign started . Now , time is running out . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; We&amp;rsquo ; re consulting experts , MAID providers , we&amp;rsquo ; re consulting persons who feel vulnerable because of the legislation , some people with disabilities , &amp;rdquo ; Lametti said . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; While we&amp;rsquo ; re doing it , we&amp;rsquo ; re trying to touch base with Canadians on a couple of specific issues around advance requests , which we know some Canadians are interested in. &amp;hellip ; Would people allow for someone who had been evaluated and approved to benefit from MAID even after they have lost consciousness or capacity ? &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; And then another advance request from someone who had been diagnosed with a well-known progressive disease , so Alzheimer&amp;rsquo ; s , if you get the diagnosis and say , &amp;lsquo ; Look , when I reach this stage , I would like the benefit of MAID . &amp;rsquo ; We&amp;rsquo ; ll see &amp;hellip ; if there is consensus among Canadians . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; As well as the consultation process under way , the government has posted an &lt;a href= " https : **33;30311;TOOLONG ... target= " blank " rel= " noopener " &gt;online questionnaire&lt;/a&gt; . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The MP for the Montreal riding of Lasalle-&amp;Eacute ; mard-Verdun , Lametti said more than 150,000 have responded already . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; He pointed out the review was also a bit of an advance scout because the original 2016 MAID legislation contained within it a five-year evaluation process that will begin in June . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Lametti said it will &amp;ldquo ; deal with the really tough issues &amp;mdash ; mature minors , mental disability and advance requests more generally . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; But he did not think the law itself was under threat : &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; The experience that we&amp;rsquo ; ve heard now from caregivers across Canada , from families who have had a loved one benefit from MAID has been I think relatively positive . I think maybe even overwhelmingly positive . &amp;hellip ; And I think there is a large acceptance of the practice amongst practitioners , amongst people who are facing , in particular , terminal illness . So I think the practice is here to stay and the question is how much farther do we push the boundaries . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Still , he acknowledged there were passionate critics of MAID , especially among some faiths. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; I hope we don&amp;rsquo ; t reach the same kind of polemics that we do with a question like abortion , &amp;rdquo ; the justice minister said . &amp;ldquo ; But there are a number of legitimate concerns that are raised by people who worry , people living with disabilities , for example , feel vulnerable , and legitimately feel vulnerable . They see themselves as being susceptible to being influenced by others who say , &amp;lsquo ; Well , you know , you&amp;rsquo ; d be better off dead . ' &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; This is about individual autonomy , he insisted . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; But we also , as a government , have to be sensitive , as a society we have to be sensitive , to what one might call quality-of-life issues . We need to make sure the quality and value of care is as best as it can be for people who choose not to have assisted dying but to end their days following a more natural progression , &amp;rdquo ; Lametti said . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; Same is true for other life issues , people living with disabilities or people living with other forms of suffering who want to live and &amp;mdash ; whether it&amp;rsquo ; s because they want to see their kids grow up , or whether it&amp;rsquo ; s because they want to share more time with loved ones , or they have other things they want to accomplish in life &amp;mdash ; we need to make sure people can live fulfilling and productive lives even with the suffering they are going through . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " **29;30346;TOOLONG " target= " blank " rel= " noopener " **36;30377;TOOLONG &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " http : //twitter.com/ianmulgrew " target= " blank " rel= " noopener " **31;30415;TOOLONG ... &lt;/p&gt; Ian MulgrewThu , 23 Jan 2020 01:04:10 **45;30448;TOOLONG ... Bramham : Outspoken activist against polygamy dieshttp : **36;30495;TOOLONG ... &lt;img src= " https : **34;30533;TOOLONG ... title= " Debbie Palmer , a former plural wife from Bountiful , in 2004 . ( Ian Smith/Vancouver Sun ) " alt= " Debbie Palmer , a former plural wife from Bountiful , in 2004 . ( Ian Smith/Vancouver Sun ) " data-portal-copyright= " Ian Smith " **28;30569;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1194741 " /&gt; &lt;iframe width= " 100% " height= " 100% " frameborder= " 0 " allowfullscreen= " true " src= " https : **34;30599;TOOLONG ... &gt; &lt;/iframe&gt; &lt;p&gt; If it weren&amp;rsquo ; t for Debbie Palmer , Canadians might not know anything about the polygamous community of Bountiful in southeastern British Columbia . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; She was the first to lift the veil of secrecy that had protected the community from outsiders in the early 1990s. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; By demanding criminal charges against the leaders for their abuse of women and children , she became a thorn in the sides of a succession of provincial attorneys-general , police and other politicians . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; But she was shunned and shamed by family and childhood friends . Palmer&amp;rsquo ; s activism took a huge toll . She died last weekend at the age of 64. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Palmer was the oldest in a family of 47 children . When she was only six , her mother died , leaving Debbie and her two siblings to be raised by their father&amp;rsquo ; s other five wives , who had multiple children of their own . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; At 15 , Palmer was one of Bountiful&amp;rsquo ; s first child brides . In a religious ceremony , she became the sixth wife of the community&amp;rsquo ; s leader , Ray Blackmore . He was 40 years older than her . Palmer was a widow by 18. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The community&amp;rsquo ; s elders &amp;ndash ; all men &amp;mdash ; placed her in another plural marriage to an man who was so abusive that she was released and later married Marvin Palmer with whom she had five children . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; But life in Bountiful finally became intolerable and , in 1988 , the mother of eight fled becoming one of the first women in either Canada or the United States to escape without leaving any of her children behind . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Almost immediately , Palmer began telling her story to journalists , including Sally Armstrong from Chatelaine and The Province&amp;rsquo ; s Fabian Dawson . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; When the RCMP ended its investigation in 1992 and recommended charges against Palmer&amp;rsquo ; s father , Dalmon Oler , and against Winston Blackmore , the **26;30635;TOOLONG ; s ministry decided not to proceed . Taking advice from former B.C. Supreme Court chief justices , it was determined that Canada&amp;rsquo ; s law against polygamy was unconstitutional . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;30663;TOOLONG ... title= " Debbie Palmer , a former plural wife from Bountiful , in 2004 . ( Ian Smith/Vancouver Sun ) " alt= " Debbie Palmer , a former plural wife from Bountiful , in 2004 . ( Ian Smith/Vancouver Sun ) " data-portal-copyright= " Ian Smith " **28;30699;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1194741 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; Disappointed , but not daunted , Palmer convinced then-women&amp;rsquo ; s equality minister Penny Priddy to fund a report about polygamy and the physical and sexual abuse of women in &amp;ldquo ; a religious community in B.C.&amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The Life in Bountiful report revealed a pattern of forced marriages , rigid demands of obedience , and a &amp;ldquo ; distinct culture &amp;hellip ; that limits individual rights to the point of virtually eliminating them . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; It questioned whether children raised there could ever be capable of exercising free choice , including giving informed consent to sex and marriage . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; It was so explosive that the government refused to release it . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Rancher Faye Street had been on the committee that helped put the report together . &amp;ldquo ; I met with every goddamned minister and everybody I could to talk about what was going on in there , &amp;rdquo ; she told me in 2007. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; I used to go home and not be able to sleep because I kept thinking , &amp;lsquo ; How can this be happening here . We&amp;rsquo ; re not from the Ozarks. &amp;rsquo ; &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Over the years , Palmer became friends with Jon Krakauer , whose 2003 book , Under the Banner of Heaven , focused international attention on the fundamentalist Mormons. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; In 2004 , Palmer&amp;rsquo ; s sister Jane Blackmore also fled Bountiful . The midwife and ex-wife of the once powerful bishop Winston Blackmore couldn&amp;rsquo ; t have been more different . Debbie was a passionate , emotional firebrand . Jane is reserved , cautious and uncomfortable with the activist role that has frequently been forced upon her as a witness subpoenaed to testify against her ex-husband , her stepbrother and others . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; At that time , both were enmeshed in a series of other events that were unfolding &amp;mdash ; a schism between two factions in Bountiful , the manhunt for Warren Jeffs , the prophet of the Fundamentalist Church of Jesus Christ of Latter Day Saints , and Utah&amp;rsquo ; s and Arizona&amp;rsquo ; s successful seizure of FLDS assets that were to be used to compensate polygamy&amp;rsquo ; s victims , including the so-called Lost Boys forced out of their communities to make polygamy&amp;rsquo ; s cruel arithmetic add up in favour of old men . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Palmer&amp;rsquo ; s self-published and thinly disguised autobiography titled Keep Sweet : Children of Polygamy was briefly a Canadian bestseller in 2006 , even though it was not readily available in Creston &amp;mdash ; the town nearest to Bountiful. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; But by then , Palmer&amp;rsquo ; s information was mostly historic . With a growing number of people leaving the FLDS , the spotlight had shifted and Palmer retreated into private life &amp;mdash ; not even emerging for B.C.&amp;rsquo ; s subsequent constitutional reference case or later trials . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Yet without her pioneering efforts , two former fundamentalist Mormon bishops &amp;mdash ; her stepbrother James Oler , and Winston Blackmore , her brother-in-law and stepson &amp;mdash ; might never have been convicted of polygamy , and Oler and Brandon Blackmore ( another of Palmer&amp;rsquo ; s stepsons ) and his wife , Gail Blackmore , might never have been jailed for illegally taking their under-aged daughters to the United States for coerced marriages . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The admonition to women and girls to Keep Sweet might have remained , spelled out in white stone on the mountainside above the government-funded school . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; To anyone exposed to her unrelenting anger and what was , at the time , her seemingly incredible tales of abuse , Palmer could be exhausting and , at times , exasperating . She needed to be all of that and more in order to be heard . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; And , in the end , Palmer proved Margaret Mead&amp;rsquo ; s contention that it is the few caring people who change the world . Deborah Ann Oler Palmer was one of those who changed hers . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " **29;30729;TOOLONG " **36;30760;TOOLONG &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Twitter : @bramhamdaphne &lt;/p&gt; Daphne BramhamWed , 22 Jan 2020 18:27:57 **45;30798;TOOLONG ... **45;30845;TOOLONG ...
